---
title: Threat model
description: What can go wrong when Rail402 verifies, settles and catalogs x402 payments on Stellar, what Stellar changes about each threat, and the code and tests that answer it.
---

{/* Sections between generated markers are rendered by `pnpm report` from docs/security/threats.json; edit the registry, not the output. tools/conformance/test/threats.test.ts checks that every cited file, symbol, test and conformance case exists. */}

This page covers the Rail402 facilitator (`/verify`, `/settle`, `/supported`), the Bazaar catalog and search,
the `upto` settlement contract, the buyer side: the SDK, the `rail402` CLI and the `rail402-mcp` server
that let agents pay, and the [Playground](/playground/overview), a page and server that pay on the testnet
from a browser. For each threat it says what Stellar makes different, which mitigations answer it, where
they live in the code, which tests prove them, and what risk remains.

Every threat is an entry in [`docs/security/threats.json`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/docs/security/threats.json).
The tables below are generated from it, and a unit test fails when a cited source file no longer contains the
cited symbol, when a cited test no longer exists, or when a cited conformance case is not declared by its
harness. The [operational test report](/security/operational-tests) shows the exercises that run these tests
and what they observed.

## Invariants

The design rests on seven properties. Each threat below is an attempt to break one of them.

1. **The facilitator is never the source of payment funds.** It sponsors fees through a fee bump and nothing
   else; any payment that makes a facilitator account a payer, a source or a party to an authorization is
   refused.
2. **A signed authorization moves funds at most once.** The Soroban host consumes its nonce, and Rail402's
   settlement ledger answers every repeat from one record.
3. **The payer's signature fixes the recipient and the amount, or the cap.** Changing either after signing
   fails signature verification on chain.
4. **The sponsor's exposure is bounded** per settlement by the fee ceiling and per hour by the fee budget.
5. **A listing is owned by the `payTo` that was settled,** and is published only after its own origin
   confirms it.
6. **Rail402 and its buyer tools never dial a non-public address** on someone else's behalf, unless loopback
   is explicitly allowed for a local test network.
7. **An agent cannot spend beyond the caps its owner set,** which are checked immediately before signing.

## Assets

{/* generated:begin assets */}

| Asset                         | What is protected                                                                                                                |
| ----------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Payer funds                   | Tokens in buyers' accounts, G or C, that a payment authorizes.                                                                   |
| Seller revenue                | What a seller is owed and whether it is told correctly that it was paid.                                                         |
| Sponsor XLM                   | The fee sponsor's balance, which pays every settlement fee and the reserves of channels and the upto authority.                  |
| Catalog and search integrity  | That listings describe real, paid resources owned by their payTo, and that search returns them faithfully.                       |
| Agent budgets                 | The caps an agent's owner set for the SDK, CLI and MCP server.                                                                   |
| Secrets                       | The sponsor seed on the facilitator; buyer signing keys in the SDK, CLI and MCP server.                                          |
| Operator infrastructure       | Private networks and metadata services reachable from where Rail402 or an agent runs.                                            |
| Availability                  | That payments and discovery keep working, or fail with a coded, retryable answer.                                                |
| Access keys and quotas        | Pubnet API keys, their per-key rate and sponsor budgets, expiry, and the usage and service-fee records metered against them.     |
| Project tracker               | The public issue tracker and the workflow token that writes to it.                                                               |
| Explorer and report integrity | That indexed rows, attribution and published totals follow the published rules and registry, and can be rebuilt.                 |
| Release integrity             | The code, npm packages, container images and contract bytes that reach production and buyers, and the records that say which.    |
| Alerting and status           | That the operator learns about failures in time and that the public status page tells the truth.                                 |
| Cold treasury                 | The offline account that funds the pubnet fee sponsor and is its high-weight signer; never on a server.                          |
| Harness treasury              | The operator account that funds pubnet conformance runs, its seed file and the run accounts derived from it.                     |
| Payer privacy                 | Which endpoint or tool a payer bought. On chain a payment shows only its payer, payTo and amount; the Bazaar knows the resource. |

{/* generated:end */}

## Trust boundaries

{/* generated:begin boundaries */}

| Boundary | Between                                 | What crosses it                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| -------- | --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| TB1      | Buyer and seller                        | HTTP or MCP between a paying client and a resource server.                                                                                                                                                    |
| TB2      | Seller and facilitator                  | /verify, /settle, /supported and the discovery API.                                                                                                                                                           |
| TB3      | Facilitator and Stellar                 | The RPC, the network and its pending transactions, which anyone can observe.                                                                                                                                  |
| TB4      | Facilitator and its state               | Postgres, the process itself, its keys and its configuration.                                                                                                                                                 |
| TB5      | Facilitator and the open internet       | Origin checks and stellar.toml fetches to hosts that sellers choose.                                                                                                                                          |
| TB6      | Agent, MCP server and paid URLs         | An agent runtime, the buyer SDK or MCP server holding its key, and the URLs it is asked to pay.                                                                                                               |
| TB7      | Contracts                               | The upto settlement contract, token contracts and smart accounts, on chain.                                                                                                                                   |
| TB8      | Playground page and server              | The Playground page in a visitor's browser, holding a testnet key, and its server: the relay to sellers, the Bazaar and facilitator proxies and the lab sellers.                                              |
| TB9      | Testnet and pubnet inside one service   | One process, one database, one rate limiter, one metrics endpoint, one log stream, one CPU and memory limit and one embedder, serving a keyless public network and a real-funds network.                      |
| TB10     | Operator key custody                    | The cold treasury on a hardware wallet, offline seed generation, file secrets on the host, the hot sponsor's signer set and the encrypted seed backup.                                                        |
| TB11     | Ledger sources and the Explorer indexer | Ledger close meta from the public data lake and from Lumeus RPC getLedgers into the classifier and the Explorer database; every ledger is verified before it is classified.                                   |
| TB12     | Explorer public pages and read API      | Anonymous visitors and scripts on the internet, the Explorer's pages and JSON API, and the reader-role connection to the Explorer database behind them; live lookups to a network RPC.                        |
| TB13     | Hosted MCP and remote agents            | The public, keyless, discovery-only Streamable HTTP MCP endpoint of each network, and the remote agents that call it.                                                                                         |
| TB14     | Monitoring and status                   | Metrics scraping, alert rules and routing, the blackbox prober, the off-host status page and dead-man's switch, and the alert channels.                                                                       |
| TB15     | Source, release and deploy              | Repository main, tags, CI, the package and image registries, the operator workstation and the deploy to the host.                                                                                             |
| TB16     | Upstream watch                          | Upstream repositories and npm metadata read by a scheduled workflow that writes the project tracker with a scoped token.                                                                                      |
| TB17     | The host and co-located services        | Other tenants' containers, the host administrator path, the shared edge proxy and its access logs, and the DNS automation token.                                                                              |
| TB18     | Conformance harness and the treasury    | Harness processes that sign with the treasury and its derived keys, the run ledger they share, and the third-party test suite one of them starts.                                                             |
| TB19     | The Lumeus discovery source             | The hosted Lumeus Activity API that tells the Explorer indexer which ledgers hold transactions of registry accounts and upto contracts, reached over https with a network-scoped server key read from a file. |

{/* generated:end */}

## Summary

STRIDE letters: Spoofing, Tampering, Repudiation, Information disclosure, Denial of service, Elevation of
privilege. "Accepted risk" means the threat cannot be fully closed by a facilitator (for example a payer racing
its own payment within one ledger); the residual risk says what bounds it.

## Replay

A Stellar payment authorization is not a signed transaction but a Soroban authorization entry: a signature
over one contract invocation, a nonce and an expiration ledger, bound to the network by its passphrase. The
host consumes the nonce when the entry is used, so funds can move only once. What remains for the facilitator
is not to pay for duplicates, to give every repeat the same answer, and to stop a seller from serving one
payment many times.

{/* generated:begin topic-replay */}

### R1. The same authorization is settled twice

Mitigated · Tampering, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** A seller, a buyer or anyone holding a payment sends the same PAYMENT-SIGNATURE to /settle again, one after another or many at once, hoping the payer is charged more than once.

**What Stellar makes different.** An exact or upto authorization is a Soroban authorization entry whose nonce the host consumes when the entry is used; a second use fails on chain with Error(Auth, ExistingValue). The nonce lives under the payer's own address, for G and C accounts alike, until the signature expires. A duplicate that reaches the network is included and fails, so the sponsor still pays its fee.

**Preconditions.** A copy of a payment payload that was already sent to /settle. **Impact.** Without protection: one wasted sponsor fee per duplicate. The payer can never be charged twice, because the host refuses the second use of the nonce.

**Mitigations**

- One settlement record per (network, payer, nonce), claimed before any RPC work; later requests answer from the record instead of submitting. ([`packages/store-postgres/migrations/0001_settlement.sql`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/migrations/0001_settlement.sql), `UNIQUE (network, payer, nonce)`)
- The settle path looks the authorization up first and answers a repeat with the recorded response, marked as a repeat. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `answerRepeat`)
- A request whose terms differ from the recorded request is refused, never given the stored success. ([`packages/facilitator/src/fingerprint.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/fingerprint.ts), `requestHash`)
- Verify reads the ledger and refuses an authorization that is being settled or already was. ([`packages/facilitator/src/scheme.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/scheme.ts), `alreadySubmitted`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts): answers a repeated settle with the original result and moves funds once
- [`packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts): settles concurrent duplicates exactly once, all callers getting the same result
- [`apps/rail402/test/idempotency.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/idempotency.integration.test.ts): settles 5 concurrent repeats once: one transfer, one original, identical bodies
- [`apps/rail402/test/idempotency.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/idempotency.integration.test.ts): never answers a request for different terms with the stored success
- [`apps/rail402/test/idempotency.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/idempotency.integration.test.ts): reports a settlement in progress to verify, then the used authorization
- [`packages/facilitator/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/testing.ts): grants exactly one claim per authorization, even under concurrency

**Conformance runs.** `live-matrix` case `replay-idempotent`, `live-matrix` case `replay-concurrent`, `live-matrix` case `replay-late`.

**Residual risk.** None for funds. A repeat costs a database read, and rate limits bound how often it can be asked.

### R2. The same authorization in a new envelope

Mitigated · Tampering · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Sponsor XLM

**Threat.** An attacker wraps an already settled authorization entry in a transaction of different bytes (another source, sequence or fee) so it is not recognised as a repeat.

**What Stellar makes different.** The authorization is bound to its invocation, not to the transaction, so it can be placed in any envelope. The host refuses the consumed nonce at apply time; a facilitator that only compared envelope bytes would submit it and pay the fee.

**Preconditions.** A copy of a settled authorization entry. **Impact.** One wasted sponsor fee per attempt if it reached the network.

**Mitigations**

- The settlement key is the payer's address and nonce, read from the authorization entry, not the envelope hash. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `keyOf`)
- A different envelope for a recorded authorization is refused with the idempotency conflict, naming the recorded transaction. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `conflict`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/idempotency.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/idempotency.integration.test.ts): refuses the same authorization in another envelope, naming the recorded transaction
- [`packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts): refuses a settled authorization in a different envelope, and verify names the replay
- [`packages/facilitator/test/state.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/state.test.ts): reports a conflict when the same authorization arrives in a different envelope

**Conformance runs.** `live-matrix` case `replay-rejected`.

**Residual risk.** None: nothing is submitted.

### R3. An authorization replayed on another network

Mitigated · Tampering · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** A payment signed for stellar:testnet is presented for stellar:pubnet, or the reverse.

**What Stellar makes different.** The signed preimage (HashIdPreimageSorobanAuthorization) includes the network id, the SHA-256 of the network passphrase, so a signature is only valid on the network it was made for.

**Preconditions.** A signed payment for one network. **Impact.** None on chain: signature verification fails on the other network.

**Mitigations**

- The network id is part of every signed preimage the buyer's guard decodes and checks. ([`packages/stellar/src/auth.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/stellar/src/auth.ts), `networkIdForPassphrase`)
- A request is served only on the network its requirements name, and a payload for another network is refused. ([`apps/rail402/src/app.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/app.ts), `invalid_network`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/stellar/test/auth.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/stellar/test/auth.test.ts): is SHA-256 of the passphrase
- [`packages/sdk/test/decide.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/decide.test.ts): refuses another network, whether in the preimage or in the requested passphrase
- [`apps/rail402/test/app.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/app.test.ts): refuses networks and schemes it does not serve without calling the core

**Conformance runs.** `rejection-audit` case `unknown-network`.

**Residual risk.** None.

### R4. Paid once, served many times

Accepted risk · Spoofing, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB1 (Buyer and seller) · asset: Seller revenue

**Threat.** A buyer pays once and sends the same PAYMENT-SIGNATURE several times, concurrently or later. A stock upfront seller calls /settle without /verify; in the authorization flow, copies sent together all pass /verify before the first one settles. Either way the seller serves on a successful /settle, and an idempotent facilitator answers every repeat with the same success.

**What Stellar makes different.** Nothing Stellar-specific allows it: it follows from answering repeats with the stored result, which the x402 idempotency requirement asks for. The Stellar part is that the answer carries the one settlement transaction hash, which a seller can deduplicate on.

**Preconditions.** A seller that serves on a successful /settle without remembering which settlements it already served. **Impact.** The seller serves a paid resource more than once for one payment. The buyer is charged once.

**Mitigations**

- Every repeated /settle answer carries a repeat marker in EXTENSION-RESPONSES (rail402.settlement.repeat), with the attempt id of the request that holds the settlement's claim as the repeat is answered (rail402.settlement.attempt): the one that started it, or the one that took a stale claim over and settled it, whose id replaces the abandoned request's, also for a repeat that waited through the takeover. The Bazaar never lists rail402 as an extension a resource declares. ([`packages/facilitator/src/responses.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/responses.ts), `markRepeat`)
- The seller helpers refuse a second request whose settlement is a repeat or a transaction already served. Each /settle they send carries a new random attempt id, and the marker is set aside only when it echoes one of their own attempts that may have settled the payment without a success served for it (the facilitator answered settlement_pending, or /settle brought no answer of the facilitator's own: anything but its x402 settle body). That success is served once. A marked success that echoes another id (another seller, replica, an earlier run, or a request that took over a stale claim), no id, or an id already resumed is refused, whatever the timing; a refusal in the facilitator's own x402 body waits for nothing, and a refused repeat never counts as the serve. ([`packages/sdk/src/seller/dedupe.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/seller/dedupe.ts), `withSettleDedupe`)
- In the authorization flow, verify refuses an authorization that is being settled or was, so a copy sent after the first settlement started never reaches the handler. ([`packages/facilitator/src/scheme.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/scheme.ts), `alreadySubmitted`)
- The demo seller settles every route and tool, exact and upto, through withSettleDedupe, and so do the Playground's lab sellers; the seller quickstart, the HTTP seller example and the seller helpers page wrap their facilitator client the same way, in both flows. ([`apps/demo-seller/src/seller.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/demo-seller/src/seller.ts), `withSettleDedupe`)
- The Playground's lab sellers wrap their facilitator client in withSettleDedupe inside the trace recorder, so a refused copy is traced as refused. ([`apps/playground/src/lab/mount.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/src/lab/mount.ts), `withSettleDedupe`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/app.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/app.test.ts): sends the recorded body and moves the repeat marker into EXTENSION-RESPONSES
- [`packages/sdk/test/seller.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/seller.test.ts): trusts Rail402's repeat marker, even for a settlement it never saw
- [`packages/sdk/test/seller.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/seller.test.ts): serves a payment the stock resource server settles again after settlement_pending
- [`packages/sdk/test/seller.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/seller.test.ts): serves a pending payment once, whichever of its copies or retries succeeds first
- [`packages/sdk/test/seller.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/seller.test.ts): serves the first success of a payment whose earlier settle failed in transit
- [`packages/sdk/test/seller.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/seller.test.ts): never waits on a payment the facilitator refused outright
- [`packages/sdk/test/seller.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/seller.test.ts): never waits again on a payment whose success it already answered, even after it fails in transit
- [`packages/sdk/test/settle-dedupe.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/settle-dedupe.test.ts): serves a payment whose first settle answer was lost once, when the buyer sends it again
- [`packages/sdk/test/settle-dedupe.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/settle-dedupe.test.ts): never serves a replayed payment again after a restart, even once the service refused a flood of its copies

- [`packages/sdk/test/seller.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/seller.test.ts): sets the marker aside only for a settlement one of its own unfinished attempts started

- [`apps/demo-seller/test/seller.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/demo-seller/test/seller.test.ts): serves concurrent copies of one paid tool call once, metered or not
- [`apps/playground/test/lab-sellers.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/lab-sellers.test.ts): serves a payment once when copies of it arrive together, and traces the refused copy

**Conformance runs.** `live-matrix` case `replay-idempotent`.

**Residual risk.** A seller on stock middleware, in either flow, that does not wrap its facilitator client with withSettleDedupe, or deduplicate PAYMENT-RESPONSE.transaction itself, can serve concurrent copies of one payment. The seller quickstart, the seller helpers page and the HTTP seller example all wrap it. A seller paired with a facilitator that does not record and echo the attempt id refuses buyers whose answer was lost or pending; with a facilitator that sends no marker at all, wrappers that can accept the same payment must share one memory.

### R5. An upto authorization settled for zero stays usable

Mitigated · Tampering · boundary TB7 (Contracts) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** A seller settles an upto payment for zero, then tries to charge the same authorization again for a positive amount.

**What Stellar makes different.** An on-chain zero settlement still uses the payer's authorization entry, so the host consumes its nonce like any other settlement. An off-chain zero (UPTO_ZERO_SETTLEMENT=offchain) sends nothing, so the nonce stays unused on chain until the signature expires.

**Preconditions.** An upto payment settled for zero. **Impact.** Without protection, a second settlement of the same authorization for up to the cap.

**Mitigations**

- The upto contract consumes the payer's nonce for a zero settlement too. ([`contracts/stellar/upto/src/lib.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/lib.rs), `pub fn settle`)
- An off-chain zero is a terminal record in the settlement ledger, so a later request for any amount is answered from it or refused as a conflict; only the named authority can settle on chain, and it is Rail402's. ([`packages/facilitator/src/upto-scheme.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/upto-scheme.ts), `offchainZero`)
- A database restore can discard that record while the authorization is still valid, so every restore is recorded (restore-dump.sh in its own transaction, creating the table for an older dump; the runbook after a point-in-time recovery; the migration that adds the record, for a database that already holds settlements or was migrated earlier). Every upto network, whatever its zero settlement mode and ledger time now, then verifies no upto payment and settles none that moves funds until the chain has passed the longest window rule 7 can accept (the largest maxTimeoutSeconds at the shortest ledger time it counts, one second), counted from a ledger recorded only by recordRestoreHold (the service's network poll): the ledger the chain can have reached by the time of a reading from a provider of that network whose newest ledger closed within the last minute (that ledger plus one per second since). Requests never record or move it and, until it is recorded, hold from their own reading. The poll starts the hold again from a reset test network's new chain only after corroborating the reset (the same checks and a second poll), and never on pubnet. ([`packages/facilitator/src/upto-scheme.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/upto-scheme.ts), `restoreHold`)

**Tests**

- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/signed.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/signed.rs): a_zero_settlement_also_consumes_the_nonce
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto.integration.test.ts): settles zero on-chain: the nonce is consumed and no funds move
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto.integration.test.ts): settles zero off-chain when configured: no transaction, the authorization used up in the ledger
- [`packages/facilitator/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/testing.ts): finishes an upto zero-amount success without an envelope, only for the claim's owner
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-scheme.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-scheme.test.ts): holds on-chain settlement where zeros settle off-chain, so a zero the restore lost is not charged again
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-scheme.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-scheme.test.ts): settles on chain again once the chain has passed the longest window rule 7 can accept
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-scheme.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-scheme.test.ts): keeps the hold whatever ledger time is set now, so raising it at the restore does not shorten it

- [`packages/store-postgres/test/restore-record.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/test/restore-record.test.ts): is created by the restore script exactly as by its migration, for a dump older than the table
- [`tools/conformance/test/roles.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/roles.integration.test.ts): restores a dump with the owner keeping every table and the runtime role its privileges
- [`tools/conformance/test/roles.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/roles.integration.test.ts): records the restore of a dump taken before restores were recorded, even without settlements
- [`apps/rail402/test/upto.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/upto.integration.test.ts): holds on-chain settlement after a database restore, from the first ledger a replica saw after it

**Conformance runs.** `live-matrix` case `upto-zero`.

**Residual risk.** With off-chain zeros and STORE=memory, a restart forgets the record; the authorization then stays settleable by Rail402's own authority until it expires. Use Postgres wherever off-chain zeros are enabled. The hold after a restore is counted in ledgers from the current TIMEOUT_MAX_SECONDS at the shortest ledger time rule 7 counts, so no ledger time setting, before or after the restore, shortens it. Lowering TIMEOUT_MAX_SECONDS in the same restore does, and so could a point-in-time recovery the operator did not record, a failover to an asynchronous standby that lost recent commits (not recorded at all), rolling back to a release that predates the record without waiting out the hold, or the runtime role setting the recorded ledger to 0 or deleting the record on a one-role host (it can change that ledger on any host, as it could already reopen an off-chain zero by deleting its settlement row): an authorization could then be settled once more, never above its cap and only to its payTo. The first ledger is recorded from one provider's reading that passed the passphrase and freshness checks (its newest ledger closed within the last minute, and not in the future), as that ledger plus one for every second since it closed, the most the chain can have reached by then, so an honest reading up to a minute old cannot set it too low; until it is recorded every upto request holds, and rail402_upto_restore_hold_pending shows it. A provider that lies (compromised or misconfigured), answering this network's passphrase with a fresh-looking ledger far below the chain, can still record a low first ledger and void the hold on any network, pubnet included; on a test network it can also restart the hold early over two polls. Configure only providers you trust; an in-process facilitator that never runs recordRestoreHold stays held after a restore until the owner records the ledger.

{/* generated:end */}

## Front-running

Because the authorization is bound to its invocation rather than to a transaction, anyone who sees it before it
lands can submit it in a transaction of their own. That can only make the signed payment happen, exactly as
signed; it cannot redirect it. The cost is Rail402's own failed transaction, and the risk is telling a seller
"failed" for a payment that settled.

{/* generated:begin topic-front-running */}

### F1. A copied exact authorization lands first

Accepted risk · Denial of service · boundary TB3 (Facilitator and Stellar) · asset: Sponsor XLM

**Threat.** Someone who sees the settlement envelope (the seller, an RPC operator, a validator's pending queue) copies the payer's authorization entry into a transaction of their own and gets it included before Rail402's.

**What Stellar makes different.** A Soroban authorization is a bearer instrument for exactly one invocation: whoever submits it can only make the signed transfer happen, from the payer to payTo for the signed amount. It cannot be redirected. Rail402's own transaction then fails on the consumed nonce and its fee is spent.

**Preconditions.** Access to the envelope between signing and inclusion, and an account to pay the copy's fee. **Impact.** One wasted sponsor fee per event. The attacker pays its own fee and gains nothing; the payment settles as signed.

**Mitigations**

- Rail402 submits as soon as the settle-time simulation passes, which leaves little time to copy the entry. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `execute`)
- Signed envelopes and authorizations are redacted from logs. ([`apps/rail402/src/logger.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/logger.ts), `REDACTED_PATHS`)
- Wasted fees are counted, so griefing is visible and bounded by the hourly budget. ([`apps/rail402/src/metrics.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/metrics.ts), `wasted`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/front-run.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/front-run.integration.test.ts): answers success with the attacker's hash; the payment moves once and our fee is wasted
- [`apps/rail402/test/logger.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/logger.test.ts): redacts secret fields at the top level and one level down

**Residual risk.** The wasted fee is not recoverable. Each event costs the attacker its own fee too.

### F2. The seller is told the payment failed although it settled

Mitigated · Repudiation · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Seller revenue

**Threat.** After a front-run, Rail402's transaction fails with a consumed nonce and the seller is told the payment failed, although the payer was charged. Likewise when Rail402's rebroadcast is refused and someone else lands its public transaction in a fee bump of their own.

**What Stellar makes different.** The chain holds the proof: the nonce entry's last-modified ledger, and in that ledger a successful transaction whose address credential (payer, nonce, signature expiration) and root invocation are byte-equal to the payer's signed entry, with the matching token transfer event.

**Preconditions.** A front-run as in F1, or a refused rebroadcast of a settlement transaction a send may have exposed. **Impact.** Without reconciliation the seller withholds a resource that was paid for.

**Mitigations**

- A failed settlement whose nonce was consumed is reconciled: Rail402 finds the other transaction on chain, proves it carried the same signed invocation after the request was claimed, and answers success with its hash. ([`packages/facilitator/src/third-party.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/third-party.ts), `findThirdPartySettlement`)
- Proof requires a byte-equal root invocation and credential; a different invocation for the same nonce stays a failure. ([`packages/facilitator/src/third-party.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/third-party.ts), `signedAuthorizationOf`)
- The failure is kept only when the search read RPCs that have reached the ledger Rail402's transaction failed in and hold the ledger that consumed the nonce; a read from a lagging provider, a backend behind a balancer or another provider after a failover, a backend whose history starts after the consuming ledger, or a read error, leaves the settlement open for the next pass, which reads the failure again and searches with fresh reads. One that stays undecided for a minute is reported as unresolvable, and a failure on the used nonce whose nonce reads unused at the ledger it failed in (its entry lapsed) is reported as unresolvable at once instead of kept. ([`packages/facilitator/src/third-party.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/third-party.ts), `rpc_behind`)
- An upto settlement whose rebroadcast the network refuses for good (a frozen sponsor key, say) after a send may have exposed its transaction keeps waiting until no later ledger can include that transaction, since anyone can wrap it in a fee bump of their own until then; the inner-hash lookup finds such a landing and the payment is answered as settled. One whose sends were all refused finishes at once. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `includableAfter`)
- An exact settlement included after the payer's authorization expired is searched the same way: the host checks the expiration before the nonce, so a copy that landed before the expiration makes Rail402's transaction fail as expired, and the payment is answered as settled with the copy's hash. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `invalid_exact_stellar_signature_expired`)
- An exact settlement whose own transaction the network refused for good, or can never apply because another transaction took its channel's sequence number, is decided from the payer's nonce, never at once: anyone who holds the payment can carry its authorization in a transaction of their own until it expires. A copy that landed is answered as settled with its hash, and the settlement is answered as failed only once the authorization expired unused, so a seller is never told to refuse a buyer, or a buyer to pay again, while the first payment can still land. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `othersCanCarry`)
- An exact settlement whose own transaction was included and failed for a cause that does not end the authorization (a balance spent, a trustline removed or deauthorized, an account or smart account that refused it for now, a token error, a failure without diagnostics) is not finished either: the failure rolls back, the payer's nonce included, so anyone who holds the payment can still land it until it expires. An expired-authorization error ends it only when the transaction failed past the payer's own expiration ledger, since a contract in the call path or a shorter-lived entry can raise one. The settlement answers settlement_pending, gives its channel back at once, and is searched as above; it is answered with its on-chain cause, the fee counted as wasted, once the nonce reads unused past the expiration. The SDK reports those causes as paid unknown, never retryable, so a buyer is not invited to pay again while the first payment can still land. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `endsAuthorization`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/front-run.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/front-run.integration.test.ts): answers success with the attacker's hash; the payment moves once and our fee is wasted
- [`packages/facilitator/test/front-run.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/front-run.integration.test.ts): refuses an authorization consumed before the request was claimed
- [`packages/facilitator/test/front-run.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/front-run.integration.test.ts): refuses when the consuming transaction carried a different invocation for the same nonce
- [`packages/facilitator/test/front-run.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/front-run.integration.test.ts): refuses an authorization whose nonce was never consumed
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): keep a failure on the consumed nonce open while the nonce read lags, then succeed through the other transaction
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): keep an exact failure on the consumed nonce open while the nonce read lags, then succeed through the copy
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): keep a failure open while the consuming ledger is scanned on an RPC that has not ingested it
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): keep a failure open while the search for the other transaction cannot be read
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): report a settlement whose search stays behind for a minute as unresolvable
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): keep a failure open while the consuming ledger is older than the history of the RPC that scans it
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): leave a used-nonce failure unresolved when its nonce reads unused at the ledger it failed in
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): keep waiting while a send may have been accepted, then succeed through someone else's fee bump
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): expire once the ledger bound passed and nothing carried the authorization
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): keep waiting when a send the look's copy of the settlement predates was accepted
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): fail at once when no send of the bytes was ever accepted
- [`packages/facilitator/test/front-run.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/front-run.integration.test.ts): answers success with the attacker's hash when ours lands after the authorization expired
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): succeed through a copy of the authorization that landed before the expiration
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): keep the failure open while the nonce read lags behind the ledger it failed in, then succeed
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): keep the failure when nothing consumed the nonce
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): answers a copy of the authorization that landed before its first submission was refused
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): answers the same copy alike when the refusal came at a later rebroadcast
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): keeps a refused first submission open while its authorization is valid, and answers a later copy
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): gives its channel back at a proven sequence conflict, and answers a later copy
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): finishes a sequence conflict as failed once the authorization expired unused, releasing nothing twice
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): keeps a failure on the payer's balance open while the authorization is valid, and answers a later copy
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): keeps a failure on a removed trustline until the authorization expired unused, then names it with the fee charged
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): keeps a smart account's failed signature open while the authorization is valid, and answers a later copy
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): names a failure on the used nonce at once when no copy carried the authorization
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): keeps an expired-authorization error raised by a smart account's own check before the payer's expiration open, and answers a later copy
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): keeps an expired-authorization error raised by a token contract before the payer's expiration open, and answers a later copy
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): names an expired authorization at once when the transaction failed past the payer's expiration
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): sends nothing more once its transaction failed inside another fee bump and gave its channel back
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): sends nothing after its own failed transaction gave the channel back, when a lagging RPC then misses it
- [`packages/sdk/test/refusal.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/refusal.test.ts): applies the table to every code it holds, and never invites a second payment

**Residual risk.** A settlement whose consuming ledger no RPC still holds stays unresolved and is reported (O4); reconciliation runs immediately after the failure, well inside the retention window. Soroban keeps a consumed nonce's entry until the later of the authorization's expiration and the consuming ledger plus the network's minimum temporary entry lifetime (about a day on pubnet, an hour on testnet); a failure without diagnostics, on an expired authorization, or on any exact cause that leaves the authorization usable, first searched after that finds no consumption and is kept as failed, while one on the used nonce is left unresolved.

### F3. A third party settles an upto payment for another amount by submitting it

Mitigated · Tampering, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB7 (Contracts) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** Someone holding an upto payload (the buyer, the seller, a TLS-terminating proxy, a log, an RPC operator who sees the settlement envelope) submits it themselves for another amount up to the cap, such as zero or the whole cap, before or instead of the seller's settlement.

**What Stellar makes different.** The payer's signature covers the cap, not the amount. The upto contract therefore also requires the authorization of the settlement authority named in the signed terms, and Rail402 makes that authority the operation's source account, so its authorization is the transaction's own signature and cannot be copied into another transaction.

**Preconditions.** A copy of an upto payload. **Impact.** None: the network refuses a settlement that the settlement authority did not authorize, and Rail402's settlement proceeds. The same request through the facilitator's own /settle is F5.

**Mitigations**

- The contract requires terms.facilitator to authorize settle(payer, terms, amount), so the authority's authorization covers one amount and nothing else. ([`contracts/stellar/upto/src/lib.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/lib.rs), `terms.facilitator.require_auth()`)
- Rail402 signs as the authority through a source-account credential that exists only inside its own transaction. ([`packages/upto/src/terms.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/src/terms.ts), `authorityEntry`)

**Tests**

- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/auth.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/auth.rs): a_different_submitter_cannot_settle
- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/auth.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/auth.rs): missing_facilitator_authorization_is_rejected
- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/auth.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/auth.rs): facilitator_authorization_for_another_amount_is_rejected
- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/signed.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/signed.rs): another_source_account_cannot_act_as_the_authority
- [`packages/facilitator/test/front-run.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/front-run.integration.test.ts): cannot be settled by a payload holder for another amount: the host refuses without the authority
- [`packages/facilitator/test/front-run.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/front-run.integration.test.ts): cannot be landed by an attacker without the settlement authority; Rail402's settlement proceeds

**Residual risk.** None against anyone without the settlement authority's key; a holder of that key is U10.

### F4. Another holder of the authority settles first

Mitigated · Tampering · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Seller revenue

**Threat.** Two replicas sharing the sponsor secret, or a stale process, settle the same upto payment; one lands first with a different amount than the seller asked for.

**What Stellar makes different.** Only a transaction signed by the authority can land; the landed amount is readable from the settle arguments and the upto_settled event.

**Preconditions.** Two processes that hold the same authority key. **Impact.** Without reconciliation the seller gets a false failure, or a success for an amount it did not request.

**Mitigations**

- The later settlement is answered with the landed transaction when it settled the same amount, and with settle_upto_stellar_settled_elsewhere, naming both amounts, when it did not. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `settle_upto_stellar_settled_elsewhere`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto.integration.test.ts): succeeds with that transaction when it settled the same amount
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto.integration.test.ts): answers settled_elsewhere when it settled a different amount
- [`packages/facilitator/test/front-run.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/front-run.integration.test.ts): answers with the authority's own earlier transaction for the same amount; our fee is wasted

**Residual risk.** None beyond one wasted fee.

### F5. A payload holder fixes the upto amount through the facilitator's /settle

Accepted risk · Tampering · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Seller revenue

**Threat.** Someone holding an upto payload asks the facilitator's own /settle to settle it for another amount up to the cap, such as zero or the whole cap, before the seller does.

**What Stellar makes different.** The payer's signature covers the cap, not the amount, and the facilitator is the settlement authority: whichever accepted /settle request comes first decides the amount it authorizes, and the payer's nonce then makes the authorization single-use.

**Preconditions.** A copy of an upto payload and access to the facilitator's /settle (keyless on the public testnet service). **Impact.** The first accepted request fixes the amount anywhere in \[0, cap\] to payTo: a buyer who settles zero first leaves the seller unpaid for that request, and anyone who settles the cap first charges the buyer the full cap. The seller's own later request for a different amount is refused as an idempotency conflict. Funds never go elsewhere and never exceed the cap.

**Mitigations**

- The facilitator settles an authorization once: the first accepted amount wins, and a later request for a different amount is refused with settle_upto_stellar_idempotency_conflict naming the amount that holds it. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `idempotencyConflict`)
- Operators can require API keys for /verify and /settle per network, so only known sellers can settle; the payload travels only from buyer to seller over TLS, and sellers settle before releasing a buffered response. ([`apps/rail402/src/app.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/app.ts), `requireApiKey`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/upto-settlement.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/upto-settlement.integration.test.ts): lands one transaction for 20 concurrent settlements of different amounts and refuses the rest
- [`apps/rail402/test/upto-settlement.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/upto-settlement.integration.test.ts): lets the first settle request fix the amount: a payload holder's zero turns the seller's settle into a conflict

**Residual risk.** Accepted for payload holders on a keyless facilitator: a buyer or anyone who sees the payload can fix the amount through /settle before the seller (unpaid seller compute and one sponsor fee, or the buyer charged up to its own cap). Operators who need more require API keys for /settle.

{/* generated:end */}

## Fee sponsorship

The sponsor pays the fee of every transaction it submits, and Soroban charges resource fees whether the
transaction succeeds or fails. Every refusal Rail402 can make before signing costs nothing; a transaction that
is included and fails costs its fee. Rail402 refuses whatever it can see coming, names and counts what it
cannot, and bounds the total.

{/* generated:begin topic-fee-sponsorship */}

### S1. A payment passes simulation and fails at apply

Accepted risk · Denial of service · boundary TB3 (Facilitator and Stellar) · asset: Sponsor XLM

**Threat.** A payer spends the balance, or changes a smart account's state, after the settle-time simulation and before inclusion, so the settlement is included and fails.

**What Stellar makes different.** Soroban resource fees are charged when a transaction is included, success or failure; the sponsor pays through the fee bump. Simulation runs against the latest ledger, so a change made in the ledgers between simulation and inclusion is not seen.

**Preconditions.** A payer (or its smart-account policy) able to act within about one ledger of the settlement. **Impact.** One wasted sponsor fee per event, never more than the fee ceiling. No funds move.

**Mitigations**

- Settle simulates again with the current ledger, so a change made before settle is refused without submitting. ([`packages/facilitator/src/scheme.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/scheme.ts), `settle`)
- The failure is named from the transaction's diagnostic events and counted as wasted fee. It rolls back, the payer's nonce included, so the exact settlement waits until the authorization expired unused before it answers the failure, and answers success with a copy's hash when someone who holds the payment lands it meanwhile (F2); its channel is given back at once. ([`packages/facilitator/src/explain.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/explain.ts), `explainFailedTransaction`)
- The hourly fee budget and the reserve floor bound the total a griefer can burn. ([`packages/facilitator/src/guard.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/guard.ts), `SponsorBudget`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/fee-exposure.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/fee-exposure.integration.test.ts): refuses at settle, with nothing submitted, a payment whose balance moved after verify
- [`packages/facilitator/test/fee-exposure.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/fee-exposure.integration.test.ts): names a balance spent between the settle-time simulation and inclusion; the fee is spent and counted as wasted
- [`packages/facilitator/test/fee-exposure.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/fee-exposure.integration.test.ts): stops settling once the hourly fee budget is committed, before any RPC work

**Residual risk.** The race inside one ledger cannot be closed by a facilitator that does not hold the funds. Each event costs the payer an on-chain transaction of its own.

### S2. An authorization expires between submission and inclusion

Accepted risk · Denial of service · boundary TB3 (Facilitator and Stellar) · asset: Sponsor XLM

**Threat.** A payment is settled with an authorization whose expiration ledger passes while the transaction waits for inclusion.

**What Stellar makes different.** Validity runs through signatureExpirationLedger inclusive; the transaction's time bound can outlive it. An included transaction whose authorization expired fails and its fee is spent.

**Preconditions.** A payment settled at the very end of its validity window. **Impact.** One wasted sponsor fee.

**Mitigations**

- Settle refuses an authorization that expires within a one-ledger margin of the current ledger. ([`packages/facilitator/src/preflight.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/preflight.ts), `invalid_exact_stellar_signature_expiration_too_soon`)
- An upto settlement's ledger bounds end at the expiration ledger, so the network refuses it before inclusion and no fee is spent. ([`packages/upto/src/facilitator/scheme.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/src/facilitator/scheme.ts), `buildTransaction`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/expiration.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/expiration.integration.test.ts): holds the lower edge at the current ledger plus a one-ledger margin
- [`packages/facilitator/test/expiration.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/expiration.integration.test.ts): names an authorization that expires between submission and inclusion; the fee is spent
- [`packages/facilitator/test/expiration.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/expiration.integration.test.ts): refuses at the network an upto settlement held past expiration_ledger; no fee is spent

**Conformance runs.** `live-matrix` case `expiry-window`.

**Residual risk.** For exact, a transaction that waits longer than the margin in the network's queue can still fail this way; it is named and counted as wasted. When a copy of the authorization landed before the expiration, the failure is searched and the payment answered as settled (F2).

### S3. A payment that is expensive to settle

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Sponsor XLM

**Threat.** A payer submits a payment whose settlement needs large resources: a heavy \_\_check_auth, a token that writes a lot of state, or rent for entries it extends.

**What Stellar makes different.** Soroban fees depend on the instructions, ledger reads and writes, and rent of the invocation, which the payer's account and the token contract choose. The fee is known from simulation before anything is signed.

**Preconditions.** A smart account or token contract whose calls are costly. **Impact.** Without a ceiling, an arbitrary fee per settlement.

**Mitigations**

- Verify and settle refuse a payment whose simulated fee exceeds MAX_TX_FEE_STROOPS (UPTO_MAX_TX_FEE_STROOPS for upto), before signing. ([`packages/facilitator/src/policy.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/policy.ts), `DEFAULT_MAX_TRANSACTION_FEE_STROOPS`)
- The inclusion-fee bid is capped. ([`packages/facilitator/src/fees.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/fees.ts), `InclusionFeeOracle`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/fee-exposure.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/fee-exposure.integration.test.ts): refuses a payment whose settlement would cost more than the fee ceiling, before signing anything
- [`packages/upto/test/facilitator.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/test/facilitator.test.ts): enforces the fee ceiling
- [`packages/facilitator/test/fees.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/fees.test.ts): bids the configured percentile, clamped between floor and cap

**Residual risk.** A payment just under the ceiling costs up to the ceiling. Operators accepting tokens that pay rent on read raise the ceiling knowingly (the TEST6 token needs more than 1,000,000 stroops on testnet, and the testnet default of 2,000,000 leaves room for its rent price to rise).

### S4. A flood of valid small payments

Accepted risk · Denial of service · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Sponsor XLM

**Threat.** Someone makes many real, valid payments of the smallest amount to drain the sponsor through fees.

**What Stellar makes different.** Every valid settlement costs the sponsor a fee of tens of thousands of stroops, far more than a one-unit USDC payment is worth.

**Preconditions.** Funds for many small payments. **Impact.** Sponsor XLM spent at the attacker's pace.

**Mitigations**

- An hourly fee budget, shared by replicas through the settlement ledger, stops new settlements once reached; pubnet refuses to start without one. ([`packages/facilitator/src/guard.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/guard.ts), `maxSpendPerHourStroops`)
- Per-client rate limits and API keys on /verify and /settle; API keys must be decided explicitly for pubnet. ([`apps/rail402/src/rate-limit.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/rate-limit.ts), `RateLimiter`)
- Per-asset minimum amounts for exact payments and upto caps (an upto settlement may still be zero; see U6). ([`apps/rail402/src/config.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/config.ts), `parseAssets`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/fee-exposure.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/fee-exposure.integration.test.ts): stops settling once the hourly fee budget is committed, before any RPC work
- [`apps/rail402/test/app.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/app.test.ts): rate-limits per client with Retry-After and a coded body
- [`apps/rail402/test/app.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/app.test.ts): rate-limits an IPv6 client by its /64 and a mapped IPv4 client as IPv4
- [`apps/rail402/test/app.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/app.test.ts): enforces API keys when the network requires them
- [`apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts): requires explicit, safe mainnet settings

**Conformance runs.** `rejection-audit` case `rate-limited`, `rejection-audit` case `api-key-required`.

**Residual risk.** On a free, keyless testnet facilitator the per-client rate limit (IPv6 clients are grouped by /64) and, where configured, the hourly budget are the only bounds. A client with many addresses passes the rate limit, so the budget is the real bound: once it is spent, every settlement is refused until the hour moves on, which trades availability for the sponsor's balance. On pubnet the budget is required, and pricing, keys and minimums are an operator decision.

### S5. The sponsor falls below its reserve

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB3 (Facilitator and Stellar) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** The sponsor's balance runs low, from normal use or an attack, until fee bumps start failing mid-settlement.

**What Stellar makes different.** The sponsor also carries the base reserves of the channel accounts and the upto authority, which it sponsors; below them it cannot pay fees at all.

**Preconditions.** Sustained settlement volume or an attack as in S4. **Impact.** Settlements fail after signing; the service becomes unavailable.

**Mitigations**

- Settlements are refused below SPONSOR_RESERVE_XLM before any RPC work, with a retryable code. ([`packages/facilitator/src/guard.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/guard.ts), `minBalanceStroops`)
- /ready fails below MIN_SPONSOR_BALANCE_XLM and /health reports it. ([`apps/rail402/src/health.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/health.ts), `sponsor_below_floor`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/fees.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/fees.test.ts): refuses while the sponsor is below its reserve, admits otherwise
- [`apps/rail402/test/observability.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/observability.integration.test.ts): counts refusals below the reserve and reports the sponsor below its floor
- [`apps/rail402/test/runtime.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/runtime.integration.test.ts): fails while the sponsor holds less than MIN_SPONSOR_BALANCE_XLM
- [`packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts): refuses while the sponsor guard is closed, then settles once it reopens

**Residual risk.** None beyond downtime until the sponsor is refunded.

### S6. Duplicate submissions of one settlement

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB3 (Facilitator and Stellar) · asset: Sponsor XLM

**Threat.** Retries, concurrent requests or two replicas submit the same settlement more than once.

**What Stellar makes different.** Each submitted transaction consumes a channel sequence number and pays its own fee; the second one fails on the consumed nonce.

**Preconditions.** Concurrent or repeated requests for one payment. **Impact.** Wasted fees and sequence conflicts.

**Mitigations**

- One claim per authorization across replicas; only the claim holder signs and submits. ([`packages/store-postgres/src/settlement-ledger.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/src/settlement-ledger.ts), `claim`)
- A crashed settlement is resumed from its recorded envelope bytes, never rebuilt. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `recordingSponsor`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/concurrency.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/concurrency.integration.test.ts): with injected duplicates, without a collision
- [`apps/rail402/test/replicas.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/replicas.integration.test.ts): with every duplicate sent to the other replica
- [`packages/upto/test/facilitator.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/test/facilitator.test.ts): submits one transaction for 20 concurrent identical settlements

**Conformance runs.** `concurrency` case `distinct-sequences`.

**Residual risk.** None.

### S7. Restoring archived state inside a buyer's payment

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB7 (Contracts) · asset: Sponsor XLM

**Threat.** A settlement touches an archived contract instance or entry, and the transaction would restore it, billing the restoration rent to the sponsor as part of the payment.

**What Stellar makes different.** Since protocol 23 a freshly simulated invocation of an archived persistent entry restores it automatically inside the transaction, and the fee payer pays the rent.

**Preconditions.** An archived settlement contract, token instance or balance entry. **Impact.** Rent paid by the sponsor on behalf of whoever let the entry expire.

**Mitigations**

- A simulation that reports archived state is refused: the upto settlement contract with invalid_upto_stellar_settlement_contract_archived, other entries with the archived-state code; nothing is submitted. ([`packages/upto/src/facilitator/simulation.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/src/facilitator/simulation.ts), `archivedStateRejection`)
- Exact payments name archived state from the simulation's restore preamble. ([`packages/facilitator/src/explain.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/explain.ts), `explainSimulation`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/upto/test/facilitator.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/test/facilitator.test.ts): refuses when the settlement contract must be restored, and names other archived state
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-scheme.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-scheme.test.ts): refuses to pay for restoring an archived settlement contract
- [`packages/facilitator/test/explain.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/explain.test.ts): names archived ledger state when simulation asks for a restore
- [`apps/rail402/test/upto-archival.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/upto-archival.integration.test.ts): refuses payments while the contract is archived, and settles once an operator restores it

**Residual risk.** None; restoration is an operator action (U7).

### S8. Dust self-payments drain the pubnet fee budget for every seller

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Sponsor XLM

**Threat.** A caller settles many valid payments of the smallest amount, often to a payTo it controls, so the sponsor pays a full fee each time and the hourly budget is spent for everyone.

**What Stellar makes different.** A token transfer settlement costs the sponsor a resource fee of tens of thousands of stroops, while the payment can be one base unit; paying oneself is legal on chain.

**Preconditions.** One funded account with a trustline; on pubnet an API key when keys are required. **Impact.** Without controls the hourly budget is exhausted within minutes and every other seller's settlements are refused until the hour moves on.

**Mitigations**

- Each access key carries its own hourly sponsor budget, keyless callers share one, and both are checked with the network budget when the settlement is claimed. ([`apps/rail402/src/access.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/access.ts), `sponsorBudgetStroopsPerHour`)
- Pubnet requires an explicit minimum amount for every accepted asset and refuses to start without one. ([`apps/rail402/src/config.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/config.ts), `config_pubnet_asset_min_missing`)
- Per-payer and per-payTo settlement limits per minute, on by default on pubnet, count only payments that proved the payer's signature (just before the sponsor signs), so forged payloads naming a seller or a wallet cannot use up its quota. The payTo limit is counted per access key, with one shared count for keyless callers, so keyless dust cannot use up a keyed seller's quota. ([`packages/facilitator/src/limits.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/limits.ts), `admitVerified`)
- On pubnet a non-zero upto settlement is held to the asset's minimum, as an exact amount is; a zero settles off-chain. ([`packages/facilitator/src/upto-preflight.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/upto-preflight.ts), `amount < asset.minAmount`)
- Finished settlements count the fee actually charged against the budgets (nothing when they expired or the network refused them), so payloads that never land stop holding budget once finished. ([`packages/store-postgres/src/settlement-ledger.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/src/settlement-ledger.ts), `WHEN state = 'expired' THEN 0`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/caller-budget.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/caller-budget.test.ts): stops one key at its hourly sponsor budget while another key keeps settling
- [`apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts): requires an explicit minimum amount for every pubnet asset
- [`packages/facilitator/test/limits.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/limits.test.ts): refuses a payer past its settlement limit once its payments are proven
- [`packages/facilitator/test/limits.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/limits.test.ts): refuses a payTo past its limit, whoever pays it
- [`packages/facilitator/test/limits.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/limits.test.ts): takes no payer or payTo quota at admission, before the payment proved its signature
- [`packages/facilitator/test/party-limits.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/party-limits.integration.test.ts): forged payloads naming a payTo or a payer use none of their quota
- [`packages/facilitator/test/limits.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/limits.test.ts): keeps a keyed seller's payTo quota apart from keyless traffic to the same payTo
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-preflight.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-preflight.test.ts): holds a non-zero pubnet settlement to the asset's minimum; zero and testnet amounts pass
- [`packages/facilitator/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/testing.ts): counts a finished settlement's charged fee, nothing when it expired or was refused unsent

**Residual risk.** Self-payments are not refused outright; the per-payTo limit and the caller budget bound them. Keyless callers share one per-payTo count, so keyless dust can still delay keyless payments to a seller. A patient attacker with many keys or payers spends up to the sum of their budgets, still bounded by the network budget; keys are issued on request.

### S9. One payer repeats settlements that are included and fail

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB3 (Facilitator and Stellar) · asset: Sponsor XLM

**Threat.** A payer deliberately makes settlements fail at apply time (spends the balance after simulation, a smart account whose check flips, an expiring authorization), again and again; each included failure costs the sponsor a fee.

**What Stellar makes different.** A failed transaction that is included still charges its fee to the fee-bump source; the race inside one ledger cannot be closed by a non-custodial facilitator.
