**Mitigations**

- Verify and settle both run an enforcing simulation, which executes \_\_check_auth; a refusal is named smart_account_rejected. ([`packages/stellar/src/diagnostics.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/stellar/src/diagnostics.ts), `classifyTransferFailure`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts): rejects a smart-account payment signed by the wrong key
- [`packages/stellar/test/diagnostics.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/stellar/test/diagnostics.test.ts): a smart-account payer rejecting in \_\_check_auth → smart_account_rejected
- [`packages/upto/test/facilitator.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/test/facilitator.test.ts): attributes refusals inside a smart account's \_\_check_auth to the account

**Conformance runs.** `live-matrix` case `c-payer-wrong-key`, `rejection-audit` case `upto-c-payer-wrong-key`.

**Residual risk.** None.

### A2. \_\_check_auth that passes simulation and fails at apply

Accepted risk · Denial of service · boundary TB7 (Contracts) · asset: Sponsor XLM

**Threat.** A smart account whose \_\_check_auth depends on state it can change (a nonce, a time, a policy budget) passes simulation and fails at inclusion, deliberately.

**What Stellar makes different.** \_\_check_auth is arbitrary contract code. Its result at apply time can differ from simulation, and the included, failed transaction's fee is the sponsor's.

**Preconditions.** A smart account the attacker controls. **Impact.** One wasted sponsor fee per payment.

**Mitigations**

- The simulation is repeated at settle, so state changed before settle is caught. ([`packages/facilitator/src/scheme.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/scheme.ts), `settle`)
- Wasted fees are metered; the hourly budget bounds the total. Since a smart account can accept the same authorization later, the settlement answers the failure only once the authorization expired unused, and a copy landed meanwhile as settled (F2). ([`packages/facilitator/src/guard.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/guard.ts), `SponsorBudget`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/fee-exposure.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/fee-exposure.integration.test.ts): names a smart account whose \_\_check_auth passes the settle-time simulation and fails at inclusion; the fee is counted as wasted
- [`packages/facilitator/test/fee-exposure.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/fee-exposure.integration.test.ts): stops settling once the hourly fee budget is committed, before any RPC work

**Residual risk.** Like S1, the race within one ledger cannot be closed. A per-payer limit on on-chain failures is not built; the hourly budget is the bound.

### A3. Policy events refuse exact payments from policy accounts

Accepted risk · Denial of service · boundary TB7 (Contracts) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** A smart account with a policy that emits its own events (such as a spending limit recording a spend) pays with exact.

**What Stellar makes different.** The upstream @x402/stellar exact verifier accepts only the token's transfer event in the simulation and refuses any other contract event as invalid_exact_stellar_payload_event_not_transfer. The upto verifier accepts events other than balance changes, so policy events do not refuse upto payments.

**Preconditions.** A payer whose \_\_check_auth publishes events. **Impact.** Such accounts cannot pay with exact through any facilitator built on the upstream verifier; nothing moves and the sponsor pays nothing.

**Mitigations**

- The refusal is passed through with its registered code and reason. ([`packages/stellar/src/codes.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/stellar/src/codes.ts), `invalid_exact_stellar_payload_event_not_transfer`)
- upto accepts non-balance events such as a policy's own. ([`packages/upto/src/facilitator/simulation.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/src/facilitator/simulation.ts), `checkSimulationEvents`)

**Tests.** None: this is a documented limit, not a behaviour Rail402 implements.

**Residual risk.** An upstream limit of the exact scheme; it fails closed. Policy accounts pay with upto.

### A4. A spending limit that does not cover upto

Mitigated · Elevation of privilege · boundary TB7 (Contracts) · asset: Agent budgets

**Threat.** An agent key constrained by a spending-limit policy pays with upto, whose settlement reaches the account as an approve rather than a transfer, and slips past a limit that only counts transfers.

**What Stellar makes different.** The upto contract spends through approve(payer, contract, cap, expiration) and transfer_from, so the account's \_\_check_auth sees an approve context for the full cap.

**Preconditions.** A smart account whose policy budgets transfer only. **Impact.** Spending beyond the account owner's budget.

**Mitigations**

- The approve-spending-limit policy counts approve and transfer against one rolling budget and reserves the full cap of each upto payment. ([`contracts/testing/approve-spending-limit/src/lib.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/approve-spending-limit/src/lib.rs), `SpendingLimitExceeded`)

**Tests**

- [`contracts/testing/approve-spending-limit/src/test.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/approve-spending-limit/src/test.rs): transfers_and_approvals_share_one_budget
- [`contracts/testing/composition/src/test.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/composition/src/test.rs): payments_within_the_budget_settle
- [`contracts/testing/composition/src/test.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/composition/src/test.rs): a_fourth_payment_after_the_budget_is_used_fails_in_the_policy
- [`contracts/testing/composition/src/test.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/composition/src/test.rs): a_cap_above_the_remaining_budget_fails_even_for_a_small_amount

**Conformance runs.** `live-matrix` case `upto-c-over-limit`.

**Residual risk.** OpenZeppelin's stock spending-limit policy refuses the approve context outright; accounts that use it cannot pay with upto.

### A5. Bypassing the policy through the Default rule

Mitigated · Elevation of privilege · boundary TB7 (Contracts) · asset: Agent budgets

**Threat.** An agent key picks a context rule without the spending policy (the Default rule, or the policy-free upto rule for the approve), or a relayer moves a signed payment to another rule.

**What Stellar makes different.** In OpenZeppelin smart accounts the signer chooses the rule per context, so a key that appears on a rule without the policy can spend without it.

**Preconditions.** An agent key listed on a rule without the policy. **Impact.** Spending beyond the budget.

**Mitigations**

- The documented rule configuration keeps the agent key off every rule without the policy, and the signature covers the rule ids. ([`contracts/testing/README.md`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/README.md), `Rule configuration for an agent key`)

**Tests**

- [`contracts/testing/composition/src/test.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/composition/src/test.rs): the_agent_key_cannot_use_the_default_rule
- [`contracts/testing/composition/src/test.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/composition/src/test.rs): the_agent_key_cannot_route_the_approval_around_the_policy
- [`contracts/testing/composition/src/test.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/composition/src/test.rs): the_rule_selection_is_bound_by_the_signature

**Residual risk.** Depends on the account owner configuring rules as documented.

### A6. Concurrent payments beyond the account's own budget

Accepted risk · Denial of service · boundary TB7 (Contracts) · asset: Sponsor XLM

**Threat.** Several payments from one policy account are verified at once; together they exceed the account's budget, and the later ones fail at apply.

**What Stellar makes different.** The policy's budget is on-chain state updated at apply time; simulations of concurrent payments each see the budget before the others.

**Preconditions.** A policy account paying several sellers at once. **Impact.** Wasted sponsor fees for the payments that fail.

**Mitigations**

- The policy refuses on chain; the failure is named and counted as wasted, within the hourly budget. ([`packages/facilitator/src/explain.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/explain.ts), `explainFailedTransaction`)

**Tests**

- [`contracts/testing/composition/src/test.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/composition/src/test.rs): a_fourth_payment_after_the_budget_is_used_fails_in_the_policy
- [`contracts/testing/approve-spending-limit/src/test.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/approve-spending-limit/src/test.rs): spends_leave_the_window_after_period_ledgers

**Residual risk.** As S1.

{/* generated:end */}

## MCP paid calls

`rail402-mcp`, the SDK and the CLI hold a signing key on the agent's side. The threats are about the agent's
money: paying more than allowed, paying the wrong party, leaking the key, paying twice, and following URLs it
should not.

{/* generated:begin topic-mcp-paid-calls */}

### M1. An agent spends beyond its caps

Mitigated · Elevation of privilege · boundary TB6 (Agent, MCP server and paid URLs) · asset: Agent budgets

**Threat.** A model, a prompt injection or a bug makes an agent pay more per call or per session than its owner allowed, including through many concurrent calls or several processes.

**What Stellar makes different.** A signed authorization is spendable by whoever holds it until it expires, so the cap must be enforced before signing, not after.

**Preconditions.** An agent with a signing key and a budget. **Impact.** Loss of the agent owner's funds up to the key's balance.

**Mitigations**

- A guarded signer checks the per-call cap and reserves the session budget immediately before signing, and signs only the payment it approved. ([`packages/sdk/src/policy/guard.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/policy/guard.ts), `createGuardedSigner`)
- The budget ledger reserves atomically, also across processes sharing one SQLite file. ([`packages/sdk/src/policy/ledger.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/policy/ledger.ts), `checkReservation`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/sdk/test/guard.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/guard.test.ts): never lets 20 concurrent payments exceed the session budget
- [`packages/sdk/test/sqlite-ledger.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/sqlite-ledger.test.ts): never lets four processes sharing one window exceed its budget
- [`packages/sdk/test/guard.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/guard.integration.test.ts): refuses to sign a payment above the per-call cap
- [`packages/mcp/test/pay.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/pay.integration.test.ts): refuses a price above the caller's cap before signing
- [`packages/sdk/test/guard.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/guard.test.ts): refuses a second signature within one call and keeps the first one's budget pending

**Conformance runs.** `rejection-audit` case `mcp-per-call-cap`, `rejection-audit` case `mcp-over-ceiling`.

**Residual risk.** The caps bound the loss; anything within them the agent may spend.

### M2. Payments on networks, assets or to recipients not allowed

Mitigated · Elevation of privilege · boundary TB6 (Agent, MCP server and paid URLs) · asset: Agent budgets

**Threat.** A seller's 402 asks for another network, another asset, or a recipient outside the allow list.

**What Stellar makes different.** Assets are contracts; the same symbol can be issued by anyone. The policy therefore compares contract ids and network passphrases, not symbols.

**Preconditions.** A seller offering unexpected terms. **Impact.** Payment in the wrong asset or to the wrong party.

**Mitigations**

- Spend decisions check the network in the signed preimage, the asset contract and the recipient against the policy. ([`packages/sdk/src/policy/decide.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/policy/decide.ts), `decideSpend`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/sdk/test/decide.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/decide.test.ts): refuses another network, whether in the preimage or in the requested passphrase
- [`packages/sdk/test/policy.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/policy.test.ts): compares muxed recipients by base account and refuses self-payment
- [`packages/sdk/test/policy.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/policy.test.ts): refuses an option priced in a different asset than the cap

**Conformance runs.** `rejection-audit` case `mcp-network-not-permitted`, `rejection-audit` case `mcp-asset-not-permitted`, `rejection-audit` case `mcp-recipient-not-permitted`.

**Residual risk.** None.

### M3. Secrets in tool schemas, results, errors or logs

Mitigated · Information disclosure · boundary TB6 (Agent, MCP server and paid URLs) · asset: Secrets

**Threat.** The signing seed, its raw bytes, a signature or a payment header leaks into an MCP tool schema, a tool result, a protocol error, a log line, the budget ledger or the CLI's output.

**What Stellar makes different.** A Stellar seed (S…) or its 32 raw bytes is the whole key; a leaked signed authorization is spendable until it expires.

**Preconditions.** Any error or log path that echoes inputs, or a seller that echoes the payment into its answer. **Impact.** Loss of the agent's funds.

**Mitigations**

- Errors and results are redacted and sanitised; loggers redact secret-like fields. ([`packages/sdk/src/redact.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/redact.ts), `redactDeep`)
- Tool schemas are published without secret-like properties or header mirroring. ([`packages/mcp/src/schemas.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/src/schemas.ts), `publishedSchema`)
- A seller that echoes its request cannot hand the payment back: a paid answer is returned and stored with the call's payment header, payload, signed entries and signatures replaced. ([`packages/sdk/src/flow/scrub.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/flow/scrub.ts), `scrubOutcome`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/mcp/test/secrets.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/secrets.integration.test.ts): leaks nothing into frames, logs or the ledger
- [`packages/sdk/test/scrub.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/scrub.test.ts): removes the payment from an HTTP answer and its stored replay, keeping the rest
- [`packages/sdk/test/scrub.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/scrub.test.ts): removes the payment from an MCP tool's content and structured content
- [`packages/sdk/test/secrets.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/secrets.integration.test.ts): leaks nothing, and the payment reached only the paid origin
- [`packages/cli/test/secrets.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/cli/test/secrets.integration.test.ts): leaks nothing on stdout, stderr or in the ledger
- [`packages/mcp/test/schemas.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/schemas.test.ts): publishes self-contained object schemas without header mirroring or secret-like fields
- [`packages/mcp/test/logger.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/logger.test.ts): writes JSON lines at or above its level, redacting secrets in the message and nested fields

**Residual risk.** The key lives in the agent's process; a compromised host is out of scope.

### M4. A retry pays twice

Mitigated · Tampering · boundary TB6 (Agent, MCP server and paid URLs) · asset: Agent budgets

**Threat.** An agent retries a paid call after a timeout or crash and pays again for the same work.

**What Stellar makes different.** Each new authorization carries a fresh nonce, so a retry signed anew is a second payment.

**Preconditions.** A retried tool call. **Impact.** Double payment.

**Mitigations**

- Calls carry an idempotency key; a repeated key replays the stored outcome without signing, and the payment identifier travels to the seller. The key belongs to the payer on its network, not to a budget window: the ledger checks it across every window and session of the payer inside its atomic reserve, so a retry after a renewal or a new session never signs again. The payment identifier is chosen at random the first time a key pays a resource and kept with the key, so it stays the same for the call and cannot be worked out from the payer and a guessable key. The SQLite ledger that keeps keys this way is schema version 3, which earlier versions (that check keys per window) refuse to open. ([`packages/sdk/src/flow/common.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/flow/common.ts), `replayStored`)
- A repeated key first reconciles its own payment on chain: it reports a consumed authorization as paid, and frees the key for a new payment only once the chain shows the authorization expired unused. ([`packages/sdk/src/flow/common.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/flow/common.ts), `replayStored`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/mcp/test/tools.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/tools.test.ts): replays a stored result for a repeated idempotency key
- [`packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts): replays a finished call for a repeated idempotency key without signing again
- [`packages/mcp/test/pay.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/pay.integration.test.ts): pays an HTTP resource by URL without custody, and replays a repeated key without paying
- [`packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts): frees an idempotency key on the retry itself, once the chain shows its authorization unused
- [`packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts): reports a keyed retry as paid once the chain shows its authorization consumed
- [`packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts): never pays twice for a keyed retry in a later budget window
- [`packages/sdk/test/mcp-flow.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/mcp-flow.test.ts): never pays twice for a keyed retry after a new budget session starts
- [`packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts): keeps a key's payment identifier across budget windows
- [`packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts): chooses a key's payment identifier at random, so the payer, network and key never give it away
- [`packages/sdk/test/sqlite-ledger.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/sqlite-ledger.test.ts): adds the payment identifier to a ledger written before it, as schema version 3
- [`packages/sdk/test/ledger.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/ledger.test.ts): keeps an idempotency key across budget windows and sessions while its payment may have moved money

**Residual risk.** A retry without the same key is a new payment by design. The in-memory ledger forgets keys when its process exits, and the browser storage ledger drops a key with its window once that window ended seven days ago and all its payments are resolved on chain.

### M5. Prompt-injected listings and bait-and-switch sellers

Accepted risk · Spoofing, Tampering · boundary TB6 (Agent, MCP server and paid URLs) · asset: Agent budgets

**Threat.** A listing's text steers the agent, or the seller's live 402 asks for a different recipient or a higher price than its listing.

**What Stellar makes different.** The listing's payTo is the account the facilitator saw settled; the live 402 is not bound to it unless the buyer checks.

**Preconditions.** A malicious or changed seller. **Impact.** Payment to an unexpected party or at an unexpected price.

**Mitigations**

- Paying through a listing binds its recipient and its listed price (HTTP resources and MCP tools at an http(s) endpoint) before anything is signed. ([`packages/sdk/src/policy/decide.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/policy/decide.ts), `checkOption`)
- Tool results bound and strip seller content. ([`packages/mcp/src/results.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/src/results.ts), `sellerJson`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts): binds a listing's recipient and its price
- [`packages/sdk/test/mcp-flow.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/mcp-flow.test.ts): binds a listing's price: a live price above the listed one is refused before signing
- [`packages/sdk/test/policy.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/policy.test.ts): binds the recipient of every listing and the price a listing carries
- [`packages/sdk/test/discovery.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/discovery.test.ts): binds the listed prices of HTTP listings and of MCP tools at an http(s) endpoint
- [`packages/sdk/test/discovery.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/discovery.test.ts): refuses listings that cannot be paid before anything is fetched from the seller
- [`packages/mcp/test/tools.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/tools.test.ts): strips control characters and drops oversized seller JSON

**Residual risk.** An agent still reads seller-controlled text; the caps (M1) bound what it can spend.

### M6. A tampered payment or a false settlement report

Mitigated · Tampering, Repudiation · boundary TB6 (Agent, MCP server and paid URLs) · asset: Agent budgets

**Threat.** A scheme client changes the payload after the guard approved it, or a seller reports a settlement that did not happen, reports another payment's transaction hash, or settles through its own contract so the transaction does not look like a direct settlement.

**What Stellar makes different.** The host records every authorization nonce it consumes in a temporary ledger entry keyed by the payer and the nonce, and only the entry the payer signed can consume it. A transaction used a payment's authorization exactly when it carries the payer's entry with that payment's nonce and its meta creates that nonce entry, whatever its call path: Soroban matches an entry below the top-level call, so a wrapper, router or batch contract can settle it too, and one transaction can consume several nonces. A transfer event alone says nothing about which authorization moved the money.

**Preconditions.** A malicious scheme client or seller. **Impact.** Paying for something else, believing a payment failed or succeeded wrongly, or a session budget that under-counts what moved.

**Mitigations**

- The guard compares the payload with what it signed. ([`packages/sdk/src/policy/guard.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/policy/guard.ts), `checkGuardedPayload`)
- A payment is proven only by its own nonce: a transaction that consumed it settles the reservation whatever its shape, for the exact price, or for upto the amount the signed settlement contract recorded for the signed terms (the whole cap when that cannot be read). A consumed authorization is never released. ([`packages/sdk/src/policy/proof.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/policy/proof.ts), `reservationSettlement`)
- A reported hash is only a claim against the payment it was reported for and counts as spent; it never removes a transaction from another payment's proof. Budget returns only when the authorization has expired and no transaction in its validity range consumed it. ([`packages/sdk/src/policy/reconcile.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/policy/reconcile.ts), `reconcile`)
- An expired payment the RPC can no longer prove either way counts as spent at its whole amount and is no longer reconciled. ([`packages/sdk/src/sqlite/ledger.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/sqlite/ledger.ts), `markUnprovable`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/sdk/test/guard.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/guard.test.ts): refuses a payload whose transaction differs from the signed authorization; the budget stays pending
- [`packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts): refuses a tampering scheme client after the probe, with nothing sent to the seller
- [`packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts): verifies a reported settlement on chain
- [`packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/http-flow.test.ts): counts a reused transaction hash against each payment that reports it
- [`packages/sdk/test/reconcile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/reconcile.test.ts): never lets a claim of another payment's hash release that payment (%s ledger)
- [`packages/sdk/test/reconcile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/reconcile.test.ts): settles every payment a batch transaction consumed (%s ledger)
- [`packages/sdk/test/reconcile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/reconcile.test.ts): settles each payer's payment from one batch transaction in a shared ledger file
- [`packages/sdk/test/reconcile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/reconcile.test.ts): counts an upto settlement made through another contract (%s ledger)
- [`packages/sdk/test/reconcile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/reconcile.test.ts): settles a payment only from a transfer made with its own authorization
- [`packages/sdk/test/reconcile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/reconcile.test.ts): never lets another payment's transfer release an upto cap early (%s ledger)
- [`packages/sdk/test/reconcile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/reconcile.test.ts): does not count an authorization carried in a transaction that never consumed it
- [`packages/sdk/test/reconcile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/reconcile.test.ts): counts expired rows the RPC can no longer prove as spent, and stops checking them (%s ledger)

**Residual risk.** A payment that is not reconciled before the RPC's retention ends counts as spent in full, even if it never moved money.

### M7. Another local process drives the MCP server over HTTP

Mitigated · Spoofing, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB6 (Agent, MCP server and paid URLs) · asset: Agent budgets

**Threat.** A web page (DNS rebinding) or another local process reaches the MCP server's HTTP transport and spends through its tools.

**What Stellar makes different.** The MCP server holds the agent's Stellar signing key. Any caller that reaches its tools can make it sign Soroban authorization entries, bounded only by its per-call and session caps (M1) and its network, asset and recipient allow lists (M2); keeping other callers out is what keeps that key the agent's.

**Preconditions.** The HTTP transport enabled. **Impact.** Spending by an unauthorised caller, within the caps.

**Mitigations**

- The HTTP transport listens on loopback, requires a bearer token and refuses foreign Host and Origin headers. ([`packages/mcp/src/http.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/src/http.ts), `hostHeaderValidationResponse`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/mcp/test/http.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/http.test.ts): refuses a Host outside the allowlist (DNS rebinding) with 403
- [`packages/mcp/test/http.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/http.test.ts): refuses a browser Origin outside the allowlist with 403
- [`packages/mcp/test/http.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/http.test.ts): listens on loopback and serves a client that presents the token

**Residual risk.** None; stdio is the default transport.

### M8. A pubnet budget that renews forever or resets, or caps set too high

Mitigated · Elevation of privilege · boundary TB6 (Agent, MCP server and paid URLs) · asset: Agent budgets

**Threat.** The default budget window renews when it expires, so a looping or prompt-injected agent spends its full budget every day with no end; or an owner configures caps far above what an agent should ever pay with real funds.

**What Stellar makes different.** Every pubnet payment moves real USDC from the agent's account under a Soroban authorization the agent signs on its own; nothing on chain limits how often a funded key signs.

**Preconditions.** An agent with a pubnet key runs unattended for longer than one budget window, or its caps were set carelessly. **Impact.** Real funds spent beyond what the owner meant, one window after another.

**Mitigations**

- On pubnet the default budget window does not renew; renewal is an explicit choice (autoRenew, RAIL402_SESSION_AUTO_RENEW). ([`packages/sdk/src/policy/budget.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/policy/budget.ts), `DEFAULT_SESSION`)
- Pubnet USDC caps above 1 USDC per call or 10 USDC per window, and caps on any other pubnet asset (which has no built-in ceiling), are refused unless larger caps are allowed explicitly (allowLargeCaps, RAIL402_ALLOW_LARGE_CAPS). ([`packages/sdk/src/policy/policy.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/policy/policy.ts), `PUBNET_CAP_CEILINGS`)
- A pubnet buyer with a key keeps its budget in the persistent SQLite store: RAIL402_BUDGET_STORE=memory is refused there, since every process start and every restart of rail402-mcp would open a fresh window with the full budget. ([`packages/sdk/src/config.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/config.ts), `resolveAgentConfig`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts): does not renew the default window on pubnet
- [`packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts): refuses a pubnet call above the per-call ceiling unless overridden
- [`packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts): refuses caps on any other pubnet asset unless larger caps are allowed
- [`packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts): keeps a keyed pubnet budget in the persistent store

**Residual risk.** Within one window, and within the caps, an agent may spend its whole budget. A program that builds a buyer in code chooses its own ledger; the SDK's default is in memory.

### M9. A pubnet payment is signed without a human, or an approval covers more than it showed

Mitigated · Elevation of privilege, Spoofing · boundary TB6 (Agent, MCP server and paid URLs) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** An agent, steered by listing text or a prompt, makes a real-money payment with nobody confirming the network, recipient and amount; or it reuses one approval to pay other recipients or listings, or shows the person a resource text that hides what is really paid.

**What Stellar makes different.** The buyer signs a Soroban authorization entry that lets the facilitator move the stated amount to payTo; once signed and settled, the transfer is final.

**Preconditions.** A pubnet key configured in the SDK, CLI or MCP server. **Impact.** A real payment the owner never saw.

**Mitigations**

- A pubnet buyer with a signer must choose how payments are confirmed: a confirm function (SDK), a form elicitation through the MCP client or RAIL402_MCP_CONFIRM=never for headless agents (MCP server). Under first-per-session an approval covers only the window, recipient base account, listing (or resource without one), asset, scheme and settlement authority it was shown for; any other one is asked about again. A declined or unavailable confirmation signs and reserves nothing. ([`packages/sdk/src/confirm.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/confirm.ts), `createConfirmationGate`)
- Every prompt says what approving covers: this payment only, or that recipient and listing again without asking, up to the per-call cap and the budget left, until the window ends. It shows the listing and the upto settlement authority, and untrusted fields stay on one line without control, line-break or bidirectional characters; an MCP endpoint whose raw text carries them is refused (egress_url_invalid), and the normalized URL is what is confirmed and reserved. ([`packages/mcp/src/confirmation.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/src/confirmation.ts), `confirmationMessage`)
- The sealed MCP intent binds the listing, the upto settlement authority and the budget window with the network, scheme, asset, recipient, resource and amount, so a retry that names another authority or listing, or whose payment falls in another window (it renewed, or the session was restarted, while the person decided), is asked about again with that window's end and budget. ([`packages/mcp/src/confirmation.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/src/confirmation.ts), `covers`)
- An MCP confirmation's request state carries a single-use id and a digest of the exact tool call it was asked for: a replayed approval is refused with rpc_confirmation_used, one reused for other arguments or another tool with rpc_confirmation_mismatch, and a forged or expired state fails its MAC check; so under RAIL402_MCP_CONFIRM=every one approval signs at most one payment. ([`packages/mcp/src/server.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/src/server.ts), `UsedConfirmations`)
- No helper defaults a pubnet buyer to never asking: openBuyer (CLI) and openConfiguredBuyer (MCP) pass no confirmation policy unless given one, so the SDK refuses a pubnet buyer with a key until the caller chooses. ([`packages/mcp/src/runtime.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/src/runtime.ts), `openConfiguredBuyer`)
- The CLI asks on the terminal before a pubnet payment (each process is its own buyer, so each pay or purchase asks), or takes --yes. This protects a person at the terminal from mistakes; it is not a control against an agent that can run the CLI, which can pass --yes or answer its own terminal. Agents get confirmation through rail402-mcp's elicitation, which the MCP client shows to the person, and are otherwise bounded by caps, tagged keys and the policy. ([`packages/cli/src/commands/common.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/cli/src/commands/common.ts), `paymentConfirmation`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts): needs a confirmation choice for a pubnet buyer that can sign
- [`packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts): signs nothing, reserves nothing and sends nothing
- [`packages/cli/test/pay-confirm.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/cli/test/pay-confirm.test.ts): asks for confirmation before the first pubnet payment unless --yes
- [`packages/cli/test/pay-confirm.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/cli/test/pay-confirm.test.ts): refuses without a terminal and without --yes
- [`packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts): asks before the first pubnet payment and refuses when the user declines
- [`packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts): refuses a pubnet payment when the client cannot show a confirmation
- [`packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts): accepts a confirmation once: replaying the approved retry signs nothing more
- [`packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts): refuses a confirmation reused for another tool call
- [`packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts): refuses a forged confirmation state
- [`packages/mcp/test/pay.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/pay.integration.test.ts): asks the user through the client before the real buyer signs, and signs nothing when declined
- [`packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts): opens a pubnet buyer with a key only with an explicit confirmation choice
- [`packages/cli/test/pay-confirm.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/cli/test/pay-confirm.test.ts): opens a pubnet buyer with a key only with an explicit confirmation choice
- [`packages/cli/test/pay-confirm.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/cli/test/pay-confirm.test.ts): asks on the terminal through the SDK's own confirmation gate before signing
- [`packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts): asks again for every new recipient or listing in a window, and says what an approval covers
- [`packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts): states the approval's scope: the recipient and listing it covers, the caps and the window end
- [`packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts): shows the upto settlement authority and binds it, with the listing, to the approval
- [`packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts): binds an approval to the budget window it was shown for
- [`packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts): asks again when the budget window changes before the approved call is retried
- [`packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts): shows untrusted fields on one line, without line breaks, controls or bidi overrides
- [`packages/cli/test/pay-confirm.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/cli/test/pay-confirm.test.ts): shows the upto authority and keeps untrusted fields on one line
- [`packages/sdk/test/mcp-flow.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/mcp-flow.test.ts): confirms and records the normalized endpoint URL, never the raw text
- [`packages/sdk/test/mcp-flow.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/mcp-flow.test.ts): refuses an endpoint the URL parser cannot read, with nothing paid

**Residual risk.** Headless agents that turn confirmation off rely on their caps (M1, M8). Under first-per-session, later payments to an approved recipient and listing in the same window are not asked about again; RAIL402_MCP_CONFIRM=every asks for each. The MCP client is trusted to show the form to a person and to report the answer honestly. The CLI confirmation guards a person, not an agent that runs the CLI.

### M10. A testnet key used on pubnet, or a key for the wrong network

Mitigated · Information disclosure, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB6 (Agent, MCP server and paid URLs) · asset: Secrets

**Threat.** A key handled as a throwaway testnet key (in examples, shell history, Playground exports) pays on pubnet, or a server meant for one network is given the other network's key.

**What Stellar makes different.** A Stellar seed is a valid key on every network, so nothing in the key itself says which network it belongs to.

**Preconditions.** A key file or a seed in the environment, and a pubnet configuration. **Impact.** Real funds paid with a key that was never protected as a real-money key.

**Mitigations**

- Key files carry a network tag (rail402 keys new/import write it); a file tagged for another network is refused, pubnet refuses untagged files unless explicitly allowed, and a seed in RAIL402_SECRET_KEY is never used on pubnet. ([`packages/sdk/src/keys.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/keys.ts), `checkKeyNetwork`)
- The Playground's key download is a key file tagged for testnet, and rail402 keys import reads a tagged file and refuses a tag for another network (key_network_mismatch), so a browser key never becomes a pubnet key unseen. ([`packages/cli/src/commands/keys.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/cli/src/commands/keys.ts), `imported`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts): refuses a testnet-tagged key on pubnet and a pubnet-tagged key on testnet
- [`packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts): refuses an untagged key file on pubnet unless explicitly allowed, and takes it on testnet
- [`packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts): refuses a seed in RAIL402_SECRET_KEY on pubnet and removes it from the environment
- [`packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts): refuses a testnet-tagged key on pubnet
- [`packages/cli/test/pay-confirm.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/cli/test/pay-confirm.test.ts): writes the network into new key files
- [`packages/cli/test/pay-confirm.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/cli/test/pay-confirm.test.ts): tags an imported key with the network it was imported for
- [`apps/playground/web/test/wallet/keystore.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/web/test/wallet/keystore.test.ts): writes a key file the CLI and SDK read, and never shows the key otherwise
- [`packages/cli/test/local.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/cli/test/local.test.ts): takes a key file the Playground exported, after the documented step

**Residual risk.** An owner can retag a key file by hand, or import a bare seed for any network.

### M11. An agent pointed at an impostor Bazaar or MCP endpoint

Accepted risk · Spoofing · boundary TB6 (Agent, MCP server and paid URLs) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** An agent is configured, or talked into using, a hostile catalog or discovery endpoint that lists attacker recipients at plausible prices.

**What Stellar makes different.** A listing names the payTo a payment authorization will credit; whoever controls the catalog chooses which Stellar accounts an agent is shown.

**Preconditions.** A buyer configured with an untrusted Bazaar URL, or an agent that follows listing IDs from an untrusted discovery endpoint. **Impact.** Payments to an attacker's account, within the caps.

**Mitigations**

- Pubnet needs an explicit https Bazaar URL; the hosted endpoint never pays, so a listing found anywhere is paid only through the buyer's own Bazaar, whose live 402 must match the listing's owner and price (M5). ([`packages/sdk/src/config.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/config.ts), `resolveAgentConfig`)
- A pubnet buyer built in code gets the same rule: createBuyer and createBrowserBuyer refuse a Bazaar or facilitator URL that is not https, carries credentials, or names an IP address, localhost, a single label or a private-network suffix (payment_policy_invalid). ([`packages/sdk/src/buyer-core.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/buyer-core.ts), `pubnetEndpointRefusal`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/sdk/test/config.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/config.test.ts): requires explicit endpoints and caps on pubnet and refuses the development switches there
- [`packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts): refuses a plain-http, IP-literal or private-network Bazaar or facilitator built in code

**Residual risk.** A user who configures a hostile catalog trusts it.

### M12. Links to the wrong network's explorer

Mitigated · Tampering, Spoofing · boundary TB6 (Agent, MCP server and paid URLs) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** A transaction or account link opens the other network's explorer, or an operator template points somewhere unexpected, so a user checks a payment against the wrong record.

**What Stellar makes different.** The same account address exists on testnet and pubnet; only the network in the link says which ledger a user is looking at.

**Preconditions.** A receipt, CLI output or Playground page showing an explorer link. **Impact.** A user believes a payment happened, or did not, on the wrong network.

**Mitigations**

- Links are built per network by the SDK's explorer provider (stellar.expert, the Rail402 Explorer or validated https templates with \{network\}/\{networkPath\}); account links come from the provider, never from rewriting a transaction template, and the Playground uses the same functions. ([`packages/sdk/src/explorer.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/explorer.ts), `explorerAccountUrl`)
- createBuyer and createBrowserBuyer validate a provider passed in code with the same rules as a configured one (https only, no credentials, the right placeholders). ([`packages/sdk/src/buyer-core.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/buyer-core.ts), `assembleBuyer`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/sdk/test/explorer.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/explorer.test.ts): links pubnet and testnet hashes to their own explorer paths
- [`packages/sdk/test/explorer.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/explorer.test.ts): links accounts and contracts on their own network
- [`apps/playground/web/test/projection/wallet.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/web/test/projection/wallet.test.ts): links accounts and contracts through the explorer's own account pages, and nothing else
- [`packages/sdk/test/explorer.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/explorer.test.ts): checks a provider passed to createBuyer the same way: https only, no credentials
- [`apps/playground/test/config.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/config.test.ts): keeps account links for a deployment that sets the earlier stellar.expert template

**Residual risk.** None.

### M13. A seller names its own settlement authority for a pubnet upto payment

Mitigated · Tampering, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB6 (Agent, MCP server and paid URLs) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** A seller puts a key it controls in an upto option's extra.facilitatorAddress, so it can settle the full cap whatever the call used, or switches the authority between the confirmation and the signature.

**What Stellar makes different.** An upto authorization lets the named settlement authority move up to the cap through the pinned upto contract; whoever holds that authority decides how much of the cap is settled.

**Preconditions.** A pubnet buyer with upto enabled (an explicit pinned upto contract). **Impact.** The full cap is charged for a call that used less, within the caps the user approved.

**Mitigations**

- On pubnet a buyer that can sign upto pins the authorities it accepts: the policy's uptoFacilitators (RAIL402_UPTO_FACILITATORS), or else the facilitatorAddress values its facilitator advertises for upto on pubnet in /supported, read once when the buyer is created; without either it refuses to open (payment_policy_invalid). Any other authority is refused before signing. ([`packages/sdk/src/buyer-core.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/buyer-core.ts), `pinUptoAuthorities`)
- The authority is shown in the MCP and CLI prompts and bound in the sealed MCP intent, so a different authority on the retry is asked about again. ([`packages/mcp/src/confirmation.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/src/confirmation.ts), `covers`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts): refuses to open a signing pubnet upto buyer with no pinned authority
- [`packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts): pins the authorities the facilitator advertises for upto on pubnet
- [`packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/pubnet-safety.test.ts): refuses when the facilitator advertises no pubnet upto authority, and keeps an explicit list
- [`packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/pubnet.test.ts): shows the upto settlement authority and binds it, with the listing, to the approval

**Residual risk.** The pinned facilitator is trusted to settle only what was used; a buyer created before the facilitator rotates its authority keeps the earlier set until it is created again. Testnet accepts any authority other than the payer and the recipient.

{/* generated:end */}

## The upto contract

With `upto` the payer signs a cap, and the settlement authority named in the terms chooses the amount at
settlement. The contract `x402-upto-stellar` has no admin, no storage and no upgrade path; the payer's
signature covers the terms and an `approve` of the cap to the contract, and the authority's authorization
covers the amount.

{/* generated:begin topic-upto-contract */}

### U1. Settling more than the cap

Mitigated · Tampering, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB7 (Contracts) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** The settlement authority, a bug or a hostile seller settles an amount above the cap the payer signed.

**What Stellar makes different.** The payer's signature covers approve(payer, contract, max_amount, expiration_ledger); the token would refuse a transfer_from above the allowance, and the contract refuses first.

**Preconditions.** Control of the settle arguments. **Impact.** None: refused.

**Mitigations**

- The contract refuses amount &gt; max_amount with error \#3 and moves nothing. ([`contracts/stellar/upto/src/lib.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/lib.rs), `AmountExceedsMax`)
- The facilitator refuses before simulating. ([`packages/upto/src/facilitator/rules.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/src/facilitator/rules.ts), `checkUptoPayment`)

**Tests**

- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/errors.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/errors.rs): rejects_an_amount_over_the_cap
- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/errors.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/errors.rs): a_rejected_settlement_moves_nothing
- [`packages/upto/test/facilitator.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/test/facilitator.test.ts): refuses an amount above the cap before simulating
- [`contracts/testing/composition/src/test.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/composition/src/test.rs): an_amount_over_the_cap_is_contract_error_3

**Conformance runs.** `live-matrix` case `upto-over-cap`, `rejection-audit` case `upto-settle-over-cap`.

**Residual risk.** None.

### U2. Changing the recipient, token, expiry, nonce or tree

Mitigated · Tampering · boundary TB7 (Contracts) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** The terms or the authorization tree are changed after signing: another recipient, token, facilitator, expiration, nonce or an extra sub-invocation.

**What Stellar makes different.** Every term is an argument of the signed settle(terms) root invocation and of its approve child; the host checks the signature over the whole tree, and the network id and nonce are in the preimage.

**Preconditions.** A copy of the payload. **Impact.** None: signature verification fails.

**Mitigations**

- The facilitator checks every bound field and the exact tree shape before simulating; the host rejects any change the facilitator missed. ([`packages/upto/src/authorization.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/src/authorization.ts), `inspectUptoAuthorization`)

**Tests**

- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/auth.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/auth.rs): mutated_terms_are_rejected
- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/signed.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/signed.rs): a_signature_over_other_terms_fails
- [`contracts/testing/upto-adversarial/src/test/classic.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/upto-adversarial/src/test/classic.rs): a_classic_signature_over_other_terms_fails
- [`contracts/testing/upto-adversarial/src/test/replay.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/upto-adversarial/src/test/replay.rs): a_signature_for_another_network_fails
- [`contracts/testing/upto-adversarial/src/test/replay.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/upto-adversarial/src/test/replay.rs): an_entry_for_one_deployment_fails_on_another
- [`packages/upto/test/upto.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/test/upto.integration.test.ts): refuses terms changed consistently in the entry and the requirements
- [`packages/upto/test/upto.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/test/upto.integration.test.ts): refuses a changed nonce
- [`packages/upto/test/upto.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/test/upto.integration.test.ts): refuses a signature expiration moved later than signed
- [`packages/upto/test/vectors.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/test/vectors.test.ts): cover every bound field and tree shape

**Conformance runs.** `rejection-audit` case `upto-wrong-recipient`, `rejection-audit` case `upto-wrong-asset`, `rejection-audit` case `upto-authorization-tree`, `rejection-audit` case `upto-forged-terms`.

**Residual risk.** None.

### U3. Settling one authorization twice

Mitigated · Tampering · boundary TB7 (Contracts) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** The same upto authorization is settled twice, for the same or different amounts.

**What Stellar makes different.** The payer's entry nonce is consumed by the host on first use and kept until the signature expires; a failed settlement rolls the nonce back.

**Preconditions.** A copy of the payload and the authority. **Impact.** None: the second use fails with Error(Auth, ExistingValue); Rail402 answers repeats from its ledger.

**Mitigations**

- Host nonce consumption; Rail402's settlement ledger and request fingerprint including the settle amount. ([`packages/facilitator/src/fingerprint.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/fingerprint.ts), `uptoFingerprintOf`)

**Tests**

- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/signed.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/signed.rs): a_second_use_of_the_same_entry_fails
- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/signed.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/signed.rs): a_failed_settlement_does_not_consume_the_nonce
- [`packages/upto/test/upto.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/test/upto.integration.test.ts): refuses a replay of a settled authorization
- [`apps/rail402/test/idempotency.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/idempotency.integration.test.ts): settles 5 concurrent requests for different amounts once: one success, four conflicts

**Conformance runs.** `live-matrix` case `upto-repeat-different-amount`, `rejection-audit` case `upto-replay`, `live-matrix` case `upto-concurrent-same-authorization`.

**Residual risk.** None.

### U4. Settling with an authority the payer did not name

Mitigated · Spoofing, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB7 (Contracts) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** A facilitator, or anyone, settles an upto payment that named another settlement authority.

**What Stellar makes different.** terms.facilitator is part of the signed terms and must authorize the settle call itself.

**Preconditions.** A copy of the payload. **Impact.** None: refused.

**Mitigations**

- Requirements whose facilitatorAddress is not this facilitator's authority are refused; the contract requires terms.facilitator's authorization. ([`packages/upto/src/errors.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/src/errors.ts), `invalid_upto_stellar_requirements_facilitator_mismatch`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/upto/test/upto.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/test/upto.integration.test.ts): refuses an authority other than the one the payer named
- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/auth.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/auth.rs): missing_facilitator_authorization_is_rejected
- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/auth.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/auth.rs): facilitator_authorization_for_another_amount_is_rejected

**Conformance runs.** `rejection-audit` case `upto-terms-authority`, `rejection-audit` case `upto-authority-not-ours`.

**Residual risk.** None.

### U5. The allowance left by a partial settlement

Mitigated · Elevation of privilege · boundary TB7 (Contracts) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** After a partial settlement, the remaining allowance (cap minus amount) is spent by someone else.

**What Stellar makes different.** The signed approve grants the settlement contract an allowance of the full cap until the expiration ledger; transfer_from spends only the settled amount.

**Preconditions.** A partial upto settlement. **Impact.** None: only the contract can spend the allowance, and it spends only inside a settlement.

**Mitigations**

- The allowance's spender is the settlement contract; it expires with the terms and the next settlement's approve overwrites it. ([`contracts/stellar/upto/src/lib.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/lib.rs), `transfer_from`)

**Tests**

- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/allowance.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/allowance.rs): nobody_can_spend_the_residual_directly
- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/allowance.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/allowance.rs): another_contract_cannot_spend_the_residual
- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/allowance.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/allowance.rs): the_next_settlement_overwrites_the_residual
- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/allowance.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/allowance.rs): the_residual_expires_with_the_terms
- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/settle.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/settle.rs): zero_leaves_an_existing_allowance_untouched

**Residual risk.** Wallets show an allowance to the settlement contract until the expiration ledger.

### U6. Zero settlements paid by the sponsor

Accepted risk · Denial of service · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Sponsor XLM

**Threat.** Anyone settles many upto payments for zero; each on-chain zero settlement costs the sponsor a fee and moves nothing. A zero needs no token balance or trustline, so the caller needs only an account that can sign.

**What Stellar makes different.** An on-chain zero consumes the nonce (so the authorization cannot be reused) but costs a Soroban transaction.

**Preconditions.** Sellers or buyers that trigger zero settlements. **Impact.** Sponsor fees for payments that earn nothing.

**Mitigations**

- UPTO_ZERO_SETTLEMENT=offchain records a zero as a terminal success without a transaction, in the durable settlement ledger (STORE=postgres is required for it). Pubnet defaults to offchain; testnet stays on-chain for a public on-chain record. ([`apps/rail402/src/config.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/config.ts), `zeroSettlement`)
- Pubnet refuses to start without an explicit hourly sponsor budget, which bounds on-chain zeros with every other settlement. ([`apps/rail402/src/config.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/config.ts), `PUBNET_MAX_SPONSOR_SPEND_XLM_PER_HOUR`)
- Zero settlements are never cataloged, on-chain or off-chain. Content planted with a settlement of one base unit is not published either: listings with an origin publish only what the origin answers (P1, P9). ([`packages/bazaar/src/codes.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/codes.ts), `bazaar_settlement_zero`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-scheme.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-scheme.test.ts): settles zero off-chain when configured: no transaction, the authorization consumed in the ledger
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-scheme.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-scheme.test.ts): drops the claim when an off-chain zero cannot settle, so it may be retried
- [`apps/rail402/test/upto.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/upto.integration.test.ts): never catalogs an off-chain zero settlement
- [`packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts): does not catalog a settlement that moved nothing, however high its cap
- [`apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts): requires explicit, safe mainnet settings

**Conformance runs.** `live-matrix` case `upto-zero`, `live-bazaar` case `upto-offchain-zero`.

**Residual risk.** On the keyless testnet service, zeros settle on-chain and each costs one fee, bounded by the per-client rate limit (IPv6 clients are grouped by /64) and, where configured, the hourly budget. A client with many addresses passes the rate limit, so the budget is the real bound, after which every settlement is refused for the rest of the hour. On pubnet zeros settle off-chain by default and all spend is bounded by the required hourly budget.

### U7. The settlement contract archived

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB7 (Contracts) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** The upto contract's instance or code entry reaches the end of its TTL and is archived, so every upto payment fails.

**What Stellar makes different.** Persistent entries are archived at liveUntilLedgerSeq; any invocation needs them restored. The contract keeps no storage of its own, so only the instance and code entries pay rent.

**Preconditions.** Nobody extends the entries for months. **Impact.** upto payments unavailable; restoring inside a payment would bill the buyer's sponsor (S7).

**Mitigations**

- The TTL monitor warns at 30 days and turns critical at 7; the keeper extends both entries before they run low. ([`apps/rail402/src/upto-keeper.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/upto-keeper.ts), `UptoTtlKeeper`)
- An operator command restores and extends where automatic restoration is off. ([`packages/facilitator/src/contract-ttl.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/contract-ttl.ts), `restoreContract`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/upto.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/upto.integration.test.ts): extends the contract from the keeper and the operator command, and reports its TTL
- [`apps/rail402/test/upto-archival.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/upto-archival.integration.test.ts): is restored by the TTL keeper where automatic restoration is allowed
- [`apps/rail402/test/upto-archival.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/upto-archival.integration.test.ts): refuses payments while the contract is archived, and settles once an operator restores it
- [`packages/facilitator/test/contract-ttl.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/contract-ttl.test.ts): never extends past the network's maximum TTL
- [`contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/settle.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/test/settle.rs): an_archived_instance_is_restored_by_the_invocation_that_uses_it

**Residual risk.** None while the keeper runs; extension and restoration are permissionless, so anyone can also keep the contract live.

### U8. A different or changed settlement contract

Mitigated · Tampering, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB7 (Contracts) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** Requirements name a hostile contract as the settlement contract, or the canonical contract's code is replaced.

**What Stellar makes different.** Contract addresses derive from the deployer and a salt; the deployed WASM hash is readable from the instance. A contract without an admin or upgrade function cannot change its code.

**Preconditions.** A seller or configuration pointing at another contract. **Impact.** A contract that spends the approved allowance differently.

**Mitigations**

- The contract has no admin, no storage and no upgrade path. ([`contracts/stellar/upto/src/lib.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/stellar/upto/src/lib.rs), `cannot be upgraded`)
- Payments naming another contract than the network's configured one are refused; the service refuses to start when the configured contract runs other code. ([`packages/upto/src/errors.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/src/errors.ts), `invalid_upto_stellar_payload_wrong_contract`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/upto/test/deployment.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/test/deployment.test.ts): runs the committed wasm
- [`packages/upto/test/deployment.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/test/deployment.test.ts): sits at the address derived from the deployer and the canonical salt
- [`apps/rail402/test/upto.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/upto.integration.test.ts): refuses to start with a contract running other code, and starts without its authority
- [`packages/upto/test/codec.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/test/codec.test.ts): resolves the settlement contract from overrides and canonical deployments

**Residual risk.** An operator can configure a contract of their own (UPTO_TESTNET_DEPLOYMENT); buyers see it in /supported.

### U9. Token differences between SAC and SEP-41 contracts

Mitigated · Tampering · boundary TB7 (Contracts) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** A token whose events or allowance semantics differ from a Stellar Asset Contract is mis-read, so a wrong transfer passes the checks or a right one is refused.

**What Stellar makes different.** Stellar Asset Contracts publish \[name, from, to, asset\] topics; OpenZeppelin SEP-41 tokens publish \[name, from, to\] with a bare amount, and their approve data is a map with live_until_ledger. Decimals are read from the token.

**Preconditions.** A custom SEP-41 asset. **Impact.** False acceptance or refusal of upto settlements.

**Mitigations**

- Both event shapes are accepted and checked field by field; exactly one transfer of the amount from the payer to payTo, and no balance event involving a facilitator account. ([`packages/upto/src/facilitator/simulation.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/src/facilitator/simulation.ts), `checkSimulationEvents`)
- Only the tokens the operator lists for the network are settled; any other token is refused before simulation, because a token contract can report a transfer it never made. ([`packages/upto/src/facilitator/rules.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/src/facilitator/rules.ts), `isAssetAccepted`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/upto/test/facilitator.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/test/facilitator.test.ts): accepts SEP-41 token events (three topics, map data)
- [`packages/upto/test/facilitator.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/test/facilitator.test.ts): requires exactly one transfer of the cap from the payer to payTo
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-preflight.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-preflight.test.ts): an asset outside the operator's list
- [`contracts/testing/upto-adversarial/src/test/hostile_token.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/upto-adversarial/src/test/hostile_token.rs): a_lying_token_settles_without_moving_value
- [`contracts/testing/upto-adversarial/src/test/hostile_token.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/upto-adversarial/src/test/hostile_token.rs): a_token_cannot_reenter_settle_even_with_a_second_valid_authorization
- [`contracts/testing/upto-adversarial/src/test/hostile_token.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/upto-adversarial/src/test/hostile_token.rs): a_token_cannot_use_the_payers_approve_authorization_on_another_asset
- [`contracts/testing/test-token/src/test.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/test-token/src/test.rs): approve_event_has_three_topics_and_map_data
- [`tools/conformance/test/assets.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/assets.integration.test.ts): refuses a payer holding less than the amount, or none of the token, as insufficient_funds

**Conformance runs.** `live-bazaar` case `second-asset`.

**Residual risk.** Tokens with fees on transfer or rebasing balances are not supported; their settlements fail the event check.

### U10. The settlement authority's key is compromised

Accepted risk · Elevation of privilege · boundary TB4 (Facilitator and its state) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** The authority's key, which is derived from the sponsor secret, leaks.

**What Stellar makes different.** The authority chooses the amount of any upto payment that names it, up to each payment's cap, within the payment's validity window; it holds no funds and cannot change recipient or token.

**Preconditions.** Leak of the sponsor secret. **Impact.** Payloads the attacker obtains could be settled for up to their caps, to their named recipients, until they expire; the sponsor's XLM is at risk.

**Mitigations**

- The authority is derived per network with HKDF from the sponsor secret, distinct from the sponsor and the channels, and holds a zero balance. ([`packages/facilitator/src/authority.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/authority.ts), `deriveAuthorityKeypair`)
- Rotation with the sponsor: payments naming the old authority are refused by the new one and expire within the maximum timeout (300 s by default). ([`docs/operations.md`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/docs/operations.md), `Rotating the sponsor key`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/authority.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/authority.test.ts): is stable per sponsor and network, and distinct from the sponsor and every channel
- [`packages/facilitator/test/authority.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/authority.test.ts): reports whether the account exists and what it holds
- [`packages/upto/test/upto.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/test/upto.integration.test.ts): refuses an authority other than the one the payer named

**Residual risk.** Within the maximum timeout after a leak, captured payloads can be charged up to their caps. Keep caps near expected prices and timeouts short.

### U11. A served buyer defaults before settlement

Accepted risk · Repudiation · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Seller revenue

**Threat.** An upto seller serves first and settles afterwards. Between /verify and the seller's /settle the buyer moves its funds, uses its authorization nonce in another transaction, or settles the payload itself for zero, and the seller's settlement fails for work already done.

**What Stellar makes different.** /verify simulates the settlement for the full cap, so the signature, balance and trustline hold at that moment, but a Soroban authorization is not an escrow: nothing is locked until the settlement transaction applies.

**Preconditions.** A buyer willing to default, and a seller that releases output before its own settlement succeeds. **Impact.** One request served unpaid. A streaming or metered seller cannot withhold what it already sent.

**Mitigations**

- Short maxTimeoutSeconds, settling immediately after serving, withholding buffered output until settlement succeeds (the stock Express middleware does), and refusing payers whose settlements fail. ([`docs/facilitator/upto.mdx`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/docs/facilitator/upto.mdx), `Payment is not guaranteed after verification`)

**Tests.** None: this is a documented limit, not a behaviour Rail402 implements.

**Residual risk.** Accepted: upto is post-paid by design; the loss is bounded by one request's cap.

### U12. Rotation strands or reopens upto authorizations

Mitigated · Denial of service, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB7 (Contracts) · asset: Seller revenue

**Threat.** After a sponsor rotation the new authority refuses payloads that name the old one, so sellers cannot settle work already served; or the old authority is kept valid longer than needed and a leaked old seed keeps settling captured payloads.

**What Stellar makes different.** The payer names the authority in the signed terms; the contract keeps no storage and has no admin, so the grace lives in the facilitator.

**Preconditions.** A sponsor rotation with upto payments in flight. **Impact.** Unsettled work, or captured payloads settled by a leaked old seed.

**Mitigations**

- The previous authority stays loaded for settlement only until PREVIOUS_AUTHORITY_UNTIL, at most TIMEOUT_MAX_SECONDS after startup; /supported advertises only the new one. ([`packages/facilitator/src/upto-scheme.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/upto-scheme.ts), `authorityFor`)
- The configuration refuses a longer grace and ignores one already over. ([`apps/rail402/src/config.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/config.ts), `PREVIOUS_AUTHORITY_UNTIL`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-rotation.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-rotation.test.ts): settles a payload naming the previous authority within the grace and refuses it after
- [`apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts): keeps the previous upto authority for at most the longest timeout after a rotation

**Residual risk.** During the grace (at most TIMEOUT_MAX_SECONDS, 300 s by default) a leaked old seed can still settle captured payloads up to their caps.

### U13. The mainnet contract is deployed wrong

Mitigated · Tampering, Spoofing · boundary TB15 (Source, release and deploy) · asset: Seller revenue

**Threat.** The mainnet deployment uses bytes other than the attested release, the wrong network, or a record that does not match the chain, so clients pin a contract nobody verified.

**What Stellar makes different.** The contract has no admin and cannot be upgraded, so a wrong deployment cannot be fixed in place; it must be replaced and every pin updated.

**Preconditions.** An operator deploying by hand, or a tampered build. **Impact.** Payments run code nobody reviewed.

**Mitigations**

- deploy.sh --network pubnet deploys only the release wasm that equals the committed fixture and carries stellar.expert's attestation, simulates the upload first and needs the typed contract address before sending. ([`scripts/contracts/deploy.sh`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/scripts/contracts/deploy.sh), `refusing to deploy other bytes to pubnet`)
