- verify.sh pubnet and the release workflow check the rebuilt, recorded and deployed bytes of every deployment record. ([`scripts/contracts/verify.sh`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/scripts/contracts/verify.sh), `pubnet.json`)
- Pubnet refuses to serve upto (holds the network) when the configured contract is absent or runs other code. ([`apps/rail402/src/runtime.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/runtime.ts), `is not on the network`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/contract-deploy.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/contract-deploy.test.ts): refuses pubnet bytes that differ from the attested release
- [`apps/rail402/test/network-isolation.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/network-isolation.integration.test.ts): holds pubnet when its upto contract is absent, without touching testnet
- [`packages/facilitator/test/pubnet-setup.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/pubnet-setup.test.ts): accepts the configured WASM and refuses an absent contract or other code

**Residual risk.** None once verified; a replaced contract needs a new pin release.

{/* generated:end */}

## `exact` payments

The `exact` scheme is verified by the upstream `@x402/stellar` verifier, which Rail402 wraps with its own
checks before any RPC work and with its settlement engine after.

{/* generated:begin topic-exact-payments */}

### E1. Tampering with amount, recipient or asset

Mitigated · Tampering · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** A seller or intermediary changes the amount, payTo or asset of an exact payment after the payer signed it.

**What Stellar makes different.** The payer signs transfer(from, to, amount) on the asset contract; any change alters the invocation the host checks the signature against.

**Preconditions.** A copy of the payload. **Impact.** None: refused at verify, or fails signature verification.

**Mitigations**

- The transfer must match the requirements; the payer's entry must authorize exactly the operation's invocation. ([`packages/facilitator/src/preflight.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/preflight.ts), `invalid_exact_stellar_payload_auth_invocation_mismatch`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts): rejects tampering: a changed amount breaks the signed authorization
- [`packages/facilitator/test/preflight.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/preflight.test.ts): rejects a transfer that does not match the requirements
- [`packages/facilitator/test/preflight.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/preflight.test.ts): rejects an authorization signed for a different call than the operation

**Conformance runs.** `live-matrix` case `tamper-amount`, `live-matrix` case `tamper-recipient`, `rejection-audit` case `tampered-amount`.

**Residual risk.** None.

### E2. The facilitator made a party to the payment

Mitigated · Elevation of privilege · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Sponsor XLM

**Threat.** A payload names a facilitator account (sponsor, channel or upto authority) as payer, transaction or operation source, or co-signer, so the facilitator's signature would move its own funds.

**What Stellar makes different.** The facilitator signs the envelope and the fee bump; if one of its accounts were the source of a transfer or the subject of an auth entry, its signature would authorize it.

**Preconditions.** Knowledge of the facilitator's public addresses (they are in /supported). **Impact.** Loss of the facilitator's funds; custody.

**Mitigations**

- Any facilitator account as payer, source or authorization party is refused. ([`packages/facilitator/src/preflight.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/preflight.ts), `invalid_exact_stellar_payload_facilitator_in_auth`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/preflight.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/preflight.test.ts): protects facilitator accounts
- [`packages/facilitator/test/preflight.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/preflight.test.ts): rejects a facilitator account as payer or co-signer
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-scheme.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-scheme.test.ts): treats the authority as a facilitator account for exact payments too
- [`test/agents.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/test/agents.integration.test.ts): paid the seller exactly what was settled, from the payer, never from the sponsor

**Conformance runs.** `rejection-audit` case `facilitator-as-cosigner`, `rejection-audit` case `facilitator-as-payer`, `rejection-audit` case `facilitator-as-source`.

**Residual risk.** None.

### E3. Extra invocations riding on the payment

Mitigated · Tampering, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** The authorization tree carries sub-invocations, or the transaction carries more operations or preconditions, so that settling the payment does more than one transfer.

**What Stellar makes different.** An authorization entry authorizes a tree of calls; a transaction may carry preconditions and several operations.

**Preconditions.** A crafted payload. **Impact.** Side effects signed by the payer and paid for by the sponsor.

**Mitigations**

- Exactly one invokeHostFunction transfer, no sub-invocations, no unsupported preconditions, bounded size. ([`packages/stellar/src/payload.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/stellar/src/payload.ts), `inspectExactTransaction`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/stellar/test/payload.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/stellar/test/payload.test.ts): reports sub-invocations and preconditions
- [`packages/stellar/test/payload.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/stellar/test/payload.test.ts): rejects more than one operation
- [`packages/stellar/test/payload.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/stellar/test/payload.test.ts): rejects a fee-bump envelope
- [`packages/stellar/test/payload.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/stellar/test/payload.test.ts): rejects an oversized payload before decoding it

**Conformance runs.** `rejection-audit` case `sub-invocations`, `rejection-audit` case `multiple-operations`, `rejection-audit` case `fee-bump-envelope`.

**Residual risk.** None.

### E4. An authorization valid for too long

Mitigated · Tampering · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** A payload signed with a far-future expiration stays spendable long after the purchase.

**What Stellar makes different.** The expiration ledger is chosen by the signer; x402 derives it from maxTimeoutSeconds as ceil(T / ledger seconds) past the latest ledger.

**Preconditions.** A client that signs long-lived authorizations. **Impact.** A payment that can be settled much later.

**Mitigations**

- Expirations beyond maxLedger plus 2 are refused; maxTimeoutSeconds outside 10 to 300 is refused. ([`packages/stellar/src/expiration.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/stellar/src/expiration.ts), `maxAcceptableExpiration`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/expiration.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/expiration.integration.test.ts): holds the upper edge at maxLedger plus 2, from upstream's ledger-time estimate
- [`packages/facilitator/test/expiration.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/expiration.integration.test.ts): derives the expiration ledger from maxTimeoutSeconds like the stock client
- [`packages/facilitator/test/expiration.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/expiration.integration.test.ts): settles before the expiration ledger and refuses the same window after it

**Conformance runs.** `rejection-audit` case `expires-too-far`, `rejection-audit` case `timeout-out-of-range`, `live-matrix` case `expiry-window`.

**Residual risk.** None.

### E5. Misreading a custom token's failures and decimals

Mitigated · Tampering · boundary TB3 (Facilitator and Stellar) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** A token whose error numbers or decimals differ from a Stellar Asset Contract is priced or explained as if it were one.

**What Stellar makes different.** Classic assets are always 7 decimals through their SAC; SEP-41 contract tokens choose their decimals and their own error numbers.

**Preconditions.** A custom SEP-41 asset configured. **Impact.** Wrong prices by orders of magnitude, or wrong refusal reasons.

**Mitigations**

- The service reads decimals() at startup and refuses to start on a mismatch. ([`apps/rail402/src/asset-check.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/asset-check.ts), `checkAssets`)
- SAC error numbers are applied only to SACs; other tokens are named by number. ([`packages/stellar/src/diagnostics.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/stellar/src/diagnostics.ts), `classifyTransferFailure`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/asset-check.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/asset-check.test.ts): fails for good, with a coded ConfigError naming the variable, when the decimals differ
- [`packages/stellar/test/diagnostics.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/stellar/test/diagnostics.test.ts): names a custom token's own error by number, never by the Stellar Asset Contract's meaning
- [`tools/conformance/test/assets.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/assets.integration.test.ts): refuses to start when a configured asset's decimals differ from the chain's

**Residual risk.** None.

{/* generated:end */}

## Operations

Crashes, outages, sequence numbers, retention windows and keys: what happens to a payment when the service
itself fails.

{/* generated:begin topic-operations */}

### O1. A crash after submission

Mitigated · Denial of service, Repudiation · boundary TB4 (Facilitator and its state) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** The process dies after signing or broadcasting a settlement and before recording its outcome; a restart submits a replacement and the payer is charged twice, or the seller is never told.

**What Stellar makes different.** A signed envelope stays valid until its time bound; the network may include it after the process died.

**Preconditions.** A crash, deploy or kill at the wrong moment. **Impact.** Double charge (without protection) or a lost success.

**Mitigations**

- The signed envelope is recorded before broadcast; recovery re-sends the same bytes and never builds a replacement. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `recordingSponsor`)
- A recorded settlement is expired only when the RPC's retained history proves it absent. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `historyCovers`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/crash-recovery.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/crash-recovery.integration.test.ts): K0: finds the transaction the network accepted before the process died
- [`apps/rail402/test/crash-recovery.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/crash-recovery.integration.test.ts): K1: rebroadcasts the identical recorded bytes when the broadcast never left
- [`apps/rail402/test/crash-recovery.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/crash-recovery.integration.test.ts): K2: finds a transaction that was included before the process saw it confirmed
- [`apps/rail402/test/crash-recovery.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/crash-recovery.integration.test.ts): K3: expires a transaction whose time bound passed while the service was down, and nothing moves
- [`packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts): broadcasts nothing when the envelope cannot be recorded first, leaving the payment retryable

**Conformance runs.** `fault-recovery` case `k0-exact`, `fault-recovery` case `k1-exact`, `fault-recovery` case `k3-exact`, `fault-recovery` case `k0-upto`, `fault-recovery` case `k1-upto`.

**Residual risk.** A settlement whose ledgers the RPC no longer retains stays unresolvable and is reported (O4).

### O2. Channel sequence collisions

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB4 (Facilitator and its state) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** Two settlements use the same channel account at once, or something else uses a channel's key, and transactions fail with a bad sequence number.

**What Stellar makes different.** An account's transactions are ordered by sequence number; one in-flight transaction per source is the rule.

**Preconditions.** Concurrency, several replicas, or external use of a channel key. **Impact.** Failed settlements and wasted fees.

**Mitigations**

- Channels are leased exclusively through Postgres across replicas. ([`packages/store-postgres/src/channel-pool.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/src/channel-pool.ts), `PostgresChannelPool`)
- Sequence conflicts are counted so external use is visible. ([`apps/rail402/src/metrics.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/metrics.ts), `rail402_sequence_conflicts_total`)
- Every call of the service's own RPC clients fails after 10 seconds; upstream's exact verifier, whose client has no timeout, is cut off after the same 10 seconds with a retryable unexpected_verify_error; and a settlement that has signed nothing by the end of its 30-second claim window signs nothing afterwards, which also bounds upstream's settlement client, so a hung provider can never let a lease expire under a settlement that may still sign. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `SIGN_WINDOW_ELAPSED`)
- RPC clients carry a call timeout below the claim and lease windows. ([`packages/facilitator/src/rpc-pool.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/rpc-pool.ts), `DEFAULT_RPC_TIMEOUT_MS`)
- A channel referenced by an open settlement is never leased again, whether its lease was released or expired, unless the settlement gave it back because its bytes can never apply. ([`packages/store-postgres/src/channel-pool.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/src/channel-pool.ts), `AND NOT EXISTS`)
- A settlement refused with txBadSeq gives its channel back at once, once the channel's sequence number on the network has reached the transaction's and neither its hash nor its inner hash landed (read from an RPC that has ingested that ledger and retains every ledger since the claim): its bytes can never apply, so the channel is quarantined for SETTLE_CHANNEL_QUARANTINE_MS, doubled for each further conflict in a row, instead of being held until the payment expires or reused at once. The settlement is marked as having given the channel back, so the channel pool may lease it again while the settlement's outcome waits, and finishing the settlement releases nothing. An upto settlement then finishes with nothing charged; an exact one when the payer's authorization expired unused, or as settled through a copy of it (F2). ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `sequenceTaken`)
- A quarantined channel is not leased by any replica until its cooldown passes, and when it is, a channel whose account sequence number moved since the conflict is quarantined again instead of settling. ([`packages/store-postgres/src/channel-pool.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/src/channel-pool.ts), `quarantined_until`)
- A conflict decided at once reads the payer's nonce, and when a send may have been accepted or the payment is exact the inner hash too, from an RPC at or past the ledger of the sequence proof; a lagging or switched provider leaves the settlement waiting (rpc_behind), so an authorization another transaction carried is never reported as unused. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `is behind the ledger the sequence proof was read at`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/concurrency.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/concurrency.integration.test.ts): with injected duplicates, without a collision
- [`apps/rail402/test/replicas.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/replicas.integration.test.ts): with every duplicate sent to the other replica
- [`apps/rail402/test/observability.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/observability.integration.test.ts): counts a sequence conflict when a channel's sequence is used behind its back
- [`packages/facilitator/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/testing.ts): never leases one channel twice, even under concurrency
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): stops there, signs nothing later and frees its claim and channel
- [`packages/facilitator/test/rpc-pool.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/rpc-pool.test.ts): fails a call to a provider that never answers
- [`packages/facilitator/test/verify-timeout.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/verify-timeout.integration.test.ts): answers unexpected_verify_error within the RPC timeout, and signs nothing
- [`packages/store-postgres/test/stores.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/test/stores.integration.test.ts): never lease a channel whose recorded settlement is still open, released or expired
- [`packages/store-postgres/test/stores.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/test/stores.integration.test.ts): lease a channel again once its open settlement gave it back
- [`packages/facilitator/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/testing.ts): lets a submitted settlement give its channel back once, and keeps that when it finishes
- [`packages/facilitator/test/sequence-conflict.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/sequence-conflict.integration.test.ts): gives its channel back within seconds, moves nothing, never settles twice and fails once the authorization expired
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): quarantines the channel at once instead of holding it, and finishes with nothing charged once the authorization expired
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): never finishes as failed when the transaction landed inside another fee bump
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): finish an upto settlement at once and quarantine the channel, since only the authority could settle it
- [`packages/facilitator/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/testing.ts): doubles the cooldown of a channel quarantined again, until a plain release
- [`packages/facilitator/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/testing.ts): tracks the sends of recorded bytes: public once one is accepted or its answer is lost
- [`packages/facilitator/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/testing.ts): hands a quarantined channel's sequence number to its next lease, once
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): is quarantined again, for twice as long, when its sequence number moved since
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): quarantines the channel at the first txBadSeq when no send was ever accepted
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): sends nothing more once it gave its channel back, while the same request still waits
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): keeps waiting when a send's answer was lost: it may have been accepted
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): is finished by another replica's reconciler from the recorded answers
- [`packages/facilitator/test/sequence-conflict.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/sequence-conflict.integration.test.ts): gives its channel back the same way when the channel's queue slot was taken first (TRY_AGAIN_LATER)
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): keeps waiting when the payer's nonce is read from an RPC behind the sequence proof
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): keep an upto settlement open while the payer's nonce is read from an RPC behind the sequence proof

**Conformance runs.** `concurrency` case `distinct-sequences`.

**Residual risk.** Reusing the sponsor seed in another deployment derives the same channels; do not. After another transaction took its sequence number, an exact settlement's outcome still waits until the payer's authorization expires, since another transaction may carry that authorization; its channel is given back at the proof. Only with a settlement ledger that cannot mark a channel given back (no releaseChannel) does the settlement keep its channel until it finishes.

### O3. The RPC or the settlement store is unavailable

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB3 (Facilitator and Stellar) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** The Stellar RPC or Postgres stops answering while payments arrive.

**What Stellar makes different.** Without the RPC nothing can be simulated, submitted or confirmed.

**Preconditions.** An outage. **Impact.** Payments refused, or worse, settled without a record.

**Mitigations**

- /ready fails while the RPC is unreachable; refusals carry retryable codes. ([`apps/rail402/src/health.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/health.ts), `HealthMonitor`)
- Nothing is broadcast unless its envelope was recorded; verify stays available when the ledger cannot be read. ([`packages/facilitator/src/scheme.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/scheme.ts), `alreadySubmitted`)
- A simulation the RPC failed without running the host, or that passes when asked again, is answered with a retryable code that says the payment was not judged, never as an invalid payment; nothing is signed or submitted. ([`packages/facilitator/src/explain.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/explain.ts), `simulationRanOnHost`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/runtime.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/runtime.integration.test.ts): fails while the RPC is unreachable
- [`apps/rail402/test/observability.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/observability.integration.test.ts): counts transport and request errors while the RPC fails, and reports it in /health
- [`apps/rail402/test/idempotency.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/idempotency.integration.test.ts): keeps verify available when the ledger cannot be read
- [`packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts): broadcasts nothing when the envelope cannot be recorded first, leaving the payment retryable
- [`packages/store-postgres/test/stores.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/test/stores.integration.test.ts): survives the server closing its idle connections, as in a restart or failover
- [`packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts): answers a retryable code when the RPC fails a simulation without running it, and settles on retry
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto.integration.test.ts): answers a retryable code when the RPC fails a simulation without running it, and settles on retry

**Residual risk.** None beyond downtime.

### O4. Settlements past the RPC's retention

Mitigated · Repudiation · boundary TB3 (Facilitator and Stellar) · asset: Seller revenue

**Threat.** A recorded settlement is examined after the RPC no longer keeps the ledgers that could prove it included or absent, or by an RPC that has not ingested them yet.

**What Stellar makes different.** An RPC keeps only a limited window of recent ledgers and transactions; older ones cannot be read from it. Providers, and the backends behind one provider's balancer, reach each ledger at different times, so consecutive reads can come from different heights.

**Preconditions.** A long outage or a restored backup; or a lagging provider, a backend behind a balancer or a provider switch while a settlement passes its bounds. **Impact.** A payment wrongly declared expired while it did settle.

**Mitigations**

- Such settlements stay unresolved and are reported, never expired; every RPC that answered not found, for the transaction's hash or its inner hash, must retain every ledger since the claim. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `historyCovers`)
- A settlement whose own hash reads not found is finished as expired, or refused, only on an inner-hash lookup and a payer's nonce read from an RPC at or past the ledger of that lookup, which for an expiry is the ledger at which its bounds were seen to pass; a lagging provider, a backend behind a balancer or another provider after a failover leaves it waiting for a later read, so a transaction that landed inside someone else's fee bump is never reported as expired. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `behind the ledger its proof holds at`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/crash-recovery.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/crash-recovery.integration.test.ts): keeps a settlement the RPC can no longer prove absent unresolved, and reports it
- [`packages/facilitator/test/recovery.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/recovery.test.ts): never declares one expired once the RPC no longer retains the ledgers that could hold it
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): keep waiting while the inner-hash lookup and the nonce read lag, then succeed through the other fee bump
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): keep waiting while the reads lag behind the ledger that passed the time bound, and expire once they catch up
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): keep an exact record open past its authorization's expiration while the inner-hash lookup lags
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-engine.test.ts): stay unresolved when the inner-hash lookup comes from an RPC that no longer retains the claim's ledgers

**Residual risk.** An operator resolves unresolved settlements from an archive or an indexer. A settlement whose reads stay behind keeps its channel until they catch up.

### O5. All channels busy

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB4 (Facilitator and its state) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** More settlements arrive than there are channel accounts.

**What Stellar makes different.** Each channel carries one in-flight transaction.

**Preconditions.** A burst of payments. **Impact.** Slow or refused settlements.

**Mitigations**

- A request waits at most SETTLE_CHANNEL_WAIT_MS for a channel and is refused with a retryable code, leaving no record. ([`packages/facilitator/src/channels.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/channels.ts), `MemoryChannelPool`)
- An exact payment whose operation sets its own source account is refused before anything is claimed: the payer's authorization does not cover the operation source, and settlement keeps it but cannot sign for it, so such a payment could never land and would hold a channel until its authorization expired. ([`packages/facilitator/src/preflight.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/preflight.ts), `invalid_exact_stellar_payload_operation_source_set`)
- A first submission the network refuses for good finishes as a submission failure, with nothing charged, and frees its channel and fee budget: an upto one at once, an exact one once the payer's authorization expired unused (anyone holding the payment can carry it until then, F2); a refusal that may pass (fee, balance, sequence, time bound) keeps the settlement open. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `firstSubmissionRefused`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts): refuses when no channel frees up in time, leaving the payment retryable
- [`packages/facilitator/test/upto-scheme.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/upto-scheme.test.ts): answers busy channels with the upto code and keeps nothing claimed
- [`apps/rail402/test/observability.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/observability.integration.test.ts): measures the wait for a channel when settlements outnumber channels
- [`packages/facilitator/test/preflight.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/preflight.test.ts): refuses any operation source, which settlement keeps but cannot sign for
- [`packages/facilitator/test/operation-source.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/operation-source.integration.test.ts): is refused at verify and settle, before any claim, channel or fee
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): finishes as a submission failure once the authorization expired unused, freeing the channel and fee budget
- [`packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/first-submission.test.ts): stays open when the resend is refused for a reason that can pass

**Residual risk.** Throughput is bounded by the channel count; raise CHANNEL_COUNT. A settlement whose submission the network accepted, or whose exact first submission was refused, holds its channel until it is final, at most until its authorization expires; one whose channel sequence number another transaction took gives it back at once.

### O6. The sponsor secret is compromised

Accepted risk · Elevation of privilege, Information disclosure · boundary TB4 (Facilitator and its state) · asset: Secrets

**Threat.** The sponsor seed leaks from the platform, a log or a backup.

**What Stellar makes different.** The sponsor seed derives the channels and the upto authority; none of them can move a payer's funds.

**Preconditions.** A leak. **Impact.** Loss of the sponsor's XLM; U10 for upto payloads in flight.

**Mitigations**

- The seed is never logged or echoed; configuration errors name the variable, not the value. ([`apps/rail402/src/logger.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/logger.ts), `REDACTED_PATHS`)
- Documented rotation: retire channels, move funds, provision with the new seed. ([`docs/operations.md`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/docs/operations.md), `Rotating the sponsor key`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts): names the variable and never echoes a secret
- [`packages/facilitator/test/state.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/state.test.ts): is deterministic per sponsor, network and index, and never collides
- [`apps/rail402/test/channels.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/channels.integration.test.ts): reports durable state and refuses to retire while a channel is leased

**Residual risk.** Keep only a working balance on the sponsor.

### O7. Degraded search indexing

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Catalog and search integrity

**Threat.** The embedding model fails or is slow, or its files are tampered with.

**What Stellar makes different.** None.

**Preconditions.** A model failure or a changed model file. **Impact.** Wrong or missing search results.

**Mitigations**

- Model files are pinned by SHA-256; search falls back to lexical results and says so with partialResults. ([`packages/search/src/embedder.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/src/embedder.ts), `sha256`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/search/test/search.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/test/search.test.ts): answers lexical-only, as partial results, when the embedder fails or runs out of time
- [`packages/search/test/embedder.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/test/embedder.test.ts): refuses to start when a model file does not match its pinned hash

**Residual risk.** None.

### O8. The pubnet RPC quota is exhausted

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB3 (Facilitator and Stellar) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** Every /verify simulates; a /verify flood, other lookups or harness runs exhaust a keyless provider's quota and settlement stops.

**What Stellar makes different.** Public keyless mainnet RPCs limit requests per IP (one documents 60 per 10 seconds) without an SLA.

**Preconditions.** A single provider with a quota. **Impact.** Settlements and verifications fail until the quota resets.

**Mitigations**

- An ordered provider list per network: after two unhealthy polls the next healthy provider takes over, with per-provider metrics and the active provider in /health; pubnet requires two providers when several networks are served. ([`packages/facilitator/src/rpc-pool.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/rpc-pool.ts), `RpcPool`)
- Pubnet /verify needs a key and each key has its own request limit. ([`apps/rail402/src/app.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/app.ts), `keyRequestsPerMinute`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/rpc-pool.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/rpc-pool.test.ts): moves to the next provider on quota errors and reports it
- [`apps/rail402/test/runtime.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/runtime.integration.test.ts): fails over to the next RPC provider and reports the active one
- [`apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts): requires two pubnet RPC providers when several networks are served, unless told otherwise

**Residual risk.** All providers down stops pubnet (O3).

### O9. A lying or compromised RPC

Accepted risk · Spoofing, Tampering · boundary TB3 (Facilitator and Stellar) · asset: Seller revenue

**Threat.** A provider returns false simulations, fee stats, balances, ledger numbers or transaction status: it can push the fee oracle to its cap, report success for a failed settlement or failure for a success.

**What Stellar makes different.** The provider cannot change what the payer signed (recipient, amount, cap) or move funds; it controls liveness, fees within the ceilings, and what the facilitator believes happened.

**Preconditions.** A compromised or faulty provider. **Impact.** Fees up to the ceilings; a seller told a payment settled that did not, or the reverse.

**Mitigations**

- Fee ceilings bound what any simulation can make the sponsor pay, whatever the provider reports. ([`packages/facilitator/src/policy.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/policy.ts), `maxTransactionFeeStroops`)
- Operators choose the providers; their URLs never appear in logs, metrics or /health. ([`apps/rail402/src/config.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/config.ts), `rpcProviders`)

**Tests.** None: this is a documented limit, not a behaviour Rail402 implements.

**Residual risk.** Accepted: providers are trusted for liveness and transaction status; a settlement reported successful is not confirmed by a second provider.

### O10. Failover resubmits a settlement

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB3 (Facilitator and Stellar) · asset: Sponsor XLM

**Threat.** A provider times out after accepting a transaction; failover sends the same or a rebuilt envelope through another provider.

**What Stellar makes different.** The payer's nonce makes a second inclusion fail; a rebuilt envelope would cost a second fee.

**Preconditions.** A provider switch while a settlement is in flight. **Impact.** At most a second fee per settlement.

**Mitigations**

- Each settlement simulates and submits through the provider active when it was claimed; the pool switches only between polls. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `pinned`)
- Only the recorded envelope bytes are ever rebroadcast, through any provider; a different transaction is never built for a recorded settlement. ([`packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/engine.ts), `rebroadcast`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/rpc-pool.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/rpc-pool.test.ts): pins a settlement to the provider active when it was claimed
- [`packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/settlement.integration.test.ts): finds the original transaction when the broadcast response is lost

**Residual risk.** At most one wasted fee.

### O11. Alerts never arrive

Mitigated · Denial of service, Repudiation · boundary TB14 (Monitoring and status) · asset: Alerting and status

**Threat.** The alerting stack runs on the host it watches; a host outage, a stopped Alertmanager or a broken notification channel means a sponsor drain or an outage goes unnoticed.

**What Stellar makes different.** A sponsor drain spends real XLM within the hour, and an unnoticed stale RPC stops settlement; both need a person within minutes.

**Preconditions.** A host, Prometheus or Alertmanager failure, or a misrouted channel. **Impact.** Failures continue without anyone being told.

**Mitigations**

- Alertmanager sends an always-firing Watchdog alert every minute to an off-host dead-man's switch, which alerts by itself when the heartbeat stops. ([`deploy/monitoring/alertmanager.yml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/monitoring/alertmanager.yml), `receiver: deadman`)
- Every alert in the catalog has a severity, an owner role and a runbook section; pages go to both on-call roles. ([`deploy/monitoring/alerts.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/monitoring/alerts.ts), `owner`)
- Prometheus scrapes each Explorer indexer, the Explorer web role and the hosted MCP endpoint by fixed target, so a stopped one stays a target; indexer down, indexing failures or chain breaks, lag, web down, MCP down and MCP upstream-budget refusals each raise a ticket. ([`deploy/monitoring/prometheus.yml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/monitoring/prometheus.yml), `rail402-explorer-indexer`)
- A page silences only the tickets of its own cause on its own network (every channel busy covers channel lease timeouts, a stale RPC covers the failover away from it); every other alert is always sent, so an unrelated page never hides an independent failure. ([`deploy/monitoring/alertmanager.yml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/monitoring/alertmanager.yml), `equal: [network, cause]`)
- The sponsor balance alert fires below each network's readiness floor and, on every network but testnet, below 100 XLM whatever the floor; pubnet refuses a floor below 1 XLM at startup, so a low, zero or missing floor never switches the page off. ([`apps/rail402/src/config.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/config.ts), `config_pubnet_sponsor_floor_invalid`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/conformance/test/monitoring.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/monitoring.test.ts): every alert has a severity, an owner and a runbook link
- [`tools/conformance/test/monitoring.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/monitoring.test.ts): reads only series Rail402 exports, or marks the alert as waiting for a newer series
- [`deploy/monitoring/alerts.test.yml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/monitoring/alerts.test.yml): Rail402SponsorBalanceLow
- [`deploy/monitoring/alerts.test.yml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/monitoring/alerts.test.yml): Rail402NoReplica
- [`tools/conformance/test/monitoring.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/monitoring.test.ts): scrapes the Explorer and the hosted MCP endpoint, and alerts when they stop
- [`deploy/monitoring/alerts.test.yml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/monitoring/alerts.test.yml): Rail402ExplorerIndexerDown
- [`tools/conformance/test/alertmanager.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/alertmanager.integration.test.ts): lets a page silence only the tickets of its own cause on its own network
- [`tools/conformance/test/monitoring.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/monitoring.test.ts): lets a page silence only the tickets of its own cause, on its own network
- [`deploy/monitoring/alerts.test.yml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/monitoring/alerts.test.yml): Rail402ChannelLeaseTimeouts
- [`apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts): refuses a pubnet sponsor floor of 0, which would switch readiness and the balance alert off

**Residual risk.** Notification latency; the dead-man's switch itself is an outside service.

### O12. Monitoring components exposed, reached from the service, or used as a probe

Mitigated · Information disclosure, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB14 (Monitoring and status) · asset: Operator infrastructure

**Threat.** A reachable Prometheus or Alertmanager leaks every series and lets anyone silence alerts; the blackbox exporter's /probe?target= becomes a request-forgery primitive into internal networks. The attacker who matters most is already inside: code execution in the facilitator, the process that holds the hot sponsor seed, could drain the sponsor and post a silence to Alertmanager's unauthenticated API so that no page fires, while the Watchdog heartbeat keeps the dead-man's switch green.

**What Stellar makes different.** The private metrics carry pubnet sponsor balances and budget figures, which tell an attacker when a drain would go unnoticed.

**Preconditions.** A published port or a route from the edge proxy to a monitoring service, or code execution in a container that shares a network with Alertmanager. **Impact.** Information disclosure, silenced alerts, or requests to internal services.

**Mitigations**

- Monitoring services publish no port and sit only on internal networks; only Alertmanager has an outbound network, for notifications. ([`deploy/monitoring/compose.example.yaml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/monitoring/compose.example.yaml), `internal: true`)
- The networks are split by who may talk to whom: the facilitator replicas share only the scrape network with Prometheus and the blackbox exporter; Alertmanager sits on an alerting network with Prometheus alone, and node_exporter on its own network, so the service can reach neither. ([`deploy/monitoring/compose.example.yaml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/monitoring/compose.example.yaml), `networks: [alerting, alerting-egress]`)
- Prometheus runs without its admin and lifecycle APIs, so whoever reaches it on the scrape network can read series but cannot delete them, reload rules or stop it. ([`deploy/monitoring/compose.example.yaml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/monitoring/compose.example.yaml), `No --web.enable-admin-api`)
- The blackbox exporter allows only two modules without redirects, and Prometheus fixes every probe target. ([`deploy/monitoring/blackbox.yml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/monitoring/blackbox.yml), `follow_redirects: false`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/conformance/test/monitoring.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/monitoring.test.ts): publishes no monitoring port and probes only listed targets
- [`tools/conformance/test/monitoring.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/monitoring.test.ts): keeps the service off the alerting network: only Prometheus reaches Alertmanager
- [`tools/conformance/test/monitoring.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/monitoring.test.ts): scrapes the private metrics with a token read from a file

**Residual risk.** The facilitator can read Prometheus' series over the scrape network (no secrets: metrics only, which it largely produces itself) and send blackbox probes to the other members of that network. Root on the host bypasses every network boundary (K6).

### O13. The public status page misstates availability

Open · Tampering, Repudiation · boundary TB14 (Monitoring and status) · asset: Alerting and status

**Threat.** The status page is measured from the host it reports on, counts only easy endpoints, or accepts an error page as up, so it shows availability the service did not have.

**What Stellar makes different.** Sellers and buyers decide whether to rely on a facilitator from its published availability; a network-wide RPC outage must not be hidden or blamed on the wrong component.

**Preconditions.** Checks run on the host, cover only some endpoints, or assert status codes only. **Impact.** A false availability record.

**Mitigations**

- Read-only checks from outside the host about every 5 minutes against every public endpoint of both networks, each with a content assertion; each network's readiness (/ready/testnet, /ready/pubnet) is its own component, and /health never stands for one network. ([`deploy/status/upptimerc.yml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/status/upptimerc.yml), `__dangerous__body_down_if_text_missing`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/conformance/test/status.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/status.test.ts): the status checks cover every pubnet endpoint
- [`tools/conformance/test/status.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/status.test.ts): only reads: https GETs without credentials

**Residual risk.** Open until the status repository is published and has recorded its first window; until then availability is measured only by the internal monitoring. Outages shorter than the check interval are not seen by the page.

### O14. A self-hosted facilitator depends on Rail402's hosted service

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB5 (Facilitator and the open internet) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** A self-hosted or in-process facilitator quietly calls a Rail402 host (for configuration, verification, metering or telemetry), so an outage, block or takeover of Rail402's hosted service stops or influences settlement for operators who never chose to depend on it.

**What Stellar makes different.** n/a.

**Preconditions.** An operator self-hosts the image or runs the facilitator in-process. **Impact.** Settlement stops with Rail402's hosted service, or a Rail402 host learns about another operator's payments.

**Mitigations**

- The facilitator reaches only the Stellar RPC providers it is configured with (and public Horizon through the upstream verifier, with a fallback); the service adds only its own database. No runtime code names a Rail402 host. ([`packages/facilitator/src/rpc-pool.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/rpc-pool.ts), `RpcPool`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/image.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/image.integration.test.ts): has no route to the internet or to any Rail402 host
- [`apps/rail402/test/image.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/image.integration.test.ts): settles exact and upto for a key holder, with the payer's funds going to the seller
- [`packages/facilitator/test/in-process.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/in-process.integration.test.ts): settles part of an upto cap chosen after the request, without an HTTP facilitator
- [`packages/facilitator/test/in-process-controls.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/in-process-controls.integration.test.ts): never asked a Rail402 host

**Residual risk.** None for Rail402 hosts. The upstream verifier's Horizon read depends on Stellar's public Horizon and falls back to a 5-second ledger estimate when it fails.

### O15. Script injected into the status page through its check history

Mitigated · Tampering, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB14 (Monitoring and status) · asset: Alerting and status

**Threat.** The status page renders the check history (history/summary.json), written by the status repository's workflow, into its HTML. Whoever can write that file (a leaked workflow token, a bad checker release) could run script on the status page and show any availability.

**What Stellar makes different.** n/a.

**Preconditions.** Write access to the status repository's history files. **Impact.** Without protection: script on the status host and a forged availability record.

**Mitigations**

- Every value from the history and the incident list is escaped, and a day's downtime is read as a whole number of minutes, so data never becomes markup. ([`deploy/status/site/index.html`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/status/site/index.html), `only a count of minutes is used`)
- The page carries a Content-Security-Policy that allows only its one inline script, by SHA-256, and the served header is built from the same script at install, so an injected script or event handler cannot run on either path. ([`deploy/status/site/index.html`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/status/site/index.html), `http-equiv="Content-Security-Policy"`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/conformance/test/status-page.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/status-page.test.ts): writes the check history into the page as text only
- [`tools/conformance/test/status-page.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/status-page.test.ts): allows its one inline script by hash and nothing else

**Residual risk.** The page loads nothing from another origin (its styles, fonts and images come from the status host, and the policy allows no other), so a visit reaches no third party. A forged history can still show wrong figures, as text.

{/* generated:end */}

## The Playground

A testnet key in a browser page, and a server that relays the page's seller requests: what a visitor, another
site or a seller could do with either.

{/* generated:begin topic-playground */}

### PG1. The relay used to reach private networks or as an open proxy

Mitigated · Spoofing, Information disclosure, Denial of service · boundary TB8 (Playground page and server) · asset: Operator infrastructure

**Threat.** A visitor, or a script calling the Playground's API, asks the relay to fetch a loopback, private or cloud-metadata address, a name that resolves to one, or a seller that redirects to one; or uses the relay to send volume to third parties from the Playground's address.

**What Stellar makes different.** Nothing Stellar-specific: the relay exists because Rail402 and most x402 sellers answer no CORS headers, so a page cannot call them itself. A paid request through it carries a PAYMENT-SIGNATURE the visitor's own key made.

**Preconditions.** Network access to the Playground server. **Impact.** Without protection: requests into the server's network, or the Playground's address used for abuse.

**Mitigations**

- The relay sends with safe-fetch under a fixed policy: https on port 443 to public addresses only, DNS answers checked and pinned, redirects refused except same-origin hops for unpaid probes, request and answer size limits. ([`apps/playground/src/relay/egress.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/src/relay/egress.ts), `RelayEgress`)
- By host name, it never contacts the Playground itself (its public host and the platform's generated domain), the facilitator, the Bazaar, the other host names of that service (a facilitator at testnet.&lt;domain&gt; also denies mainnet., pubnet. and explorer.&lt;domain&gt;) or an operator-denied host. ([`apps/playground/src/relay/egress.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/src/relay/egress.ts), `operatorSiblings`)
- Per-client, global, per-host, in-flight and daily byte limits, with Retry-After, and a switch to turn the relay off. ([`apps/playground/src/limits.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/src/limits.ts), `DailyBudget`)
- Global limits are fair-shared: one network (an IPv4 address or an IPv6 /48) holds at most an eighth of a pool, or its per-client limit when that is larger; clients are keyed by IPv4 address or IPv6 /56. ([`apps/playground/src/limits.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/src/limits.ts), `SharedRate`)
- Only a payment that decodes as an x402 payment counts against the paid limits, whether it travels in PAYMENT-SIGNATURE or in an MCP tool call's \_meta; anything else is refused before a token is taken. ([`apps/playground/src/payment-header.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/src/payment-header.ts), `mcpPaymentOf`)
- The demo seller has a paid pool of its own, a paid request that reached no seller gives the shared pool its token back, and the lab sellers return it when the facilitator was never asked, so payments that only have the right shape cannot use up the pools the demo flows need. ([`apps/playground/src/relay/relay.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/src/relay/relay.ts), `trustedPaidGlobal`)
- Unpaid requests wait no longer than the SDK's probe timeouts; bodies pass only as JSON, text or octet-stream; slots of an abandoned upload or an unread answer are released; connections to hosts other than the demo seller are closed as soon as each answer's body ends, even when the seller never read the upload or keeps sending after a compressed body, and a redirect's without waiting for its body. ([`apps/playground/src/relay/relay.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/src/relay/relay.ts), `timeoutsFor`)
- Unpaid requests and answer bytes are also limited per network, bytes for all clients per day, requests in flight per destination host, and each network gets at most its share of a host's budget. ([`apps/playground/src/limits.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/src/limits.ts), `KeyedSharedRate`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/playground/test/ssrf.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/ssrf.test.ts): refuses every private, local, reserved or off-policy literal before any lookup
- [`apps/playground/test/ssrf.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/ssrf.test.ts): refuses a public name whose DNS answers include any private address
- [`apps/playground/test/ssrf.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/ssrf.test.ts): does not let the relay reach the Playground, the facilitator, the Bazaar or a denied host by any spelling
- [`apps/playground/test/ssrf.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/ssrf.test.ts): does not let the relay reach the other network and Explorer hosts of the same facilitator
- [`apps/playground/test/ssrf.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/ssrf.test.ts): never follows a redirect off the seller's origin, even for unpaid requests that allow redirects
- [`apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts): limits requests per destination host across clients
- [`apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts): stops a client past its daily transfer budget
- [`apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts): refuses a payment header that holds no payment before any limit counts it
- [`apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts): gives one network at most its share of the global paid budget
- [`apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts): gives back the slots of an upload the client abandons
- [`apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts): counts an MCP tool call carrying a payment in its body as paid
- [`apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts): keeps a paid pool of its own for the trusted hosts
- [`apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts): gives one network at most its share of a destination host's budget
- [`apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts): forwards a body only as JSON, text or octet-stream
- [`apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts): treats the platform's generated domain as the Playground itself
- [`apps/playground/test/relay-wire.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/relay-wire.test.ts): ends a body nobody reads at its deadline and cancels the seller's stream
- [`packages/safe-fetch/test/fetch.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/safe-fetch/test/fetch.test.ts): closes kept connections that sit unused and caps connections across hosts
- [`packages/safe-fetch/test/fetch.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/safe-fetch/test/fetch.test.ts): closes a refused redirect instead of reading its body, and frees its connection
- [`packages/safe-fetch/test/fetch.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/safe-fetch/test/fetch.test.ts): closes the connection once the answer is read
- [`packages/safe-fetch/test/fetch.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/safe-fetch/test/fetch.test.ts): closes the connection when a compressed body ends before the answer
- [`apps/playground/e2e/playground.spec.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/e2e/playground.spec.ts): never follows a redirect to another origin, and signs nothing

**Conformance runs.** `live-playground` case `ssrf-literal`, `live-playground` case `ssrf-metadata`, `live-playground` case `redirect`.

**Residual risk.** For a client that is not a browser, the relay is an open https egress to public hosts at the Playground's address, bounded only by the limits; IP-keyed limits cannot stop a client with many addresses or networks. The payment check stops only malformed payments: a payment that merely has the right shape is counted as paid, so enough addresses can still use up a shared paid pool for a minute at a time. Loop and deny lists match host names: another name of a denied host (beyond the derived sibling names) is caught only when the operator lists it in RELAY_DENY_HOSTS. Its user agent names the Playground, and the operator can deny hosts or switch the relay off.

### PG2. Another site drives the relay from a visitor's browser

Mitigated · Spoofing, Information disclosure · boundary TB8 (Playground page and server) · asset: Operator infrastructure

**Threat.** A page on another origin makes a visitor's browser send requests through the Playground's relay or proxies, or reads their answers.

**What Stellar makes different.** The relay holds no key and no session, so a forged request carries no authority the attacker lacks; a payment needs a signature from the key in the Playground's own page.

**Preconditions.** A visitor with the Playground open, or not, visits the attacker's page. **Impact.** Without protection: the Playground's relay used through visitors' browsers.

**Mitigations**

- The relay needs its own request headers, which a cross-site form cannot set; a browser's Sec-Fetch-Site must be same-origin and its Origin the Playground's public origin. ([`apps/playground/src/security.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/src/security.ts), `sameOrigin`)
- The relay answers no CORS headers, so no other origin can read an answer. ([`apps/playground/src/shared/relay-protocol.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/src/shared/relay-protocol.ts), `x-relay-target`)
- The Bazaar and /supported proxies refuse another site's requests the same way, so no page can spend their shared upstream budget through visitors' browsers. ([`apps/playground/src/proxy/bazaar.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/src/proxy/bazaar.ts), `proxyOrigin`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts): answers only same-origin requests from the page
- [`apps/playground/test/proxy.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/proxy.test.ts): refuses requests another site's page makes, before any upstream fetch

**Residual risk.** Clients that are not browsers send neither header and pass. They get nothing a browser visitor lacks, but they can use the relay as an https egress at the Playground's address, bounded only by the limits (PG1).

### PG3. Script injected into the page uses the testnet key

Mitigated · Tampering, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB8 (Playground page and server) · asset: Secrets

**Threat.** Seller-supplied text (a description, an error, a response body) or a third-party script runs in the page and signs with, or reads, the wallet's key.

**What Stellar makes different.** The key signs Soroban authorization entries through the SDK's signing guard; a script in the page could ask it to sign within the visitor's caps.

**Preconditions.** A seller or listing the visitor's page displays. **Impact.** Without protection: testnet funds spent within the caps, or the testnet key read out.

**Mitigations**

- A content security policy without inline or third-party script, styles or frames from the Playground's origin only, and connections only to the configured Stellar endpoints. ([`apps/playground/src/security.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/src/security.ts), `contentSecurityPolicy`)
- Seller text is rendered as text, and an untrusted URL becomes a link only when it is https and parses; no view renders raw markup. ([`apps/playground/web/src/ui/links.tsx`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/web/src/ui/links.tsx), `safeHttpsUrl`)
- The stored seed is encrypted under a key WebCrypto generated as non-extractable; only the wallet modules touch the key. ([`apps/playground/web/src/wallet/keystore.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/web/src/wallet/keystore.ts), `non-extractable`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/playground/test/app.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/app.test.ts): sends the security headers and a strict content security policy on every response
- [`apps/playground/web/test/engine/dependency.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/web/test/engine/dependency.test.ts): the page never renders raw markup
- [`apps/playground/web/test/engine/dependency.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/web/test/engine/dependency.test.ts): only the wallet modules touch the key itself
- [`apps/playground/web/test/app/shell.test.tsx`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/web/test/app/shell.test.tsx): does not render HTML from seller data
- [`apps/playground/e2e/playground.spec.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/e2e/playground.spec.ts): serves the built page with a strict CSP and makes a funded test wallet on the first visit

**Conformance runs.** `live-playground` case `secret-scan`.

**Residual risk.** A script that did run in the page would not be bound by the caps: it could open the page's IndexedDB, decrypt the stored seed with the stored key (non-extractable protects the key, not what it decrypts) and send the seed out. The CSP and the absence of markup sinks make that unlikely. The key is meant for testnet only: the page makes it for the visitor and never shows it.

### PG4. The lab sellers take real payments or pollute the catalog

Mitigated · Tampering, Denial of service · boundary TB8 (Playground page and server) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** The Playground's misbehaving sellers (the offers, the greedy upto route) take a payment that sticks, or every visitor's sandbox sale adds a listing to the public Bazaar.

**What Stellar makes different.** An upto authorization caps what any settlement can move; Rail402 refuses a settlement above it, and the settlement contract enforces the cap on chain too.

**Preconditions.** A visitor running the lab. **Impact.** Without protection: visitors charged more than they authorized, or a catalog flooded with sample listings.

**Mitigations**

- The offers answer a 402 only and never verify or settle. ([`apps/playground/src/lab/offers.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/src/lab/offers.ts), `offerHandler`)
- The greedy route asks to settle twice its cap, which Rail402's upto rules refuse; it declares no discovery metadata. ([`packages/upto/src/facilitator/rules.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/upto/src/facilitator/rules.ts), `invalid_upto_stellar_payload_settlement_exceeds_amount`)
- The sandbox route answers the same 402 for every city and visitor, so it is one listing paid to one operator account. ([`apps/playground/src/lab/sell.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/src/lab/sell.ts), `declareSellRoute`)
- Its discovery metadata always carries the declared example city, never the one a request names, and a path that is not a city name gets no 402, so no payer writes text into the shared listing. ([`apps/playground/src/lab/sell.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/src/lab/sell.ts), `sellBazaarExtension`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/playground/test/lab-sellers.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/lab-sellers.test.ts): answer a valid x402 version 2 402 only, never contacting the facilitator
- [`apps/playground/test/lab-sellers.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/lab-sellers.test.ts): asks the facilitator to settle twice the cap and reports the refusal
- [`apps/playground/test/lab-sellers.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/lab-sellers.test.ts): answers the same 402 every time, for any city, so the origin check always agrees
- [`apps/playground/test/lab-sellers.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/lab-sellers.test.ts): keeps the declared example in every 402, whatever city the request names
- [`apps/playground/test/lab-sellers.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/lab-sellers.test.ts): answers anything but a city name with a coded 404, before any 402
- [`apps/playground/web/test/integration/engine.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/web/test/integration/engine.integration.test.ts): blocks a seller that charges over the cap: nothing moves, the hold returns after expiry
- [`apps/playground/web/test/integration/engine.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/web/test/integration/engine.integration.test.ts): sells through the sandbox route: settled, cataloged, origin-checked and found by search

**Conformance runs.** `live-playground` case `overcharge`, `live-playground` case `refusal-network`, `live-playground` case `refusal-asset`, `live-playground` case `refusal-amount`.

**Residual risk.** None for funds. The sandbox listing is public and permanent, as one listing; its resource URL names the city of the payment that created it.

### PG5. The stored wallet read or overspent

Mitigated · Information disclosure, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB8 (Playground page and server) · asset: Agent budgets

**Threat.** The wallet's stored record is swapped or tampered with, or two tabs spend one session budget at the same time.

**What Stellar makes different.** The budget ledger reserves each payment before it is signed; two writers of one stored ledger could each see room the other already used.

**Preconditions.** Access to the browser profile, or two open tabs. **Impact.** Without protection: a different key loaded, or the session budget exceeded.

**Mitigations**

- The stored record is bound to its address and network; a tampered or rebound record is refused. ([`apps/playground/web/src/wallet/keystore.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/web/src/wallet/keystore.ts), `additionalData`)
- One tab holds the wallet through a Web Lock; the others are read-only. ([`apps/playground/web/src/wallet/tab-lock.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/web/src/wallet/tab-lock.ts), `acquireTabLock`)
- A stored ledger that cannot be read is refused until it is reset, never silently emptied. ([`packages/sdk/src/policy/storage-ledger.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/src/policy/storage-ledger.ts), `payment_ledger_unavailable`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/playground/web/test/wallet/keystore.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/web/test/wallet/keystore.test.ts): stores the seed encrypted under a non-extractable key and loads it back
- [`apps/playground/web/test/wallet/keystore.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/web/test/wallet/keystore.test.ts): refuses a tampered or rebound record instead of loading another key
- [`apps/playground/web/test/wallet/tab-lock.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/web/test/wallet/tab-lock.test.ts): lets one tab hold the wallet; another waits until it lets go
- [`apps/playground/e2e/playground.spec.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/e2e/playground.spec.ts): keeps the wallet across a reload, and in one tab only

**Conformance runs.** `live-playground` case `refusal-session`.

**Residual risk.** The record is encrypted under a key kept in the same browser profile, not under a passphrase: anyone using that profile can use the wallet through the page. It is a testnet key with testnet funds.

### PG6. Payments or the key leak into logs, traces or storage

Mitigated · Information disclosure · boundary TB8 (Playground page and server) · asset: Secrets

**Threat.** A PAYMENT-SIGNATURE, a signed authorization or the key ends up in the server's logs or traces, an error, the page's storage, a run report or a request to a third party.

**What Stellar makes different.** A signed authorization is spendable by whoever holds it until it expires.

**Preconditions.** Any run. **Impact.** Without protection: an authorization replayable by whoever reads the channel, or the key exposed.

**Mitigations**

- The relay logs the host, status and outcome only, never the target path, query, headers or body. ([`apps/playground/src/relay/relay.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/src/relay/relay.ts), `upstreamStatus`)
- The lab sellers' trace header carries verify and settle outcomes, never payloads. ([`apps/playground/src/lab/trace.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/src/lab/trace.ts), `TraceRecorder`)
- Run reports are built from the run's events, which hold no key, signature, preimage or payment header. ([`apps/playground/web/src/engine/run-report.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/web/src/engine/run-report.ts), `runReport`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/playground/test/secret-scan.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/secret-scan.test.ts): keeps the payment and the key out of every channel but the seller and the facilitator
- [`apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/test/relay.test.ts): logs the host and outcome, never the target path, query, headers or body
- [`apps/playground/web/test/integration/engine.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/web/test/integration/engine.integration.test.ts): keeps the key and the payments out of every other channel
- [`apps/playground/e2e/playground.spec.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/playground/e2e/playground.spec.ts): keeps the key and the payments out of every other channel

**Conformance runs.** `live-playground` case `secret-scan`.

**Residual risk.** The relay holds a payment header in memory for the one request that carries it, as any seller does.

{/* generated:end */}

## Upstream watch

A scheduled workflow reads upstream x402 and npm, and writes one public tracking issue with a token that can
write issues: what upstream text, a forged issue or a failed fetch could do with it.

{/* generated:begin topic-spec-watch */}

### SW1. Upstream text injects mentions, links or markup into the tracker

Mitigated · Tampering, Spoofing · boundary TB16 (Upstream watch) · asset: Project tracker

**Threat.** Upstream commit titles and diffs carry @mentions that ping people, HTML, Markdown links to phishing pages or hidden comments that imitate the tracking marker, and the watcher copies them into a public issue.

**What Stellar makes different.** Nothing Stellar-specific: the watched text comes from the x402 repository and npm, which anyone can contribute to.

**Preconditions.** An upstream commit or file whose text the watcher reports. **Impact.** Without escaping: people pinged from the project's tracker, rendered markup or links under the project's name, or a forged marker that misleads the next run.

**Mitigations**

- Upstream text is only placed in code spans (one line, backticks replaced, control and format characters removed) or in fences longer than any backtick run inside; comment openers are broken up, so the marker only ever appears on the first line, where the next run reads it. ([`tools/upstream-watch/src/render.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/upstream-watch/src/render.ts), `escapeUpstream`)
- Links are built by the watcher, to github.com and npmjs.com only; pull request numbers in commit titles become links to the upstream repository. ([`tools/upstream-watch/src/render.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/upstream-watch/src/render.ts), `pullLinks`)
