- The body is capped at 60,000 characters: diffs and commit lists shrink first, then the body is cut. ([`tools/upstream-watch/src/render.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/upstream-watch/src/render.ts), `MAX_BODY`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/upstream-watch/test/lifecycle.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/upstream-watch/test/lifecycle.test.ts): escapes mentions, HTML and marker look-alikes from upstream text
- [`tools/upstream-watch/test/lifecycle.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/upstream-watch/test/lifecycle.test.ts): keeps the body under the size limit

**Residual risk.** None.

### SW2. The workflow token can do more than write issues

Mitigated · Elevation of privilege · boundary TB16 (Upstream watch) · asset: Project tracker

**Threat.** A broad token, an unpinned action, a script built from workflow inputs, or a step that checks out and runs upstream code turns the scheduled job into a way to write the repository or read secrets.

**What Stellar makes different.** Nothing Stellar-specific.

**Preconditions.** A compromised action tag, a malicious upstream change, or a crafted dispatch input. **Impact.** Without the limits: repository writes or secret disclosure from a job that runs every week unattended.

**Mitigations**

- The workflow grants contents read and issues write only, pins every action by commit SHA, uses no secret but its own token, does not keep the checkout's credentials, installs only the watcher's own dependencies without install scripts, passes inputs through the environment, never checks out or runs upstream code, and runs in this repository only. ([`.github/workflows/upstream-watch.yml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/.github/workflows/upstream-watch.yml), `issues: write`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/upstream-watch/test/workflow.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/upstream-watch/test/workflow.test.ts): grants only contents read and issues write and pins every action by SHA
- [`tools/upstream-watch/test/workflow.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/upstream-watch/test/workflow.test.ts): uses no secret but its own token and never fetches or runs upstream code
- [`tools/upstream-watch/test/workflow.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/upstream-watch/test/workflow.test.ts): runs weekly and on demand, in this repository only

**Residual risk.** The job trusts GitHub's runner, the pinned actions and the npm packages in the lockfile it installs (zod).

### SW3. Someone else's issue taken for the tracking issue

Mitigated · Spoofing, Tampering · boundary TB16 (Upstream watch) · asset: Project tracker

**Threat.** Anyone can open an issue with the label and a copy of the hidden marker, so the watcher edits or closes it, or ignores the real tracking issue.

**What Stellar makes different.** Nothing Stellar-specific.

**Preconditions.** Permission to open issues in the public tracker. **Impact.** Without the check: a person's issue rewritten or closed by the workflow, or real upstream drift hidden behind a forged fingerprint.

**Mitigations**

- Only issues opened by the workflow's own identity, with the label and the marker on the first line, count as tracking issues. ([`tools/upstream-watch/src/issues.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/upstream-watch/src/issues.ts), `isTrackingIssue`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/upstream-watch/test/lifecycle.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/upstream-watch/test/lifecycle.test.ts): ignores a marked issue by another author
- [`tools/upstream-watch/test/lifecycle.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/upstream-watch/test/lifecycle.test.ts): writes nothing on a rerun with the same drift, even beside other labelled issues

**Residual risk.** None.

### SW4. A fetch failure closes the drift issue

Mitigated · Tampering, Repudiation · boundary TB16 (Upstream watch) · asset: Project tracker

**Threat.** A rate limit, an error or an empty answer reads as "no drift", so the issue is closed as resolved while upstream still differs, or a run that checked nothing renders as a pass.

**What Stellar makes different.** Nothing Stellar-specific.

**Preconditions.** GitHub or npm unavailable, rate-limited or answering without the expected fields. **Impact.** Without the check: upstream changes silently dropped from review.

**Mitigations**

- Every watched entry must be checked; any fetch error, non-2xx answer or empty answer fails the run with exit code 2 before the issue is read or written. ([`tools/upstream-watch/src/compare.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/upstream-watch/src/compare.ts), `FetchIncomplete`)
- A watch list without files or packages is refused. ([`tools/upstream-watch/src/watch-list.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/upstream-watch/src/watch-list.ts), `watchListSchema`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/upstream-watch/test/lifecycle.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/upstream-watch/test/lifecycle.test.ts): neither edits nor closes the issue when an upstream fetch fails
- [`tools/upstream-watch/test/lifecycle.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/upstream-watch/test/lifecycle.test.ts): never renders zero cases as a pass

**Residual risk.** None.

{/* generated:end */}

## One service, two networks

One facilitator process serves the keyless testnet and pubnet: what one network's traffic, faults or
access could do to the other.

{/* generated:begin topic-network-isolation */}

### N1. A testnet flood takes pubnet's request capacity

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB9 (Testnet and pubnet inside one service) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** Free testnet traffic from many addresses saturates the shared limiter, the event loop and the CPU, so pubnet /verify and /settle time out.

**What Stellar makes different.** n/a.

**Preconditions.** One service serving both networks. **Impact.** Pubnet refusals or timeouts caused by testnet load.

**Mitigations**

- Before the body is read, a /verify or /settle with a usable access key is charged to a bucket of that key on every host, never to its client address: keyless traffic of either network, unknown paths and refused requests from the same address or IPv6 /64 never spend it. The bucket holds what the key may send a minute on all its networks, and at least RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE, so the key's own per-network limits are what refuse it. A request whose network does not admit the key pays its address's bucket as well, so a key never carries keyless payments past the address's limit. ([`apps/rail402/src/app.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/app.ts), `callerLimit`)
- Once the network and caller are known, each network applies its own limit: per key on pubnet, never the testnet address bucket. ([`apps/rail402/src/app.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/app.ts), `networkLimiter`)
- A held or starting network refuses only its own requests. ([`apps/rail402/src/network-state.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/network-state.ts), `NetworkGate`)
- The responses of a connection, including those the server gives without reaching the app, must be handed to the operating system's socket buffer within RESPONSE_WRITE_TIMEOUT_MS of being ready, and one connection may have at most MAX_PIPELINED_REQUESTS requests waiting for their answers, on every route; otherwise the connection is reset and every response it held released. A response's clock starts once it and every response before it on the connection are ready, so a pipelined backlog shares one deadline and the time handlers take to answer never counts. ([`apps/rail402/src/server.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/server.ts), `deadlineOf`)
- A discovery request keeps its admission slot until its response has been handed to the client's connection, so the slots bound the discovery pages the process keeps for clients that have not taken them, and one client (an address, or a forwarded client of a discovery-internal key) holds at most DISCOVERY_MAX_IN_FLIGHT_PER_CLIENT requests of a lane. ([`apps/rail402/src/app.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/app.ts), `afterSent`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts): does not rate-limit a pubnet key while testnet clients from the same address are limited
- [`apps/rail402/test/server.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/server.test.ts): resets the connection of a client that stops reading, and releases the response it held
- [`apps/rail402/test/server.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/server.test.ts): resets a connection whose pipelined responses are not all taken within one deadline of being ready
- [`apps/rail402/test/server.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/server.test.ts): caps the requests one connection may have waiting for their answers, on every route
- [`apps/rail402/test/server.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/server.test.ts): are timed: a connection that does not read them is reset within the deadline
- [`apps/rail402/test/server.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/server.test.ts): does not cut a request whose handler answers after the deadline
- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.test.ts): hold their slot until they are sent, so the pages one process keeps for its clients are bounded
- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.test.ts): limit how many one client holds at once, so one client cannot take every slot
- [`apps/rail402/test/public-hosts.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/public-hosts.test.ts): never lets anonymous or other-network traffic from its address or /64 spend a key holder's limit
- [`apps/rail402/test/public-hosts.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/public-hosts.test.ts): sizes a key's own limit to every network it may use, never below RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE
- [`apps/rail402/test/public-hosts.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/public-hosts.test.ts): keeps a key the host's network does not admit on its address's limit
- [`apps/rail402/test/public-hosts.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/public-hosts.test.ts): never lets a key carry payments past its address's limit on a network that does not admit it
- [`apps/rail402/test/pubnet-pool.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/pubnet-pool.integration.test.ts): admits a pubnet key holder on every host after anonymous testnet traffic spent its address's limit

**Residual risk.** A flood above the container's raw capacity still degrades both networks; a dedicated pubnet process or host is the later option. Clients from enough addresses can fill a discovery lane's slots, slow readers included, for as long as they keep asking. Open connections are bounded only by the proxy in front and the process's file-descriptor limit: each holds at most MAX_PIPELINED_REQUESTS answers for at most RESPONSE_WRITE_TIMEOUT_MS.

### N2. Search embedding on testnet starves pubnet settlement

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB9 (Testnet and pubnet inside one service) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** Every search runs the embedding model in the facilitator process; a testnet search flood or an index rebuild after testnet churn uses the CPU that pubnet settlement needs.

**What Stellar makes different.** One process serves keyless testnet discovery and pubnet settlement on the same CPUs.

**Preconditions.** Many distinct searches, or frequent catalog changes. **Impact.** Without protection: pubnet verify and settle slow down or time out.

**Mitigations**

- Embedder concurrency cap shared by queries and index builds; a query that finds the model busy is answered lexical-only, as partial results. ([`packages/search/src/service.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/src/service.ts), `EmbedGate`)
- Query-embedding LRU cache and a cost-weighted per-client discovery bucket. ([`apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts), `searchCost`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/search/test/search.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/test/search.test.ts): answers lexical-only once the embedder is at its concurrency cap
- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery.test.ts): limits uncached searches more tightly than cached ones
- [`tools/loadtest/test/settle.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/loadtest/test/settle.integration.test.ts): settles real payments during a search flood and reports their latency percentiles

**Residual risk.** Search quality degrades to lexical under load. Settle latency under a search flood is measured with the load package's settle probe (tools/loadtest, local private network); the measurement against the production-config release stack is still to be recorded.

### N3. The shared database pool is exhausted by testnet work

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB9 (Testnet and pubnet inside one service) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** Testnet settlements, cataloging and limiter writes hold every connection in the one pool, so pubnet settlement claims wait or fail.

**What Stellar makes different.** n/a.

**Preconditions.** Heavy testnet load on Postgres. **Impact.** Pubnet settlements wait or fail.

**Mitigations**

- With several networks served, pubnet's settlement ledger and channel leases use a connection pool of their own. ([`apps/rail402/src/database-pools.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/database-pools.ts), `PUBNET_POOL_SIZE`)
- Pubnet's request limit, per-payer and per-payTo limits and metering use that pool too, routed by network. ([`apps/rail402/src/network-stores.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/network-stores.ts), `createAccessStores`)
- On the pubnet host (PUBLIC_HOSTS), and in a process that serves pubnet alone, the service-wide limit decides the key in memory first: a key pubnet admits is limited on pubnet's pool (the one pool when pubnet is served alone), every other caller in the process only, so no pubnet /verify or /settle on the pubnet host waits for a shared connection to be admitted and no anonymous caller's per-address limit there takes a connection of the pool pubnet's settlements use; where pubnet requires keys, such a caller is refused before any database work. With the Bazaar the discovery routes keep their limits on the discovery pools; without it a /discovery/ path is limited like any other. ([`apps/rail402/src/app.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/app.ts), `callerLimit`)
- Pubnet's account provisioning and upto contract TTL rounds take their sponsor lock through pubnet's pool. ([`apps/rail402/src/runtime.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/runtime.ts), `pubnetLock`)
- The public, unlimited /metrics answers from memory: channels in use is counted every second in the background, one count per network at a time, so scrapes never take a database connection. ([`apps/rail402/src/metrics.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/metrics.ts), `refreshChannelsInUse`)
- The runtime role's connection limit must hold every pool of every replica: the service reads it at startup, logs how many replicas it holds, and with pubnet refuses to start below one process's pools, so anonymous load on the shared and discovery pools never takes the role's slots that pubnet's pool and readiness need. ([`apps/rail402/src/database-checks.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/database-checks.ts), `checkConnectionBudget`)
- A client already over its limit is refused from memory, without another write, until its window ends (measured from a monotonic clock read before the refused write, so never longer); only requests already on their way to the database when its first refusal comes back still write. ([`packages/store-postgres/src/access.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/src/access.ts), `overLimit`)
- Every pool fails a query after DATABASE_ACQUIRE_TIMEOUT_MS without a connection, so pubnet's fail-closed limiters refuse with a code instead of queueing without end. ([`packages/store-postgres/src/database.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/src/database.ts), `acquireTimeoutMs`)
- Reconciliation runs per network, one round at a time per network; a network whose ledger or RPC fails or hangs never holds up another network's round. ([`packages/facilitator/src/facilitator.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/facilitator.ts), `reconcileNetwork`)
- The public /ready probes the database through one connection of its own, at most once every 2 seconds, and concurrent requests share the running probe, so a readiness flood never takes a connection from settlements and a queue for the shared pool is never reported as an outage. ([`apps/rail402/src/runtime.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/runtime.ts), `readinessProbe`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/pubnet-pool.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/pubnet-pool.integration.test.ts): settles pubnet through its own pool while testnet holds every shared connection
- [`apps/rail402/test/pubnet-pool.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/pubnet-pool.integration.test.ts): refuses pubnet with a code, not a hang, once its own pool is saturated
- [`apps/rail402/test/pubnet-pool.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/pubnet-pool.integration.test.ts): stays ready and admits a pubnet key holder at once while keyless testnet traffic holds the shared pool
- [`apps/rail402/test/pubnet-pool.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/pubnet-pool.integration.test.ts): gives keyless and refused mainnet-host callers no pubnet connection and no per-address write
- [`apps/rail402/test/pubnet-pool.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/pubnet-pool.integration.test.ts): takes pubnet's sponsor lock without a shared connection
- [`apps/rail402/test/public-hosts.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/public-hosts.test.ts): limits a pubnet key holder through pubnet's limiter and nobody else there through a shared one
- [`apps/rail402/test/public-hosts.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/public-hosts.test.ts): serves keyless callers where pubnet allows them, limited in the process and by pubnet's keyless limit
- [`apps/rail402/test/database-roles.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/database-roles.integration.test.ts): refuses pubnet a role whose connection limit cannot hold one process's pools
- [`apps/rail402/test/database-pools.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/database-pools.test.ts): adds pubnet's own pools when it is served next to testnet
- [`packages/store-postgres/test/stores.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/test/stores.integration.test.ts): refuses a key over its limit from memory, without another write, until its window ends
- [`packages/store-postgres/test/stores.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/test/stores.integration.test.ts): never refuses from memory past the window's end, however long the refused write waited
- [`apps/rail402/test/public-hosts.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/public-hosts.test.ts): decides the key first in a process that serves pubnet alone, through its one database limiter
- [`apps/rail402/test/public-hosts.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/public-hosts.test.ts): without the Bazaar, limits a discovery path like any other: no anonymous write for pubnet
- [`apps/rail402/test/pubnet-pool.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/pubnet-pool.integration.test.ts): answers /metrics from memory, so anonymous scrapes take no database connection
- [`apps/rail402/test/app.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/app.test.ts): is counted in the background, never by a scrape, keeping the last value when a count fails
- [`packages/facilitator/test/reconcile-isolation.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/reconcile-isolation.test.ts): still reconciles pubnet when testnet's ledger fails
- [`packages/facilitator/test/reconcile-isolation.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/reconcile-isolation.test.ts): reconciles pubnet on its own while testnet's round hangs
- [`apps/rail402/test/cached-probe.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/cached-probe.test.ts): runs once for any number of concurrent requests and answers from its result for the TTL

**Residual risk.** The database server itself is shared; an outage or saturation of the server stops both networks (O3). On a host not listed in PUBLIC_HOSTS the network is known only from the body, so a pubnet key holder's service-wide limit there stays on the shared pool: serve pubnet callers through the pubnet host. /usage and cataloging a pubnet settlement's discovery metadata read the shared pool (the one pool when pubnet is served alone; there, /usage with a key pubnet does not admit, such as a discovery-internal key, is limited per process only), so while it is saturated /usage fails and a pubnet /settle that carries discovery metadata answers after at most the 2-second catalog budget (a /verify preview after 250 ms), reporting the cataloging as processing. The process cannot know how many replicas run, so a role connection limit that holds one replica's pools but not two is only logged (replicas: 1), not refused.

### N4. One network's failure marks the other unready

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB9 (Testnet and pubnet inside one service) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** /ready is all-or-nothing, so an underfunded pubnet sponsor, a pubnet RPC outage or missing pubnet channels pull the whole service out of a load balancer, and the reverse.

**What Stellar makes different.** n/a.

**Preconditions.** Any per-network fault. **Impact.** Both networks taken out of service.

**Mitigations**

- Readiness per network (/ready/testnet, /ready/pubnet) and a per-network serving gate that refuses only that network's /verify and /settle with a coded 503; the aggregate /ready gates on no network when several are served. ([`apps/rail402/src/network-state.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/network-state.ts), `NetworkGate`)
- The aggregate /ready checks the database through a connection of its own that gives up after DATABASE_ACQUIRE_TIMEOUT_MS, so neither network's load on the shared pool or on pubnet's fails it, while a database that refuses the connection or stops answering does; the role's connection limit is sized so that connection always has a slot. ([`apps/rail402/src/runtime.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/runtime.ts), `readinessProbe`)
- Chain checks at startup run per network, concurrently, and hold instead of exiting when several networks are served; whatever a failing RPC throws (an error, a JSON-RPC error object, a string or nothing), it holds only that network with a code, in either mode, and only a check the chain contradicts stops a single-network process. ([`apps/rail402/src/runtime.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/runtime.ts), `contradictionOf`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/startup-hold.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/startup-hold.test.ts): calls only an RPC failure unreachable: an RPC error, a hang, or something that is not an Error
- [`apps/rail402/test/startup-failures.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/startup-failures.integration.test.ts): holds a network whose sponsor does not exist as sponsor_missing, not as an unreachable RPC
- [`apps/rail402/test/network-isolation.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/network-isolation.integration.test.ts): refuses pubnet settlements, not testnet ones, while the pubnet RPC is down
- [`apps/rail402/test/network-isolation.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/network-isolation.integration.test.ts): serves pubnet once its RPC answers again, without a restart
- [`apps/rail402/test/network-gate.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/network-gate.test.ts): reports readiness per network and keeps /ready process-wide
- [`apps/rail402/test/pubnet-pool.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/pubnet-pool.integration.test.ts): stays ready and admits a pubnet key holder at once while keyless testnet traffic holds the shared pool
- [`apps/rail402/test/runtime.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/runtime.integration.test.ts): fails the database check within the acquire timeout once its connection stops answering
- [`apps/rail402/test/startup-failures.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/startup-failures.integration.test.ts): holds pubnet only when an RPC call itself throws %s
- [`apps/rail402/test/main.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/main.integration.test.ts): starts with its network held, not exiting, when the RPC makes startup throw %s

**Residual risk.** None.

### N5. Testnet listings use up pubnet's cataloging quota

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB9 (Testnet and pubnet inside one service) · asset: Catalog and search integrity

**Threat.** A catalog-wide new-listing quota or origin-check queue would serve listings of both networks together, so free testnet settlements could block or delay pubnet sellers from being cataloged, or a facts refresh queue shared by both networks would let slow testnet hosts delay pubnet trust.

**What Stellar makes different.** Testnet payments are free; pubnet payments cost real money.

**Preconditions.** A flood of testnet settlements with bazaar extensions. **Impact.** Without protection: pubnet sellers refused with bazaar_rate_limited, or left pending behind a testnet origin-check backlog.

**Mitigations**

- Every catalog budget (global, per owner, per payer, per origin host) is kept per network, with stricter pubnet defaults that testnet settings never loosen. ([`packages/bazaar/src/policy.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/policy.ts), `catalogBudgetFor`)
- Origin checks are claimed and run in one lane per network, a bounded number at a time; a network whose lane is busy is skipped, so a testnet backlog never delays pubnet listings. ([`packages/bazaar/src/catalog.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/catalog.ts), `checkOrigins`)
- The network-wide hourly quota counts only listings published now, so pending listings from dust payments never block another seller; Stellar facts and domain trust are refreshed in one lane per network. ([`packages/bazaar/src/catalog.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/catalog.ts), `enrichNetwork`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts): does not let testnet listings use the pubnet new-listing quota
- [`packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts): applies pubnet's stricter budget unless pubnet is configured, whatever the catalog-wide settings
- [`packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts): checks each network's origins in its own lane, so a testnet backlog of unanswering hosts never delays pubnet
- [`packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts): never lets unconfirmed listings use up the network-wide quota, so dust payments cannot block a seller
- [`packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts): refreshes each network's facts in its own lane, so slow testnet hosts never delay pubnet trust

**Residual risk.** A testnet backlog delays other testnet listings only.

### N6. Public metrics and health reveal pubnet budget state

Mitigated · Information disclosure · boundary TB9 (Testnet and pubnet inside one service) · asset: Sponsor XLM

**Threat.** /metrics is public for testnet's published report, and one process exposes pubnet series too: sponsor balance, budget refusals, per-key counters; readiness shows the balance.

**What Stellar makes different.** Sponsor balances are public on chain; budget and refusal timing are not.

**Preconditions.** Anyone reading /metrics or /health. **Impact.** An attacker times a drain and watches its effect.

**Mitigations**

- The public /metrics keeps only allow-listed pubnet series and no series labelled with an access key; /metrics/private and /health/private need a token. ([`apps/rail402/src/metrics-exposure.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/metrics-exposure.ts), `PUBLIC_PUBNET_METRICS`)
- Pubnet series on the public /metrics lose their reason label (series that differ only in it are added up), so a budget or limit refusal looks like any other failure; the private view keeps it. ([`apps/rail402/src/metrics-exposure.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/metrics-exposure.ts), `publicLabels`)
- Public /health shows whether the pubnet sponsor is funded, never its balance or budget figures, and leaves out pubnet's sponsor_budget_high warning; pubnet readiness details carry no balance. ([`apps/rail402/src/health.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/health.ts), `publicHealth`)
- The public /health shows no access-key counts or reload state, and its reload warning names no error. ([`apps/rail402/src/health.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/health.ts), `access: _access`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/metrics-exposure.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/metrics-exposure.test.ts): serves no pubnet series on the public metrics endpoint outside the allow-list
- [`apps/rail402/test/metrics-exposure.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/metrics-exposure.test.ts): cannot tell pubnet budget exhaustion from any other failure on the public metrics endpoint
- [`apps/rail402/test/health.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/health.test.ts): warns at 80 % of the hourly fee budget, never in the public view of pubnet
- [`apps/rail402/test/network-gate.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/network-gate.test.ts): omits the pubnet sponsor balance from public health and shows funded instead
- [`apps/rail402/test/metrics-exposure.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/metrics-exposure.test.ts): requires the private token for /metrics/private and /health/private
- [`apps/rail402/test/health.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/health.test.ts): keeps access keys out of the public view: no counts, no reload state, no error code

**Residual risk.** Sponsor balances are public on chain anyway, and public failure counts still rise during a drain, indistinguishable from other failures; a caller sees the refusal codes of its own requests. Budget figures, budget warnings and refusal reasons are private.

### N7. A testnet-path flaw reaches the pubnet seed in the same process

Accepted risk · Information disclosure, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB9 (Testnet and pubnet inside one service) · asset: Secrets

**Threat.** The process that holds the pubnet sponsor seed also serves the keyless public testnet, makes outbound origin checks, parses seller schemas and runs the embedding model; a code-execution or memory-disclosure bug on any of those paths reads the seed.

**What Stellar makes different.** n/a.

**Preconditions.** A code-execution or disclosure bug. **Impact.** The pubnet seed exposed.

**Mitigations**

- With pubnet enabled every secret comes from a file read once at start, never the environment, and never appears in configuration digests, health or errors. ([`apps/rail402/src/secrets.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/secrets.ts), `readSecretFile`)
- The sponsor's signer set keeps a cold signer above the hot key, so a leaked seed spends only the hot balance. ([`packages/facilitator/src/signers.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/signers.ts), `assertSignerSet`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/secrets.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/secrets.test.ts): refuses a pubnet sponsor secret from the environment
- [`apps/rail402/test/secrets.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/secrets.test.ts): never echoes a file secret in a startup error

**Residual risk.** Accepted: a process compromise still exposes the pubnet seed; the bound is the hot balance plus in-flight upto caps. A dedicated pubnet process or host is the next step if needed.

### N8. A superuser database role shared by both networks

Mitigated · Elevation of privilege, Tampering · boundary TB4 (Facilitator and its state) · asset: Secrets

**Threat.** The service connects as the Postgres superuser; a SQL injection or a compromised dependency on the keyless testnet path can rewrite the pubnet settlement ledger, keys and listings, or run COPY … TO PROGRAM for code execution in the database container.

**What Stellar makes different.** The settlement ledger decides whether a recorded Stellar transaction is answered as settled; rewriting it can make a seller serve unpaid requests or misreport pubnet payments.

**Preconditions.** A SQL injection, or code execution in the service process. **Impact.** Tampered settlement and catalog records for both networks, and code execution in the database container.

**Mitigations**

- With pubnet enabled the service and check-config refuse a superuser or role-creating connection, a member of such a role or of the server-file and server-program roles, and a role that owns the schema or its tables. ([`packages/store-postgres/src/roles.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/src/roles.ts), `assertRuntimeRole`)
- Pubnet never applies migrations through the service's connection, which would make it the tables' owner; they run as the schema owner through the migrate command. ([`apps/rail402/src/database-checks.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/database-checks.ts), `config_pubnet_migration_role_missing`)
- Separate owner and runtime roles: migrations run as the owner; the runtime role reads and writes rows and cannot create, alter, drop or truncate tables, create roles or run server programs; the database and the maintenance databases are revoked from PUBLIC. ([`deploy/postgres/roles.sql`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/postgres/roles.sql), `REVOKE CONNECT ON DATABASE postgres FROM PUBLIC`)
- The runtime role's privileges are re-applied after every migration and restore; the migration bookkeeping is read-only to it and the listing history append-only. ([`deploy/postgres/grants.sql`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/postgres/grants.sql), `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE ON public.listing_versions`)
- A logical restore creates every object as the owner role and re-applies the grants, so a restore never leaves the tables owned by the superuser. ([`deploy/postgres/restore-dump.sh`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/postgres/restore-dump.sh), `SET ROLE :"owner"`)
- With pubnet the service refuses DATABASE_MIGRATION_URL: the process that holds the sponsor seed never holds the schema owner's credentials; the migrate command reads them on its own. ([`apps/rail402/src/config.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/config.ts), `config_pubnet_migration_url_in_service`)
- A deploy checks a release that adds a migration before touching the database with CHECK_CONFIG_PENDING_OK=true, which skips only the pending-migration refusals and still refuses a privileged or owning role; the migrations then run as the schema owner, never through the service's role. ([`apps/rail402/src/database-checks.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/database-checks.ts), `pendingOk`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/conformance/test/roles.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/roles.integration.test.ts): the runtime role cannot create tables, run server programs or rewrite history
- [`tools/conformance/test/roles.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/roles.integration.test.ts): migrates the schema as the owner role, which is not a superuser
- [`tools/conformance/test/roles.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/roles.integration.test.ts): keeps other roles out of the database, and the service's roles out of the maintenance databases
- [`tools/conformance/test/roles.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/roles.integration.test.ts): restores a dump with the owner keeping every table and the runtime role its privileges
- [`tools/conformance/test/restore-drill.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/restore-drill.integration.test.ts): records a restore drill with row counts and checksums
- [`packages/store-postgres/test/roles.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/test/roles.integration.test.ts): refuses to start as a superuser on pubnet
- [`packages/store-postgres/test/roles.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/test/roles.integration.test.ts): refuses a role that is a member of a privileged role or owns the schema's tables
- [`packages/store-postgres/test/roles.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/test/roles.integration.test.ts): the runtime role cannot create tables or run server programs
- [`apps/rail402/test/check-config.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/check-config.integration.test.ts): refuses to run as a superuser when pubnet is enabled
- [`apps/rail402/test/database-roles.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/database-roles.integration.test.ts): never migrates through the service's role, and refuses a role that owns the tables
- [`apps/rail402/test/secrets.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/secrets.test.ts): refuses the schema owner's connection in the service process when pubnet is enabled
- [`apps/rail402/test/database-roles.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/database-roles.integration.test.ts): lets the preflight expect pending migrations, but never skips the role check

**Residual risk.** The runtime role can still write both networks' rows; rows are separated by their network column.

### N9. A setting tuned for testnet weakens pubnet

Mitigated · Tampering, Denial of service · boundary TB9 (Testnet and pubnet inside one service) · asset: Sponsor XLM

**Threat.** Process-wide settings tuned for a public testnet silently apply to pubnet, or a testnet default (auto-provisioned channels, on-chain zeros, USDC by default) is assumed on pubnet.

**What Stellar makes different.** n/a.

**Preconditions.** An operator enabling pubnet. **Impact.** Pubnet runs with limits meant for a free network.

**Mitigations**

- Every money- or abuse-relevant setting has a pubnet rule, pubnet refuses to start when a required one is missing, and startup logs a digest of each network's effective settings. ([`apps/rail402/src/config.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/config.ts), `networkSettingsDigest`)
- An in-process facilitator on pubnet refuses to start without a sponsor guard, explicit fee ceilings, an inclusion floor of at least 200 stroops and explicit accepted assets, each with a stable code; testnet's USDC default never applies to pubnet. ([`packages/facilitator/src/facilitator.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/facilitator.ts), `config_pubnet_assets_missing`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts): requires explicit, safe mainnet settings
- [`apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts): keeps testnet defaults out of the pubnet configuration
- [`apps/rail402/test/check-config.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/check-config.test.ts): exits 78 with the code the service would stop with
- [`packages/facilitator/test/pubnet-setup.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/pubnet-setup.test.ts): refuses pubnet without explicit accepted assets, where testnet defaults to USDC
- [`apps/rail402/test/startup-codes.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/startup-codes.test.ts): documents every code the in-process facilitator refuses a setup with, and nothing else

**Residual risk.** None.

### N10. Restarts for one network interrupt the other

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB9 (Testnet and pubnet inside one service) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** A deploy for a testnet change, an out-of-memory kill or a crash on a testnet path restarts the one process and drops pubnet requests in flight.

**What Stellar makes different.** A settlement in flight when its process stops is finished from its recorded envelope, but the seller waits; during the gap no new payment can be verified on either network.

**Preconditions.** A deploy, a crash, or a resource limit hit by load on either network. **Impact.** Both networks stop answering until the process is back.

**Mitigations**

- Two replicas behind a proxy that routes only to a replica whose /ready answers; deploys restart them one at a time. ([`deploy/proxy/Caddyfile.example`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/proxy/Caddyfile.example), `health_uri /ready`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/conformance/test/proxy.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/proxy.integration.test.ts): serves every request while one replica restarts

**Residual risk.** A crash loop on both replicas stops both networks.

### N11. A testnet discovery flood sheds pubnet discovery

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB9 (Testnet and pubnet inside one service) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** Keyless testnet discovery requests from many addresses fill the discovery admission slots and queue, or every connection of the discovery pool, so pubnet discovery requests are refused with 503 discovery_overloaded.

**What Stellar makes different.** n/a.

**Preconditions.** One service serving both networks; enough parallel slow discovery requests, such as uncached searches or seller usage aggregates. **Impact.** Without protection: the pubnet Bazaar's read surface unavailable while testnet is flooded.

**Mitigations**

- Discovery admits in two lanes with their own gates: requests scoped to pubnet (network parameter or a pubnet host) and everything else; a full shared lane never refuses a pubnet-scoped request. ([`apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts), `export class DiscoveryAdmissions`)
- With pubnet served next to another network, pubnet-scoped discovery reads the catalog and keeps its per-address and identity limits through a discovery pool of its own. ([`apps/rail402/src/runtime.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/runtime.ts), `pubnetDiscoveryDb`)
- Stores and limiters pick the lane's connections from the request's lane, and background work keeps the shared pool. ([`apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts), `export function laneRouted`)
- Search index builds, which searches of both lanes join, and the background index refresh read the catalog through a pool of their own that no request takes. ([`apps/rail402/src/database-pools.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/database-pools.ts), `SEARCH_INDEX_POOL_SIZE`)
- A search whose index build cannot read the catalog answers from the previous index instead of failing. ([`packages/search/src/service.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/src/service.ts), `private async recent`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.test.ts): keeps admitting pubnet discovery while testnet discovery fills every slot of its lane
- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.test.ts): reads a pubnet request through the pubnet lane's store while the shared one is busy
- [`apps/rail402/test/pubnet-pool.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/pubnet-pool.integration.test.ts): serves pubnet-scoped discovery through its own pool while the shared discovery pool is saturated
- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.test.ts): answers a pubnet search while the shared lane's store has no connection for an index build
- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.integration.test.ts): keeps the search index current while every discovery connection is held
- [`packages/search/test/search.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/test/search.test.ts): builds the index, and reads a refresh's revision, through the index store only
- [`packages/search/test/search.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/test/search.test.ts): answers from the previous snapshot while an index build cannot read the catalog

**Residual risk.** Discovery routes without a network parameter (listing detail, versions) share the testnet lane unless asked on a pubnet host; a flood above the process's raw capacity still degrades both networks (N1).

{/* generated:end */}

## Access keys and quotas

Pubnet callers present an API key with its own rate and sponsor budget: what a leaked, shared or
misused key, or a failing limiter, could do.

{/* generated:begin topic-access-keys */}

### Q1. The rate limiter fails open

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB4 (Facilitator and its state) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** A database error, slow query or exhausted pool makes the limiter throw; requests are then served without any limit, exactly when the service is under load.

**What Stellar makes different.** n/a.

**Preconditions.** A limiter outage. **Impact.** Unlimited requests reach verification and settlement.

**Mitigations**

- On pubnet the per-network limiter fails closed: requests are refused with a retryable 503 while it cannot answer. ([`apps/rail402/src/app.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/app.ts), `Rate limiting is unavailable`)
- The address limiter falls back to a per-process bucket while the shared limiter is down, so no route goes unlimited. ([`apps/rail402/src/app.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/app.ts), `fallbackLimiter`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts): refuses pubnet settlements with a retryable code while the rate limiter is unavailable
- [`apps/rail402/test/app.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/app.test.ts): falls back to a local bucket for testnet while the shared limiter is unavailable

**Residual risk.** During a limiter outage pubnet settlement stops; that is intended.

### Q2. An API key is read from logs or reused by someone else

Mitigated · Spoofing, Information disclosure · boundary TB17 (The host and co-located services) · asset: Access keys and quotas

**Threat.** A key sent in X-API-Key is written to an access log, an application log or a shared example, and a third party settles with it, spending its holder's budget.

**What Stellar makes different.** n/a.

**Preconditions.** A key in a log or a leaked file. **Impact.** Settlements and fees charged to the key's holder.

**Mitigations**

- The application logger redacts X-API-Key and Authorization headers; refusals never echo a key. ([`apps/rail402/src/logger.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/logger.ts), `x-api-key`)
- Keys expire and are revocable without a restart: the key file is re-read when it changes, and a broken file keeps the last good keys. ([`apps/rail402/src/access.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/access.ts), `reload`)
- The operations guide tells operators to keep X-API-Key out of the edge proxy's logs. ([`docs/operations.md`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/docs/operations.md), `X-API-Key`)
- The proxy example deletes X-API-Key and the payment headers (X-Relay-H-Payment-Signature, the Playground relay's, included) in every logger: the sites' access logs, the handler-error logger, and the default logger, which takes Caddy's other entries about a request (a response cut off mid-body, a Host matched only without case or not at all, debug output). ([`deploy/proxy/Caddyfile.example`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/proxy/Caddyfile.example), `log default`)
- Keys can be read from a mounted directory (API_KEYS_DIR): the file is looked up by path each round and compared by inode, size and time, so a revocation renamed into place is seen without a restart; /health/private shows the digest of the file in force and a failed reload raises a critical warning and a counter. ([`apps/rail402/src/config.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/config.ts), `API_KEYS_DIR`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/logger.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/logger.test.ts): redacts X-API-Key and Authorization headers
- [`apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts): refuses a revoked key without a restart, and keeps the last good keys when the file breaks
- [`apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts): follows the table of outcomes for a network that requires keys and one that does not
- [`apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts): sees a file replaced by rename inside its directory, even with the same size and time
- [`apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts): reads keys.json from API_KEYS_DIR, and refuses it together with API_KEYS_FILE
- [`tools/conformance/test/proxy.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/proxy.test.ts): keeps payment headers and API keys out of the log of failed requests
- [`tools/conformance/test/proxy.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/proxy.test.ts): keeps payment headers and API keys out of every log besides the sites' access logs
- [`tools/conformance/test/proxy.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/proxy.integration.test.ts): keeps them out of the log of a request no replica could answer
- [`tools/conformance/test/proxy.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/proxy.integration.test.ts): keeps them out of the log of a response a replica cut off
- [`tools/conformance/test/proxy.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/proxy.integration.test.ts): keeps them out of the log of a request whose Host differs in case
- [`tools/conformance/test/proxy.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/proxy.integration.test.ts): answers a host no site claims with 404 and keeps its credentials out of the log

**Residual risk.** A stolen key can spend its own budget until it is revoked or expires.

### Q3. One key's budget starves the others

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** The sum of per-key budgets exceeds the network budget, so one heavy or compromised key consumes the network budget before other keys reach theirs.

**What Stellar makes different.** n/a.

**Preconditions.** A key with a large budget. **Impact.** Other keys' settlements are refused until the hour moves on.

**Mitigations**

- Admission checks the caller's budget and the network budget atomically with the claim; each key is capped by its own. ([`packages/facilitator/src/guard.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/guard.ts), `refusedByLedger`)
- A /health warning (operator-only for pubnet) and a metric report when the committed fees reach SPONSOR_BUDGET_WARN_PERCENT of the hourly budget. ([`apps/rail402/src/health.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/health.ts), `sponsor_budget_high`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/caller-budget.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/caller-budget.test.ts): stops one key at its hourly sponsor budget while another key keeps settling
- [`packages/facilitator/test/in-process-controls.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/in-process-controls.integration.test.ts): holds a caller to its own hourly sponsor budget and a payer to its settlement rate
- [`apps/rail402/test/health.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/health.test.ts): warns at 80 % of the hourly fee budget, never in the public view of pubnet

**Residual risk.** Keys together can still exhaust the network budget; it is the outer bound by design.

### Q4. A testnet key opens pubnet

Mitigated · Elevation of privilege · boundary TB9 (Testnet and pubnet inside one service) · asset: Access keys and quotas

**Threat.** API keys are one process-wide set, so a key issued for testnet harnesses, CI or a tester also passes the pubnet gate.

**What Stellar makes different.** n/a.

**Preconditions.** Any testnet key. **Impact.** Mainnet settlements and fees under a key never meant for them.

**Mitigations**

- Each key record names the networks it may use; a request with a key not scoped to its network is refused with 403 where keys are required. ([`apps/rail402/src/access.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/access.ts), `is not allowed on`)
- API_KEY_SHA256, whose keys are valid on every network, is refused once pubnet is enabled. ([`apps/rail402/src/config.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/config.ts), `config_legacy_api_keys_with_pubnet`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts): refuses a testnet-only key on pubnet and accepts it on testnet
- [`apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts): refuses API_KEY_SHA256 once pubnet is enabled

**Residual risk.** None.

### Q5. Usage and service fees read, inflated or repriced

Mitigated · Information disclosure, Repudiation, Tampering · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Access keys and quotas

**Threat.** A key holder reads another key's usage through /usage, or a price change is applied retroactively to past settlements, or usage is lost so a charge cannot be justified.

**What Stellar makes different.** n/a.

**Preconditions.** A key; or an operator changing the price. **Impact.** Wrong or unjustifiable charges.

**Mitigations**

- The service-fee price is recorded on each settlement when it is claimed; /usage and the export sum the recorded prices. ([`packages/store-postgres/migrations/0016_settlement_service_fee.sql`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/migrations/0016_settlement_service_fee.sql), `service_fee_usd`)
- /usage answers only for the presented key's own subject; the export recomputes usage from the settlement ledger. ([`apps/rail402/src/usage-export.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/usage-export.ts), `exportUsage`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/metering.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/metering.test.ts): keeps the price recorded at settlement after the price changes
- [`apps/rail402/test/metering.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/metering.test.ts): shows a key only its own usage
- [`apps/rail402/test/metering.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/metering.integration.test.ts): exports usage per caller, network and day, never billing what Rail402 did not land

**Residual risk.** None.

### Q6. The internal discovery identity is used to pay or to dodge limits

Mitigated · Elevation of privilege, Denial of service · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** The key a hosted discovery front end uses to call the discovery routes with a large bucket is stolen or misused: to call /verify and /settle, to search without limits, or to charge its traffic to other users by forging the forwarded client header.

**What Stellar makes different.** n/a.

**Preconditions.** A leaked discovery-internal key, or any caller sending X-Rail402-Client. **Impact.** Payments by an identity never meant to pay; discovery load beyond the per-address limits.

**Mitigations**

- A discovery-internal key names no network and is refused on /verify and /settle everywhere. ([`apps/rail402/src/access.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/access.ts), `This key may call the discovery routes only`)
- It draws on its own bounded bucket, and the forwarded client header is trusted only from it, each client with its own limit. ([`apps/rail402/src/app.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/app.ts), `discoveryWait`)
- The discovery cost bucket honours the identity too: a request is charged to its forwarded client's bucket and to the identity's, never to the identity's address. ([`apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts), `limits.charges`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts): never reaches verify or settle
- [`apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts): draws on its own bucket, per forwarded client, instead of the per-address limit
- [`apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts): charges discovery costs to each forwarded client and to the identity, never to the identity's address

**Residual risk.** A leaked key spends its own discovery bucket until revoked; discovery moves no money.

{/* generated:end */}

## Key custody

How the pubnet sponsor's keys are made, stored, controlled and funded.

{/* generated:begin topic-key-custody */}

### K1. The sponsor seed is visible in the environment

Mitigated · Information disclosure · boundary TB10 (Operator key custody) · asset: Secrets

**Threat.** An environment-variable seed is readable through container inspection by anyone with access to the container runtime, through the process environment by root, and in configuration dumps.

**What Stellar makes different.** n/a.

**Preconditions.** Access to the host's container runtime. **Impact.** The pubnet sponsor seed exposed.

**Mitigations**

- Every secret can come from a file; with pubnet enabled the environment form is refused, files writable by group or others are refused, and the pubnet sponsor seed files must be readable by their owner only (0400 or 0600; group-readable is refused). ([`apps/rail402/src/secrets.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/secrets.ts), `readSecretFile`)
- With pubnet, database URL and cursor secret files readable by others are refused, and pubnet RPC URLs that carry credentials must come from a file. ([`apps/rail402/src/config.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/config.ts), `refuseCredentialsInEnv`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/secrets.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/secrets.test.ts): refuses a pubnet sponsor secret from the environment
- [`apps/rail402/test/secrets.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/secrets.test.ts): refuses a secret file readable by group or others
- [`apps/rail402/test/secrets.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/secrets.test.ts): refuses database and cursor secret files readable by others when pubnet is enabled
- [`apps/rail402/test/secrets.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/secrets.test.ts): refuses pubnet RPC URLs with credentials in the environment, and takes them from the file

**Residual risk.** Root on the host can still read the file or the process memory.

### K2. One leaked seed controls the sponsor, every channel and the authority

Mitigated · Elevation of privilege · boundary TB10 (Operator key custody) · asset: Sponsor XLM

**Threat.** Channels and the upto authority derive from the sponsor seed, so a leak gives control of all of them, and a single-signer sponsor lets the attacker change signers or merge the account.

**What Stellar makes different.** Fee bumps need only the low threshold; create-account, payment and invoke need medium; signer changes and account merge need high.

**Preconditions.** A leaked hot seed. **Impact.** The sponsor account taken over.

**Mitigations**

- Pubnet startup reads the sponsor's signers and thresholds and refuses (or holds pubnet) unless the hot key meets medium but not high and the configured cold signer (PUBNET_SPONSOR_COLD_SIGNER) alone reaches high. ([`packages/facilitator/src/signers.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/signers.ts), `assertSignerSet`)
- The cold signer must be the only way to high: keys derived from the sponsor seed (channels, the upto authority) may not be signers, and the hot key with every other signer, pre-authorized transaction, hash-x and signed-payload signers included, stays below high. ([`packages/facilitator/src/signers.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/src/signers.ts), `derived from the sponsor seed`)
- Pubnet refuses to start without PUBNET_SPONSOR_COLD_SIGNER while the signer check is on, and refuses the sponsor's own key as the cold signer. ([`apps/rail402/src/config.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/config.ts), `config_pubnet_cold_signer_missing`)
- The leak response starts with the cold key: a reviewed, unsigned transaction from the cold account (its source and fee payer) sets the hot key's weight to 0 on the sponsor (high threshold, cold key only), so the leaked key cannot block it through the sponsor's sequence; a separate cold-signed sweep follows. The testnet rehearsal and the local drill bump the sponsor's sequence with the hot key first and show the freeze applying and the hot key refused afterwards. ([`deploy/keys/ceremony.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/keys/ceremony.ts), `buildFreeze`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/facilitator/test/signers.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/signers.integration.test.ts): refuses a pubnet sponsor whose own key meets the high threshold
- [`packages/facilitator/test/signers.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/signers.integration.test.ts): reads hash-x signers and refuses them as the way to reach high
- [`packages/facilitator/test/signers.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/signers.test.ts): accepts a hot key that meets medium but not high, with a cold signer reaching high
- [`packages/facilitator/test/signers.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/signers.test.ts): never lets a derived key or a hash-x signer stand in for the cold signer
- [`packages/facilitator/test/signers.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/facilitator/test/signers.test.ts): refuses a configured cold signer that is the sponsor key or derived from its seed
- [`apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts): requires the pubnet sponsor's cold signer for the signer check, never the sponsor key itself
- [`tools/conformance/test/ceremony.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/ceremony.test.ts): freezes from the cold account, so the leaked key cannot block it through the sponsor's sequence
- [`tools/conformance/test/ceremony.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/ceremony.test.ts): sweeps in a separate cold-sourced transaction after the freeze

**Residual risk.** A leaked hot seed spends the hot balance and can settle in-flight upto payloads up to their caps until the cold key's freeze applies.

### K3. A testnet seed reused on mainnet

Mitigated · Information disclosure, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB10 (Operator key custody) · asset: Secrets

**Threat.** An operator uses a seed that was handled with testnet care (env files, fixtures, CI) as the pubnet sponsor.

**What Stellar makes different.** n/a.

**Preconditions.** Seed files mixed up. **Impact.** The mainnet sponsor protected only as well as a test key.

**Mitigations**

- A pubnet sponsor key file must carry the pubnet network tag, and a file tagged for another network is refused on any network. ([`apps/rail402/src/secrets.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/secrets.ts), `sponsorSeedFrom`)
- One process refuses the same sponsor for two networks. ([`apps/rail402/src/config.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/config.ts), `each network needs its own sponsor account`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/secrets.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/secrets.test.ts): refuses a pubnet secret file tagged for testnet, or not tagged at all
- [`apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/config.test.ts): refuses one sponsor for two networks

**Residual risk.** An operator who copies a seed across tags by hand defeats the check.

### K4. The cold treasury is misused or a top-up is misdirected

Accepted risk · Spoofing, Tampering · boundary TB10 (Operator key custody) · asset: Cold treasury

**Threat.** A mistyped or substituted destination, a compromised workstation preparing the transaction, or a lost hardware wallet; the cold key also signs threshold changes.

**What Stellar makes different.** The cold key is the sponsor's high-weight signer: it alone can change signers or merge the account, and a payment it signs to a wrong address is final.

**Preconditions.** Operator error, a phished address or a compromised workstation. **Impact.** Treasury XLM sent to the wrong account, or the sponsor's signer set changed.

**Mitigations**

- Top-ups are built only to the sponsor address recorded at creation and come with a review of network, source, operations and transaction hash to compare on the device. ([`deploy/keys/ceremony.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/keys/ceremony.ts), `recordedSponsor`)
- The signer setup is reviewed the same way, and check-signers confirms that only the cold key meets the high threshold. ([`deploy/keys/ceremony.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/keys/ceremony.ts), `signerSetProblems`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/conformance/test/ceremony.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/ceremony.test.ts): records a cold-to-hot top-up with its reviewed summary
- [`tools/conformance/test/ceremony.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/ceremony.test.ts): refuses a destination that is not the recorded sponsor, and bad amounts
- [`tools/conformance/test/ceremony.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/ceremony.test.ts): refuses a pubnet sponsor whose own key meets the high threshold

**Residual risk.** Operator error on the hardware wallet remains possible; amounts are small by design.

### K5. Database backups leak, are altered or cannot be restored

Mitigated · Information disclosure, Tampering, Denial of service · boundary TB10 (Operator key custody) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** Off-host copies of the database carry key digests, settlement and usage records; a backup readable or writable by the wrong party leaks data or plants records on restore, and a backup that was never restored may not restore at all.

**What Stellar makes different.** The settlement ledger holds the recorded envelopes the reconciler needs to finish settlements after a loss; a database restored from a good point is safe because a payment already on chain can never settle again (its nonce is spent).

**Preconditions.** Access to the backup storage or its credentials, or a host loss. **Impact.** Disclosure of usage and key digests, tampered records after a restore, or a database that cannot be recovered.

**Mitigations**

- Continuous WAL archiving with pgBackRest, encrypted with AES-256 before upload; the repository key and passphrase reach pgBackRest as a file secret, never as environment variables. The restore drill finds a marker row in the data files and nowhere in the repository. ([`deploy/postgres/restore-drill.sh`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/postgres/restore-drill.sh), `plaintextMarker`)
- Off-host copies are kept in two tiers that fit pgBackRest's rewrites and expiry: versioning with a host key that cannot delete versions, and object lock on the versions. ([`deploy/postgres/pgbackrest.conf.example`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/postgres/pgbackrest.conf.example), `Immutability, in two tiers`)
- A restore drill restores to a point in time, compares every table's row count and checksum with the target, and checks that the runtime role still works. ([`deploy/postgres/restore-drill.sh`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/postgres/restore-drill.sh), `fingerprint`)
- Archiving failures, a growing archive backlog, a stale backup and a filling disk page the on-call. ([`deploy/monitoring/alerts.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/monitoring/alerts.ts), `Rail402WalArchiveFailing`)
- With archiving on for 6 hours and no completed backup listed (none taken, or the repository unreadable), the stale-backup alert pages too. ([`deploy/monitoring/alerts.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/monitoring/alerts.ts), `firstBackupGraceHours`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/conformance/test/restore-drill.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/restore-drill.integration.test.ts): records a restore drill with row counts and checksums
- [`tools/conformance/test/monitoring.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/monitoring.test.ts): watches WAL archiving, backups and host disk space, and pages on each
- [`deploy/monitoring/alerts.test.yml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/monitoring/alerts.test.yml): Rail402WalArchiveFailing
- [`deploy/monitoring/alerts.test.yml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/monitoring/alerts.test.yml): Rail402BackupStale

**Residual risk.** Whoever holds the repository passphrase can read the backups. The drill uses a local repository: the off-host store and its locked retention must be exercised with the same steps before relying on them.

### K6. The host's administrator path or DNS automation token is abused

Accepted risk · Elevation of privilege, Spoofing · boundary TB17 (The host and co-located services) · asset: Secrets

**Threat.** A container escape from a co-located service, the host administrator path, or a DNS automation token that can edit every record lets an attacker read the pubnet seed or point the facilitator's host name at an impostor.

**What Stellar makes different.** A hot seed spends the sponsor's balance; an impostor facilitator can answer /supported with its own signers and refuse or misreport payments, but cannot move a payer's funds beyond what the payer signed.

**Preconditions.** Root on the host, or the DNS automation token. **Impact.** Loss of the hot balance, or buyers and sellers talking to an impostor.

**Mitigations**

- The pubnet seed is a file secret readable only by the service user, and the cold key keeps sole control of the sponsor account, so a stolen hot seed cannot change signers. ([`deploy/keys/ceremony.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/keys/ceremony.ts), `SIGNER_PLAN`)

**Tests.** None: this is a documented limit, not a behaviour Rail402 implements.

**Residual risk.** Accepted while services share a host: root on the host can read the process memory, and the DNS token must be scoped and given an expiry by the operator; a dedicated host is the upgrade.

### K7. The sponsor seed handled on a networked machine during setup

Mitigated · Information disclosure · boundary TB10 (Operator key custody) · asset: Secrets

**Threat.** The one-time signer setup is signed with the hot seed; doing that on a networked workstation with the full dependency tree lets a compromised dependency capture the seed before the account has its cold signer.

**What Stellar makes different.** Until the setup applies, the seed is the account's only signer and meets every threshold, so whoever holds it can take the account over.

**Preconditions.** A compromised development dependency or workstation at setup time. **Impact.** The new sponsor account taken over before the cold signer exists.

**Mitigations**

- The setup is built unsigned on the networked machine (optionally with the sequence given, without any network) and signed on the offline machine by the zero-dependency key tool, which refuses a key tagged for another network and an envelope that is already signed; only the signed XDR crosses back. ([`deploy/stellar-keys.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/stellar-keys.ts), `signEnvelope`)
- The unsigned setup and its review come from the ceremony tool without the seed. ([`deploy/keys/cli.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/keys/cli.ts), `--unsigned`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/conformance/test/ceremony.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/ceremony.test.ts): signs an unsigned envelope without the SDK exactly as the SDK does

**Residual risk.** The offline machine and the copy of the key tool carried to it must be checked as the ceremony describes.

{/* generated:end */}

## Releases and deploys

How code, images and packages get from the repository to production and to buyers: what a stolen
credential, a careless deploy or a compromised dependency could ship.

{/* generated:begin topic-supply-chain */}

### C1. A release published without passing CI

Mitigated · Tampering · boundary TB15 (Source, release and deploy) · asset: Release integrity

**Threat.** A version tag publishes a container image built from a commit that never passed the checks, or from a commit outside main, with nothing that proves how the image was built.

**What Stellar makes different.** The image holds the code that sponsors fees from a funded Stellar account and decides which authorizations it submits; a broken or altered build moves the operator's XLM and can misreport settlements.

**Preconditions.** Push access to the repository, or a tag pushed by mistake on an untested commit. **Impact.** An untested or unreviewed image reaches the registry under a release version.

**Mitigations**

- The release workflow builds the image only after a verify job runs the full checks on the tagged commit and confirms the commit is on main. ([`.github/workflows/release.yml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/.github/workflows/release.yml), `needs: verify`)
