- The published image carries a build provenance attestation and an SBOM, and its digest is written to the job summary. ([`.github/workflows/release.yml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/.github/workflows/release.yml), `actions/attest-build-provenance`)
- The service, the Explorer and the hosted MCP endpoint are released as three images from the same verified commit, each with its own provenance attestation and SBOM, and CI builds every deployable image on every change. ([`.github/workflows/release.yml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/.github/workflows/release.yml), `matrix.image.dockerfile`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/conformance/test/workflows.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/workflows.test.ts): release runs only after CI passes and attests provenance
- [`tools/conformance/test/workflows.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/workflows.test.ts): grants write permissions only to the job that publishes
- [`tools/conformance/test/workflows.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/workflows.test.ts): builds every deployable image in CI and publishes each with its own attestation

**Residual risk.** A maintainer who can change the workflow itself can still weaken it; workflow changes go through review on main.

### C2. Unsigned release tags

Mitigated · Spoofing, Tampering · boundary TB15 (Source, release and deploy) · asset: Release integrity

**Threat.** A stolen push credential creates a release tag that looks like the maintainers' and is deployed.

**What Stellar makes different.** Nothing Stellar-specific: the tag decides which code holds the sponsor seed of a funded account.

**Preconditions.** A leaked push credential or a compromised workstation. **Impact.** An attacker's commit is deployed as a release.

**Mitigations**

- A release tag is deployable only with an SSH signature from a key listed in the committed deploy/release-signers, verified with ssh-keygen against that file alone; git's tag verification is not used, so an OpenPGP or X.509 signature that the operator's keyring would accept is refused. ([`deploy/check-deployable.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/check-deployable.ts), `verifySshTagSignature`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts): refuses an unsigned tag
- [`tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts): refuses a commit that is not a release tag
- [`tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts): checks tag signatures against the committed release signers only
- [`tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts): refuses an OpenPGP-signed tag even when the operator's keyring trusts its key

**Residual risk.** Branch protection and required signatures are repository settings outside the code.

### C3. Production deployed from a local working tree

Mitigated · Tampering · boundary TB15 (Source, release and deploy) · asset: Release integrity

**Threat.** A deploy ships whatever is checked out on the operator's machine: unpushed, unreviewed or CI-red code, or uncommitted edits.

**What Stellar makes different.** Nothing Stellar-specific: the deployed process holds the sponsor seeds of both networks.

**Preconditions.** Operator error, or a compromised operator workstation. **Impact.** Code that never passed review or CI serves payments on mainnet.

**Mitigations**

- A committed gate that the production deploy runs first: HEAD is a signed vX.Y.Z tag on origin/main, the tree is clean, every check run for the commit is green, and images are named by digest, optionally with a verified attestation. ([`deploy/check-deployable.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/check-deployable.ts), `deployProblems`)
- The tag must be on origin as the same tag object, and every image must carry an attestation from the release workflow run for that tag. ([`deploy/check-deployable.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/check-deployable.ts), `--source-ref`)
- A deployment can require every image it runs (facilitator, Explorer, hosted MCP endpoint) to pass the gate, and the gate prints the digest of the deploy kit kept outside the release, which the host recomputes and records with the release. ([`deploy/check-deployable.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/check-deployable.ts), `kitDigest`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts): refuses a commit not on origin/main
- [`tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts): refuses a commit whose CI is not green
- [`tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts): refuses uncommitted changes, images named by tag and unverified attestations
- [`tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts): refuses a tag that is not on origin, or that origin has as another object
- [`tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts): refuses a deploy that leaves out a required image
- [`tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/check-deployable.test.ts): digests a deploy kit the same way a shell on the host does

**Residual risk.** The deploy scripts and the files a deployment keeps outside the release (compose files, host scripts and the service settings, including a pubnet network's fee ceilings, budgets, asset minimums and cold signer) are not reviewed by the gate: it names exactly which kit shipped (its digest), and the service logs a digest of each network's effective settings, so two deploys can be compared.

### C4. A tampered npm package drains agents

Accepted risk · Tampering, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB15 (Source, release and deploy) · asset: Payer funds

**Threat.** The SDK, CLI and MCP server hold buyer keys and enforce spending caps; a package published with a leaked token could ship a signer that ignores the caps.

**What Stellar makes different.** A Stellar buyer key signs Soroban authorization entries directly; a malicious signer can authorize any transfer the key's account can make.

**Preconditions.** A leaked npm publishing token or account. **Impact.** Agents that install the tampered version can lose funds beyond their caps.

**Mitigations**

- Packages are published by hand from the tagged commit, by an npm account with two-factor authentication for publishing, after the full checks and the tarball check. ([`docs/operations.md`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/docs/operations.md), `two-factor authentication`)

**Tests.** None: this is a documented limit, not a behaviour Rail402 implements.

**Residual risk.** Accepted for now: packages carry no npm provenance until publishing moves to CI with trusted publishing; registry compromise is out of scope.

### C5. A vulnerable dependency or a re-tagged base image

Mitigated · Tampering, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB15 (Source, release and deploy) · asset: Secrets

**Threat.** A dependency in the process that holds the sponsor seeds, or a base image whose tag was moved, carries a known vulnerability or malicious code.

**What Stellar makes different.** The facilitator process signs fee bumps with the sponsor seed of a funded account, so code execution there is direct access to XLM.

**Preconditions.** A published advisory in a dependency, or a compromised image registry tag. **Impact.** Known-vulnerable code ships, or the image changes without a commit.

**Mitigations**

- CI scans every lockfile with osv-scanner and the contract crates with cargo-audit; ignored advisories need a reason and an expiry. ([`.github/workflows/ci.yml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/.github/workflows/ci.yml), `osv-scanner`)
- Base images and the Dockerfile syntax image are pinned by digest in every Dockerfile git tracks (an explicit list, with reasons, exempts the standalone example seller and the docs check), and every workflow action by commit. ([`Dockerfile`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/Dockerfile), `@sha256:`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/conformance/test/workflows.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/workflows.test.ts): runs dependency scanners and pins base images by digest
- [`tools/conformance/test/workflows.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/workflows.test.ts): pins every action by commit and gives every ignored advisory a reason and an expiry

**Residual risk.** Zero-day vulnerabilities, and advisories published between scans.

{/* generated:end */}

## Discovery cost and size limits

The Bazaar's public read routes run searches, page through the catalog and keep index snapshots for
cursors: what expensive or oversized requests could cost the shared process.

{/* generated:begin topic-discovery-limits */}

### D1. Expensive searches

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** Unauthenticated searches each run an embedding on the CPU that settlement also needs; a flood of distinct queries, within the service-wide request limit, keeps the model busy.

**What Stellar makes different.** Pubnet settlements share the process, so search load delays real-money payments.

**Preconditions.** Many distinct queries from one client or many clients. **Impact.** Without protection: slow searches and settlements for everyone.

**Mitigations**

- A per-client cost bucket for discovery: an uncached search costs more than a cached one, deep offsets cost more than the first page. ([`apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts), `searchCost`)
- A bounded least-recently-used query-embedding cache, so repeated queries run no model. ([`packages/search/src/service.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/src/service.ts), `queryCacheSize`)
- An embedder concurrency cap: a query that finds the model busy is answered lexical-only, as partial results, instead of queueing. ([`packages/search/src/service.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/src/service.ts), `EmbedGate`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery.test.ts): limits uncached searches more tightly than cached ones
- [`packages/search/test/search.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/test/search.test.ts): serves a repeated query's embedding from a bounded cache, least recently used first
- [`packages/search/test/search.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/test/search.test.ts): answers lexical-only once the embedder is at its concurrency cap

**Residual risk.** Results degrade to lexical under sustained load; each replica keeps its own buckets.

### D2. Search index snapshots exhaust memory

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** Catalog growth on either network, catalog changes and cursor requests make the search index hold more memory than the process has: every listing's content in every snapshot, many snapshots alive for their cursor lifetime, or listings written to maximise the index's size.

**What Stellar makes different.** Testnet catalog churn is free, and the process also serves pubnet.

**Preconditions.** Many listings (free on testnet for originless mcp:// tools), frequent catalog changes, and a search with a cursor after each. **Impact.** Without protection: memory growth until the process is killed, interrupting both networks.

**Mitigations**

- A snapshot keeps only what ranking and filters read; a result page reads its listings from the catalog. The catalog is read in batches with a statement timeout, so a build never holds every listing's content. ([`packages/search/src/service.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/src/service.ts), `function indexed`)
- Each field indexes a bounded number of terms, and each network's listings may take a bounded share of a snapshot (unverified listings at most half), so free testnet listings never push pubnet out of search. ([`packages/search/src/service.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/src/service.ts), `maxIndexBytesPerNetwork`)
- Count and byte caps on kept snapshots, oldest superseded first (their cursors expire), with estimates calibrated against measured heap, and metrics for the snapshots and for listings left out. ([`packages/search/src/service.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/src/service.ts), `maxSnapshots`)
- Each network holds a bounded number of published listings, and listings no origin confirmed (and, where set, idle ones) expire after their last settlement. ([`packages/bazaar/src/catalog.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/catalog.ts), `async expire(`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/search/test/search.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/test/search.test.ts): expires the oldest cursor's snapshot when the snapshot cap is reached
- [`packages/search/test/memory.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/test/memory.test.ts): estimates a snapshot at no less than the heap it holds, for thousands of maximum-size listings
- [`packages/search/test/memory.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/test/memory.test.ts): stays within its memory caps under a flood of free testnet listings, and keeps every pubnet listing searchable
- [`packages/search/test/search.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/test/search.test.ts): shows a result as the store holds it now, and never one withdrawn since its snapshot
- [`packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts): scans the published listings in batches, in catalog order, at the revision read with them
- [`packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts): refuses a new listing while its network holds its maximum of published listings, and publishes none past it
- [`packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts): withdraws an unverified tool after its retention and lists it again on its owner's next settlement
- [`packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts): withdraws listings idle for longer than the network's idle retention, until a settlement relists them

**Residual risk.** Under a flood, the newest listings of the flooded network are left out of search (discovery still lists them) until older ones expire; old cursors expire early under churn.

### D3. Discovery pages too large for stock clients

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** Listings near their per-field caps, or with many payment options, make a page exceed the 4 MiB that upstream's withBazaar client accepts, so every stock client's catalog listing fails, and large pages cost memory.

**What Stellar makes different.** None.

**Preconditions.** A seller publishing large info, schema or many options, through its own 402. **Impact.** Without protection: stock clients cannot read the catalog.

**Mitigations**

- A per-listing published-size cap and at most 8 options per listing, enforced at cataloging and at origin checks. ([`packages/bazaar/src/catalog.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/catalog.ts), `listingBytes`)
- A page byte budget that ends a list or search page early; its limit, or its cursor, continues where it ended. ([`apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts), `itemsWithin`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery.test.ts): ends a page before it exceeds the byte budget and continues on the next
- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery.test.ts): ends a search page within the byte budget, and its cursor continues after it
- [`packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts): refuses a listing larger than the per-listing size limit
- [`packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts): withdraws a listing whose origin describes more than a listing may publish
- [`packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts): publishes at most eight of an origin's payment options
- [`packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts): keeps the settled option of a resource or tool whose origin offers more than eight

**Residual risk.** None.

### D4. Deep offsets and long histories scan the catalog

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB4 (Facilitator and its state) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** /discovery/resources accepts offsets up to 1,000,000, so repeated deep pages make the database scan and sort far; a listing's version history grows with every confirmed change, so reading it whole returns tens of megabytes.

**What Stellar makes different.** None.

**Preconditions.** Many deep-offset requests, or a listing whose owner keeps changing it. **Impact.** Without protection: database load that slows settlement, which shares the database.

**Mitigations**

- Deep offsets cost more of the client's discovery budget. ([`apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts), `offsetCost`)
- A 5-second statement timeout on every list page and every page of version history. ([`packages/store-postgres/src/catalog-store.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/store-postgres/src/catalog-store.ts), `statement_timeout`)
- Version history is read a page at a time (at most 100 versions, ended early at the page byte budget); listings and history pages spend the client's discovery budget, and every cost-limit refusal is counted by route. ([`apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts), `versionsCost`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery.test.ts): charges deep offsets against the cost bucket
- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery.test.ts): charges a listing and its version history, and reports every refusal by route
- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery.test.ts): pages a listing's history oldest first, 100 versions at most, continuing after the last read
- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery.test.ts): ends a page of history before it exceeds the byte budget, and next continues after it
- [`packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/bazaar/src/testing.ts): reads a page of version history after a given version

**Residual risk.** None.

### D5. Discovery beyond capacity starves settlement and health checks

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB2 (Seller and facilitator) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** Discovery requests arriving faster than the process completes them (uncached searches over a large catalog, from many addresses, each within its own cost budget) pile up inside the process: every one waits for a database connection of the pool settlement uses, the event loop falls behind, and /health, /ready and /settle wait behind them; searches fail with 500 after the pool's acquire timeout or hang until the client gives up.

**What Stellar makes different.** One process serves testnet and pubnet: free testnet discovery load must never delay real-money pubnet settlements or take a network out of its load balancer.

**Preconditions.** A request rate above the discovery throughput the process reaches (measured with the load-test package), spread over enough addresses to stay within the per-client limits. **Impact.** Without protection: settlement, health and readiness slow down or time out for every network, and discovery answers with uncoded 500s.

**Mitigations**

- Load shedding ahead of every limit and database read: at most DISCOVERY_MAX_IN_FLIGHT discovery requests run at once and DISCOVERY_QUEUE_LENGTH wait, each at most DISCOVERY_QUEUE_TIMEOUT_MS; the rest are refused at once with 503 discovery_overloaded and Retry-After, counted in rail402_discovery_shed_total. ([`apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts), `class DiscoveryAdmission`)
- Discovery reads the catalog and keeps its per-address and internal-identity limits through a database pool of its own (DISCOVERY_DATABASE_POOL_SIZE connections, DISCOVERY_DATABASE_ACQUIRE_TIMEOUT_MS), so it never holds a connection settlement, limits, metering or readiness need. ([`apps/rail402/src/runtime.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/runtime.ts), `discoveryDb`)
- The search index is built through a pool of its own (SEARCH_INDEX_POOL_SIZE connections, DISCOVERY_DATABASE_ACQUIRE_TIMEOUT_MS) that no request takes, so discovery load never holds a connection an index build needs. ([`apps/rail402/src/database-pools.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/database-pools.ts), `SEARCH_INDEX_POOL_SIZE`)
- A busy or unreachable catalog database is answered with a coded 503 (discovery_overloaded, discovery_catalog_unavailable) and Retry-After, never a 500. ([`apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/discovery.ts), `discoveryFailure`)
- A result page reuses listings read within the revision read interval at the same catalog revision, so repeated results cost no database read. ([`packages/search/src/service.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/src/service.ts), `pageCacheSize`)
- /health never does I/O, /ready/&lt;network&gt; answers from the last poll, and /ready answers from the last database probe while a new one runs. ([`apps/rail402/src/cached-probe.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/cached-probe.ts), `staleWhileRunning`)
- Sustained shedding raises a ticket (Rail402DiscoveryShedding) naming the reason, so refused searches do not go unnoticed. ([`deploy/monitoring/alerts.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/monitoring/alerts.ts), `Rail402DiscoveryShedding`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.test.ts): serves up to its limit, queues up to its queue length and refuses the rest at once
- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.test.ts): refuses discovery beyond its slots and queue with 503 while health and settlement routes answer
- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.test.ts): refuse with a coded 503 and Retry-After, never a 500
- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.test.ts): answers from the last result instead of waiting for a slow probe
- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.integration.test.ts): sheds a discovery flood with coded 503s while settlement, /health and /ready answer
- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.integration.test.ts): refuses discovery with a code while its pool is held, and settles through the other pools
- [`apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/discovery-overload.integration.test.ts): keeps the search index current while every discovery connection is held
- [`packages/search/test/search.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/search/test/search.test.ts): reads a page's listings once per revisionMaxAgeMs at one revision, never past a withdrawal
- [`deploy/monitoring/alerts.test.yml`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/deploy/monitoring/alerts.test.yml): Rail402DiscoveryShedding

**Residual risk.** Beyond capacity, a share of discovery requests is refused with 503 and must be retried; the limits are per replica.

{/* generated:end */}

## The hosted MCP endpoint

A public MCP endpoint offers discovery tools to remote agents, without a signing key: what a client
could make it fetch, flood or carry into an agent.

{/* generated:begin topic-hosted-mcp */}

### H1. The hosted MCP used as a fetch proxy

Mitigated · Information disclosure, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB13 (Hosted MCP and remote agents) · asset: Operator infrastructure

**Threat.** A remote agent asks the hosted endpoint to fetch or quote a URL, reaching internal services from inside the host's network.

**What Stellar makes different.** The endpoint sits next to the facilitator that holds each network's fee sponsor key; a request it could be made to send would come from that network position.

**Preconditions.** A public hosted endpoint. **Impact.** Requests to internal services or metadata endpoints.

**Mitigations**

- Hosted tools only search and read listings through a discovery client whose fetch refuses every origin but the configured Bazaar; no fetch, quote or pay tool is registered. ([`packages/mcp/src/hosted.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/src/hosted.ts), `upstreamFetch`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts): makes no outbound request beyond the catalog
- [`packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts): lists only the read-only discovery tools

**Residual risk.** None.

### H2. The hosted MCP started with a signing key

Mitigated · Elevation of privilege · boundary TB13 (Hosted MCP and remote agents) · asset: Secrets

**Threat.** A deployment mistake gives the public endpoint a buyer key or token, turning it into a public wallet.

**What Stellar makes different.** Any Stellar seed in reach of a public tool would sign authorization entries for anyone who calls it.

**Preconditions.** A misconfigured deployment. **Impact.** Anyone could spend from the key.

**Mitigations**

- Hosted mode refuses to start (exit 78) when RAIL402_SECRET_KEY, RAIL402_SECRET_KEY_FILE or RAIL402_MCP_TOKEN is set, even empty. ([`packages/mcp/src/hosted.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/src/hosted.ts), `resolveHostedConfig`)
- Hosted mode is dispatched before anything else loads and is built only on @rail402.dev/sdk/discovery: a running hosted process never loads the SDK's key loading, signing guard, budget ledgers, payment flows or scheme clients, nor the local server's payment tools; a test records the process's module graph and fails if any appears. ([`packages/mcp/src/bin.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/src/bin.ts), `hostedMain`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts): refuses to start with a signing key present
- [`packages/mcp/test/hosted-graph.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/hosted-graph.test.ts): loads no key, signer, ledger or payment module in hosted mode
- [`packages/mcp/test/hosted-graph.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/hosted-graph.test.ts): does see the payment modules when the local server starts, so the check can fail

**Residual risk.** None.

### H3. Hosted MCP calls used to flood discovery

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB13 (Hosted MCP and remote agents) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** A remote client loops tool calls, each a search, to load the Bazaar and the host shared with settlement.

**What Stellar makes different.** Search runs in the service that also verifies and settles Stellar payments, so its cost competes with settlement.

**Preconditions.** A public hosted endpoint. **Impact.** Slower or refused discovery and settlement.

**Mitigations**

- Per-client limit (IPv4 address or IPv6 /64), an in-flight cap answered with rpc_overloaded, a 64 KiB body limit, at most 10 results per search, a 3 s upstream timeout, and no sessions. ([`packages/mcp/src/hosted.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/src/hosted.ts), `ClientLimiter`)
- One request carries one JSON-RPC message: a batch is refused with rpc_batch_unsupported before any tool runs, so the per-client limit and the in-flight cap bound tool calls, not just HTTP requests. ([`packages/mcp/src/hosted-server.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/src/hosted-server.ts), `createHostedHandler`)
- A process-wide budget of tool calls per minute (RAIL402_MCP_UPSTREAM_PER_MINUTE, default 300, each at most one Bazaar request) answers rpc_upstream_budget itself. ([`packages/mcp/src/hosted-server.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/src/hosted-server.ts), `createHostedHandler`)
- With a Bazaar discovery-internal access key (read from the file named by RAIL402_MCP_UPSTREAM_KEY_FILE, never from the environment, never logged), every upstream request carries the key and an X-Rail402-Client identifier per remote client (an HMAC of its address key under the access key), so the Bazaar budgets each remote client apart instead of charging every hosted user to the endpoint's address. ([`packages/mcp/src/hosted.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/src/hosted.ts), `upstreamFetch`)
- At the Bazaar, a hosted endpoint that calls with a discovery-internal key and its client in X-Rail402-Client is charged per client: each remote client has its own discovery cost budget (SEARCH_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE units, as large as a direct client's) within the identity's own, so one client cannot use up the others' searches. ([`apps/rail402/src/app.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/src/app.ts), `discoveryCharges`)
- TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS has no default, so a deployment must state its proxy chain; X-Forwarded-For arriving while it is 0 is logged once and counted in rail402_mcp_warnings_total. ([`packages/mcp/src/hosted.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/src/hosted.ts), `resolveHostedConfig`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts): rate-limits tool calls per client
- [`packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts): refuses calls beyond the in-flight cap with rpc_overloaded
- [`packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts): mints no session ids
- [`packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts): refuses a JSON-RPC batch, so one request runs at most one search
- [`packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts): caps tool calls across all clients with rpc_upstream_budget
- [`packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts): needs TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS set explicitly, 0 included
- [`packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts): warns when a proxy forwards requests and TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS is 0
- [`packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts): reads the key from a file, refuses it inline, and never echoes a malformed one
- [`packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts): sends the key and a per-client identifier upstream, so each client has its own discovery budget
- [`apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/rail402/test/access.test.ts): charges discovery costs to each forwarded client and to the identity, never to the identity's address

**Residual risk.** The limits live in one process's memory (run one replica, or divide them by the replica count). Without an access key, all hosted users are one client of the endpoint's address at the Bazaar and share its discovery budget; with one, each remote client is budgeted under the key's identity, and the identity's own limit still bounds the endpoint as a whole. A client with many addresses gets many budgets.

### H4. Browsers and rebinding drive the hosted MCP

Mitigated · Spoofing · boundary TB13 (Hosted MCP and remote agents) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** A page in a visitor's browser calls the endpoint through DNS rebinding or with a foreign Host header.

**What Stellar makes different.** Nothing Stellar-specific is reachable: the endpoint holds no key and no state.

**Preconditions.** A browser visiting a hostile page. **Impact.** Requests counted against the visitor's address.

**Mitigations**

- The Host header must be one of PUBLIC_HOSTS; there are no credentials or state, so any Origin is harmless. ([`packages/mcp/src/hosted-server.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/src/hosted-server.ts), `createHostedHandler`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts): refuses a Host outside the public name

**Residual risk.** None.

### H5. Seller text injected into remote agents through the hosted MCP

Accepted risk · Spoofing, Tampering · boundary TB13 (Hosted MCP and remote agents) · asset: Agent budgets

**Threat.** Listing text served by the hosted endpoint carries instructions aimed at the remote agent.

**What Stellar makes different.** An agent steered by listing text may pay a Stellar account it should not, with its own key elsewhere.

**Preconditions.** A seller with a listing in the Bazaar. **Impact.** A misled agent, within its own caps.

**Mitigations**

- The hosted tools use the same bounded result shapes as the local server, and their descriptions and instructions mark seller text as untrusted data. ([`packages/mcp/src/results.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/src/results.ts), `searchResult`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/mcp/test/hosted.test.ts): bounds and marks seller text as untrusted

**Residual risk.** Agents still read seller text (M5).

{/* generated:end */}

## Explorer indexing

The Explorer reads public ledgers and counts x402 settlements under published indexing rules: what
forged events, spoofed attribution or bad ledger data could do to its numbers.

{/* generated:begin topic-explorer */}

### I1. A fake upto_settled event counted as a payment

Mitigated · Spoofing, Tampering · boundary TB11 (Ledger sources and the Explorer indexer) · asset: Explorer and report integrity

**Threat.** Any contract can emit an event with the upto_settled topic and plausible data, or emit it during a call to the real settlement contract.

**What Stellar makes different.** Contract events carry the emitting contract's id, but nothing stops another contract from choosing the same topic and data.

**Preconditions.** Any account that can deploy a contract. **Impact.** Without protection: invented upto payments, payers and sellers in the Explorer and the report.

**Mitigations**

- R-UPTO counts only a settle call on a registry contract, reads only the upto_settled event that contract emitted, refuses a registry contract whose instance runs other WASM, and checks the token transfer against the event. ([`packages/x402-index/src/rules/upto.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/src/rules/upto.ts), `fromRegistryContract`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts): ignores upto_settled from a contract outside the registry
- [`packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts): ignores upto_settled emitted by another contract during a registry settle
- [`packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts): refuses a registry contract whose instance runs other WASM

**Residual risk.** None.

### I2. A fake SAC asset topic

Mitigated · Spoofing · boundary TB11 (Ledger sources and the Explorer indexer) · asset: Explorer and report integrity

**Threat.** A custom SEP-41 token emits transfer events whose fourth topic says USDC with Circle's issuer.

**What Stellar makes different.** The asset topic is data the token contract chooses; only a Stellar Asset Contract's id is derived from its asset.

**Preconditions.** Any account that can deploy a token contract. **Impact.** Without protection: worthless tokens shown and totalled as USDC.

**Mitigations**

- An asset is verified only when Asset(code, issuer).contractId(passphrase) equals the emitting contract; otherwise it is a custom token with no code or issuer. ([`packages/x402-index/src/asset.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/src/asset.ts), `verifySacAsset`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts): does not verify a token whose SAC topic names another asset
- [`packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts): verifies only names whose contract id matches, including native

**Residual risk.** None.

### I3. Attribution spoofed or inflated

Mitigated · Spoofing, Repudiation · boundary TB11 (Ledger sources and the Explorer indexer) · asset: Explorer and report integrity

**Threat.** Accounts get into the registry without evidence, a shared relayer's other traffic is counted as x402, attribution follows discovery time instead of the ledger, or unattributed look-alikes are added to the headline.

**What Stellar makes different.** The exact scheme leaves no x402 marker on chain: any sponsored single transfer with the payer's address authorization has the same shape.

**Preconditions.** A registry change, or transactions shaped like x402 settlements. **Impact.** Without protection: facilitators credited with payments they did not settle, and inflated network totals.

**Mitigations**

- Registry entries need public evidence archived under its SHA-256 and an epoch and ledger range; one address belongs to one facilitator per ledger; the shaped tier is never a registry tier. ([`packages/x402-index/src/registry.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/src/registry.ts), `registrySchema`)
- Attribution looks accounts up by epoch and ledger, never by when an entry was added; unmatched settlements are labelled shaped. ([`packages/x402-index/src/attribution.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/src/attribution.ts), `attribute`)
- Settlements through the concluded beta's contracts (rule R-UPTO-BETA, WASM checked on chain) are labelled beta history whoever submitted them, so Rail402's own beta never lands on the unattributed line; every beta contract's range ends at the beta's end, so later calls through the still-callable contracts are not beta history. ([`packages/x402-index/src/attribution.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/src/attribution.ts), `betaAttribution`)
- Rows of a facilitator marked sharedRelayer carry attribution.sharedRelayer, and the totals show that part of the headline on its own line (headlineSharedRelayer), on the report and in aggregates.json. ([`apps/explorer/src/rebuild/aggregates.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/rebuild/aggregates.ts), `headlineSharedRelayer`)
- Totals keep the rail402 and facilitator tiers in the headline and report shaped matches, the concluded beta and self-payments apart. ([`apps/explorer/src/rebuild/aggregates.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/rebuild/aggregates.ts), `HEADLINE_TIERS`)
- Every account the hosted service's /supported advertises (signers and the upto settlement authority) must have an open entry of the rail402 facilitator before it settles; registry-check --hosted-&lt;network&gt; checks it at launch and after every rotation. ([`apps/explorer/src/registry-check.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/registry-check.ts), `checkHostedAccounts`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/x402-index/test/registry.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/registry.test.ts): refuses a registry entry without evidence or a ledger range
- [`packages/x402-index/test/registry.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/registry.test.ts): refuses an address claimed by two facilitators in overlapping ledgers
- [`packages/x402-index/test/attribution.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/attribution.test.ts): attributes by ledger range, not by discovery time
- [`apps/explorer/test/rebuild.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/rebuild.test.ts): counts self-payments outside the headline
- [`packages/x402-index/test/registry.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/registry.test.ts): labels settlements through the beta's testnet contracts as beta history, whoever submitted them
- [`apps/explorer/test/rebuild.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/rebuild.test.ts): flags shared-relayer rows on the row and totals them on their own line
- [`packages/x402-index/test/attribution.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/attribution.test.ts): flag the rows of a facilitator marked sharedRelayer
- [`packages/x402-index/test/registry.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/registry.test.ts): closes every concluded beta contract's range, so later calls are not beta history
- [`apps/explorer/test/registry-check.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/registry-check.test.ts): names an advertised account the registry does not list, or lists only in a closed range

**Residual risk.** Shaped matches stay heuristic and are labelled as such; a shared relayer's rows can include transfers that were not x402 payments, which each row and the totals' own line say.

### I4. Tampered or truncated ledger data

Mitigated · Tampering · boundary TB11 (Ledger sources and the Explorer indexer) · asset: Explorer and report integrity

**Threat.** Ledger bytes from the public data lake or Lumeus RPC are altered, swapped, replayed or invented, planting or hiding settlements.

**What Stellar makes different.** Each ledger header commits to the previous ledger's hash, to its transaction set and to its results. Metas are not hashed by the header, but a successful contract call's result records the SHA-256 of its return value and events, so events are bound to consensus through the result.

**Preconditions.** Control of, or a man in the middle on, a ledger source. **Impact.** Without protection: invented or missing settlements that look rebuildable.

**Mitigations**

- Every ledger read must hash to its own header, its transaction set and results must match the header's hashes, and it must be the ledger asked for; a failure marks the ledger failed and stops the indexer. The full-scan rebuild also requires each ledger's previous hash to be the last verified ledger's hash. ([`apps/explorer/src/pipeline.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/pipeline.ts), `indexSingle`)
- Before reading a successful candidate's events, the classifier recomputes SHA-256 of InvokeHostFunctionSuccessPreImage \{ returnValue, events \} from the meta and requires the operation result's success hash; a mismatch stops the indexer (events_unbound). The upto event must also equal the signed settle(payer, terms, amount) arguments. ([`packages/x402-index/src/integrity.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/src/integrity.ts), `assertEventsBound`)
- Independent anchors: the indexer commits a ledger, so serves and reports its rows, only after its hash matched a Horizon with full history (required on pubnet); a different hash marks the ledger failed and stops the indexer, and the anchors checked are stored and listed in the published manifest. The rebuild checks every anchor it is given and refuses one it cannot check. ([`apps/explorer/src/indexer/ingester.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/indexer/ingester.ts), `async #anchor(`)
- Lumeus RPC getLedgers answers are checked ledger by ledger: each ledger's own header must name the sequence it is listed under. ([`packages/ledger-source/src/rpc.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/src/rpc.ts), `headerSequence(meta) !== sequence`)
- The data lake reader is pinned to one https host, refuses redirects, caps object and decompressed sizes, and checks each object holds exactly the ledger asked for. ([`packages/ledger-source/src/lake.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/src/lake.ts), `decodeLakeObject`)
- The report covers the range only up to the first candidate ledger that is not indexed yet or failed, says where and why it stops, and is not marked reproducible while a ledger is failed. ([`apps/explorer/src/api/report.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/api/report.ts), `coverageOf`)
- A live lookup is answered only when the RPC's envelope hashes (outer, or inner for a fee bump) on the network include the requested hash; otherwise explorer_lookup_unavailable. ([`apps/explorer/src/web/live-lookup.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/web/live-lookup.ts), `answersFor`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/ledger-source/test/chain.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/chain.test.ts): stops at a ledger whose previous hash does not match
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/chain.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/chain.test.ts): stops at a gap instead of skipping it
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/chain.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/chain.test.ts): refuses a tampered header, transaction set or result
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/lake.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/lake.test.ts): refuses an object that holds another ledger or is not a ledger
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/lake.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/lake.test.ts): refuses redirects
- [`apps/explorer/test/rebuild.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/rebuild.test.ts): checks an anchor for the ledger before the range, and refuses anchors it cannot check
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/anchors.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/anchors.test.ts): reads a ledger's hash and checks the answer names that ledger
- [`packages/x402-index/test/integrity.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/integrity.test.ts): stops a ledger whose settlement events were rewritten, although its header, set and results agree
- [`packages/x402-index/test/integrity.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/integrity.test.ts): stops on an upto settlement whose event and transfer were rewritten together
- [`packages/x402-index/test/integrity.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/integrity.test.ts): stops on a concluded beta settlement whose transfer was rewritten
- [`packages/x402-index/test/integrity.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/integrity.test.ts): refuses an event that reports another amount than the settle call, even with a matching transfer
- [`apps/explorer/test/live-lookup.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/live-lookup.test.ts): refuses an RPC answer for another transaction than the hash asked for
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): stops at a ledger whose bytes are not the ledger asked for, marks it failed and holds the report before it
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): commits a ledger only after its hash matched the anchor, stops on another hash and waits while it is unavailable
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer-units.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer-units.test.ts): stops before the first candidate that is pending or failed
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/rpc.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/rpc.test.ts): refuses a ledger whose own header names another sequence than the one listed

**Residual risk.** Ledger-entry changes in the meta (used only for the upto WASM check) are not bound by the result hash; a registered upto contract cannot be upgraded, so a forged instance entry can only exclude a row, never add one. Live single-transaction lookups come from one RPC answer bound to the requested hash, whose result is not checked against a ledger header. Registry-scope ledgers are verified one by one and not chained to each other: on testnet with anchors turned off, a dishonest byte source could serve an invented, self-consistent ledger; pubnet refuses to run without anchors.

### I5. Hostile XDR crashes or stalls the classifier

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB11 (Ledger sources and the Explorer indexer) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** Corrupt or unusual envelopes, results or metas make the classifier throw, allocate without bound, or read an unknown meta version as an empty transaction and skip it.

**What Stellar makes different.** A protocol upgrade can introduce a new transaction meta version that an older XDR library does not know.

**Preconditions.** Any transaction on the network, or a corrupted ledger object. **Impact.** Without protection: a stalled indexer, or settlements silently missing after an upgrade.

**Mitigations**

- The classifier returns malformed for base64 that does not decode and none for an envelope that does not read, bounds its output, and throws only coded errors that stop the indexer at that ledger: an unknown meta version, an undecodable ledger, or events that do not hash to their result. ([`packages/x402-index/src/classify.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/src/classify.ts), `classifyTransaction`)
- A ledger whose candidate transactions have no result, or that does not decode, raises a coded error instead of being skipped. ([`packages/x402-index/src/ledger.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/src/ledger.ts), `LedgerDecodeError`)
- Bytes an attacker chooses never reach a row as they are: a CAP-67 to_muxed_id is taken only as a u64 (a text or hash memo form is transfer_event_mismatch in every rule), and every row field must have its expected shape and be text without NUL, valid as UTF-8 and bounded, or the transaction is excluded as malformed, so a hostile transaction is counted and never stops a commit. ([`packages/x402-index/src/storable.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/src/storable.ts), `unstorableFields`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/x402-index/test/fuzz.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/fuzz.test.ts): bounds output on random envelopes and metas, or stops with a coded error
- [`packages/x402-index/test/fuzz.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/fuzz.test.ts): either classifies a corrupted ledger or stops with a coded error
- [`packages/x402-index/test/classify.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/classify.test.ts): stops on an unknown meta version
- [`packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts): never takes a text or hash memo form of to_muxed_id into a row, in any rule
- [`packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts): refuses any row field that could not be stored as it is
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): counts a hostile token's NUL muxed id as an exclusion and keeps indexing

**Residual risk.** None.

### I6. Gaps, epochs and lag hide or duplicate rows

Mitigated · Repudiation, Tampering · boundary TB11 (Ledger sources and the Explorer indexer) · asset: Explorer and report integrity

**Threat.** A missed range, a testnet reset that reuses ledger numbers, a replayed or broken discovery walk, a bounded run that ends before ledgers already found, a rebuilt discovery index, or a stalled tail makes the Explorer silently incomplete, double-counts rows or merges two epochs.

**What Stellar makes different.** Testnet resets start ledger numbers again; transaction hashes include the network passphrase, which testnet keeps.

**Preconditions.** A testnet reset, a crash or failure mid-walk, a second indexer on the same database, a bounded run, a registry change, or a discovery index rebuild. **Impact.** Without protection: wrong totals with no visible sign.

**Mitigations**

- Rows are keyed by (network, epoch, transaction hash) and written as upserts. A testnet reset is a new discovery generation whose head is more than 1,000 ledgers behind the indexed candidates: it stops the indexer before anything of the new network is stored, until the new epoch is registered; a head that is only behind (a lagging replica, or a source without a new generation) and anything on pubnet is counted and logged, never a stop. A bounded run that cannot discover or read a ledger through its end exits non-zero (range_incomplete). ([`apps/explorer/src/epoch.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/epoch.ts), `export function isReset`)
- Every discovery page's candidate ledgers and the checkpoint it vouches for (the ledger before its last record, or scanned_through_ledger at the end of a window; records must come in ascending order) are stored in one transaction, so a failure loses at most the page under way; candidates are upserts, the checkpoint only moves forward, and a ledger is committed only while it is pending, so a restart or a second indexer never loses or repeats one. ([`apps/explorer/src/store.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/store.ts), `async recordWindow`)
- Routes keep their registry bounds only: a bounded run's last ledger bounds its walks and is never stored, so a later run resumes where it stopped; a registry range that ends before a route's checkpoint moves the checkpoint back to its end, so a widened range is walked again. ([`apps/explorer/src/store.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/store.ts), `Walked past`)
- A discovery answer under another generation or decoder than the route's last page walks the route again from its first ledger, so records the source added or corrected in a rebuild are found. ([`apps/explorer/src/indexer/ingester.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/indexer/ingester.ts), `async #newReadView`)
- Routes follow the registry: added accounts are walked from their first ledger, removed ones are dropped with the ledgers only they listed and those ledgers' rows; ledgers indexed under older rules or upto contracts are read again, and rows under an older registry version are re-attributed in place with the classifier's own attribution function. ([`apps/explorer/src/store.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/store.ts), `reconcileRegistry`)
- Lag (the discovery source's head minus the ledger every route has walked), Lumeus request and error counts, candidate ledgers by status and failure metrics; health is degraded while behind and stopped after a failure. ([`apps/explorer/src/metrics.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/metrics.ts), `rail402_explorer_lumeus_requests_total`)
- Commits refresh the daily and per-account totals under a transaction-scoped advisory lock per network and epoch, so commits and removals that touch the same seller or day take turns. ([`apps/explorer/src/store.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/store.ts), `lockTotals`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): keys rows by epoch, and stops when a new discovery generation's head falls behind the indexed ledgers
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): resumes every route from its checkpoint and reads each candidate once
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): follows registry changes: routes come and go with their ledgers, and re-attribution equals a fresh index
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): reads the ledgers again when the upto contracts change
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): keeps a route's checkpoint when discovery fails for now, and fails a bounded run that cannot finish
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): follows the head as discovery publishes new ledgers, then stops on request
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): refresh shared sellers and days concurrently without colliding on the totals
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): never takes a lagging or dishonest head for a reset: without a new generation it is only counted
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): resumes after a bounded run that ends before ledgers already found, and finds them all again
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): walks a route again from its first ledger when the discovery source's generation or decoder changes
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): stores every page as it is read, so a failure later in a window loses at most that page
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): leaves no full-scan row, total or report behind, and serves no old report
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer-units.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer-units.test.ts): sees a testnet reset only in a new generation whose head is far behind what was indexed
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts): hands over each page as it is read, so a failure later in the window loses at most that page
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts): refuses records that go back in the ledger order, since a page's checkpoint relies on it

**Residual risk.** None.

### I7. A compromised Explorer reaches the facilitator

Mitigated · Elevation of privilege, Information disclosure · boundary TB9 (Testnet and pubnet inside one service) · asset: Secrets

**Threat.** The Explorer is public and parses attacker-chosen data; if it shared the facilitator's database or role, a compromise would read the settlement ledger and API keys or alter them.

**What Stellar makes different.** Every byte the indexer parses comes from public ledgers, where anyone can place transactions.

**Preconditions.** A code-execution or SQL bug in the Explorer. **Impact.** Without protection: the facilitator's records and keys exposed through the public Explorer.

**Mitigations**

- A separate database with an owner, a writer and a reader role; the reader is SELECT-only with read-only transactions and a statement timeout; no Explorer role may connect to the facilitator's database. ([`apps/explorer/sql/roles.sql`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/sql/roles.sql), `REVOKE CONNECT ON DATABASE`)
- Each process checks its role at startup and refuses superusers, role creators and any role that can reach the facilitator's database; the reader check also requires read-only, SELECT-only access with a timeout. The indexer holds no keys. ([`apps/explorer/src/roles.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/roles.ts), `assertReaderRole`)
- The reader's statement timeout is enforced by the client: each statement carries statement_timeout, and one still running after it is cancelled with pg_cancel_backend, so SET statement_timeout = 0 does not lift it. The writer cannot modify schema_migrations, and the indexer refuses a writer that can. ([`apps/explorer/src/db.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/db.ts), `cancelBackend`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/explorer/test/roles.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/roles.integration.test.ts): the reader role cannot write or read the facilitator database
- [`apps/explorer/test/roles.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/roles.integration.test.ts): the writer role writes the Explorer tables but cannot reach the facilitator database
- [`apps/explorer/test/roles.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/roles.integration.test.ts): refuses to run as a superuser
- [`apps/explorer/test/roles.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/roles.integration.test.ts): keeps the migration log out of the writer's reach
- [`apps/explorer/test/roles.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/roles.integration.test.ts): cancels a reader statement that outlives the timeout, even after SET statement_timeout = 0

**Residual risk.** Both databases share one Postgres cluster, so a cluster-level exploit crosses them.

### I8. Published totals cannot be reproduced

Mitigated · Repudiation · boundary TB11 (Ledger sources and the Explorer indexer) · asset: Explorer and report integrity

**Threat.** Report numbers change between runs or are edited, and nobody can show how they were derived.

**What Stellar makes different.** Public ledgers are permanent and readable by anyone, so every number can be derived again from them.

**Preconditions.** Any published total. **Impact.** Without protection: totals that have to be taken on trust.

**Mitigations**

- The rebuild command writes canonical NDJSON sorted by position, aggregates and a manifest with the rules and registry versions, the ledger range and hashes, per-partition row hashes and file hashes; the same inputs give the same manifest hash. ([`apps/explorer/src/rebuild/rebuild.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/rebuild/rebuild.ts), `writeManifest`)
- A registry-scope report's manifest lists every ledger it read with its hash; \`pnpm explorer:rebuild --ledgers-from manifest.json\` reads those ledgers from the public data lake, with no discovery source, and writes the same manifest. ([`apps/explorer/src/rebuild/rebuild.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/rebuild/rebuild.ts), `rebuildLedgers`)
- The report's network-wide block is a full-scope rebuild of a recent window by the same code; it prints its \`pnpm explorer:rebuild --from --to\` command (with \`--anchor-horizon\` when anchored) and its manifest is served at /api/v1/\{network\}/network-window/manifest, so anyone can rebuild that exact window and compare the hash. ([`apps/explorer/src/window/window.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/window/window.ts), `runNetworkWindow`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/explorer/test/rebuild.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/rebuild.test.ts): rebuilds the fixture slice to the committed manifest hash
- [`apps/explorer/test/rebuild.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/rebuild.test.ts): is byte-identical across runs and request concurrency
- [`apps/explorer/test/rebuild.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/rebuild.test.ts): fails rather than shortening a range with a missing ledger
- [`apps/explorer/test/rebuild.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/rebuild.test.ts): rebuilds only the listed ledgers, the same across runs and concurrency, and lists them with their hashes
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): finds the attributed rows a full scan of the range finds, and its report rebuilds from the ledger list
- [`apps/explorer/test/network-window.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/network-window.test.ts): rebuilds the newest window in full, by tier, and its command reproduces the manifest hash

**Residual risk.** None.

### I9. Indexing load starves the facilitator

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB17 (The host and co-located services) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** Indexing every ledger (hundreds of gigabytes of downloads and tens of CPU-hours for pubnet history) runs on the host and database cluster the facilitator uses.

**What Stellar makes different.** Settlement latency matters: an x402 payment waits for the facilitator's answer.

**Preconditions.** An indexer running beside the facilitator. **Impact.** Without protection: slower or failing settlements while history is indexed.

**Mitigations**

- The indexer never reads every ledger: discovery selects the ledgers that hold transactions of registry accounts and upto contracts, and only those are read, one object or one getLedgers page at a time. The full scan is a separate command run where its load does no harm. ([`apps/explorer/src/indexer/discovery.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/indexer/discovery.ts), `discoveryPlan`)
- Discovery and ledger reads share one limit of at most 8 requests in flight, as Lumeus asks for history walks; settings above it are refused. ([`packages/ledger-source/src/lumeus.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/src/lumeus.ts), `LUMEUS_MAX_CONCURRENCY`)
- Ledger reads hold at most EXPLORER_BYTES_IN_FLIGHT_MIB (64 MiB) of raw fetched bytes, size getLedgers pages to half of it and decode one ledger at a time, releasing it before the next, so a busy stretch of large ledgers cannot exhaust the indexer's memory; the bytes held are exported as rail402_explorer_ledger_bytes_in_flight. ([`apps/explorer/src/indexer/bytes.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/indexer/bytes.ts), `maxBytesInFlight`)
- The network-wide window is its own process, off unless EXPLORER_NETWORK_WINDOW_HOURS is set (at most 168 hours). Each run has a time budget, a download budget (required explicitly on pubnet) and a row budget, reads the data lake within a byte budget with one decoded ledger at a time and rows streamed to files it removes, and keeps the last published window when a budget stops it. It reads no Lumeus product and nothing of the facilitator. ([`apps/explorer/src/config.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/config.ts), `networkWindowConfig`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): indexes only the ledgers the recorded Lumeus discovery selects, through the real Activity client
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts): keeps at most its number of tasks in flight
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer-units.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer-units.test.ts): refuse invalid values
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer-units.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer-units.test.ts): holds at most the byte limit of fetched ledgers and decodes each one when it is taken
- [`apps/explorer/test/memory.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/memory.test.ts): indexes rounds of large ledgers inside a 128 MB heap
- [`apps/explorer/test/network-window.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/network-window.test.ts): stops at its download, row and time budgets and leaves no files
- [`apps/explorer/test/network-window.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/network-window.test.ts): fails closed on pubnet: an explicit download budget, independent anchors and a safe role
- [`apps/explorer/test/memory.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/memory.test.ts): rebuilds a full scope of large ledgers inside a 128 MB heap with a byte budget

**Residual risk.** A registry facilitator with very busy accounts (a shared relayer) makes discovery read more ledgers; CPU and memory limits for the indexer's container are set where it is deployed.

### I10. Discovery leaves out or invents candidate ledgers

Mitigated · Tampering, Repudiation · boundary TB19 (The Lumeus discovery source) · asset: Explorer and report integrity

**Threat.** The discovery source, or someone between it and the indexer, leaves ledgers out of an account's or contract's activity, lists ledgers that hold nothing, or answers for another route, window or network.

**What Stellar makes different.** Activity records are decoded data a service computes from ledgers; nothing on chain commits to them. The ledgers themselves are bound by their hashes.

**Preconditions.** A faulty or dishonest discovery source, or control of the connection to it. **Impact.** Without protection: invented settlements, settlements silently missing from pages and totals, or unbounded reads (billed on mainnet) of ledgers a source lists.

**Mitigations**

- Discovery only selects ledgers: every row comes from a ledger read in full, verified against its own hashes (and anchors where configured) and classified by the unchanged rules, so discovery cannot create a settlement that is not on chain. A listed ledger that holds no registry transaction adds only that ledger's own real rows (unattributed look-alikes, failed attempts, exclusion counts); the manifest lists it and the report counts such ledgers (ledgersWithoutAttributedRow). ([`apps/explorer/src/pipeline.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/pipeline.ts), `indexSingle`)
- Activity pages are checked: the first page pins the read view and later pages must keep it, every record must lie inside the requested window, the page must be for the indexer's network, and next links must stay on the same origin and route. ([`packages/ledger-source/src/lumeus.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/src/lumeus.ts), `function parsePage`)
- The report states its scope and lists every ledger read; the full-scan audit command reads every ledger of the range from the public data lake, and its attributed rows must equal the report's, which shows an omission. ([`apps/explorer/src/api/report.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/api/report.ts), `auditCommand`)
- Each upto contract whose settlements emit an event has its own contract route; a concluded beta contract, which emits no event and moves no funds itself (its contract activity is empty), is found through topic search for the token approve events that name it as the spender, which each of its settlements carries in the same transaction. ([`apps/explorer/src/indexer/discovery.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/indexer/discovery.ts), `R-UPTO-BETA`)
- A route adds at most EXPLORER_MAX_CANDIDATES_PER_ROUND new candidate ledgers per round and continues next round, counted in rail402_explorer_candidates_capped_total; candidates with no attributed row are counted in rail402_explorer_candidates_unattributed_total. ([`apps/explorer/src/indexer/ingester.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/indexer/ingester.ts), `maxCandidatesPerRound`)
- The report counts only rows of the ledgers its manifest lists, whatever else the tables hold, and the move to registry scope removes every row, total and report of the earlier full scan; a stored report of another scope or manifest version is never served. ([`apps/explorer/src/api/report.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/api/report.ts), `Only rows of the listed ledgers count`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): finds the attributed rows a full scan of the range finds, and its report rebuilds from the ledger list
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts): refuses pages that leave the window, the read view or the network
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts): refuses a next link to another origin or route, and never sends the key there
- [`apps/explorer/test/lumeus.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/lumeus.integration.test.ts): finds and classifies the known payments in ledgers
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): finds a beta upto settlement by the approve event that names its contract, through the real Activity client
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): reads at most a capped number of new candidates per route and round, and reports candidates with no attributed row
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): leaves no full-scan row, total or report behind, and serves no old report
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts): walks the approve events that name a beta upto contract, through topic search
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer-units.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer-units.test.ts): walks beta upto contracts by the approve events naming them, and channels at a slow cadence
- [`apps/explorer/test/lumeus.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/lumeus.integration.test.ts): finds a concluded beta upto settlement through topic search, as a full scan does

**Residual risk.** An omission shows only in the full-scan audit. A settlement whose fee payer is no registry account and that does not go through a registry upto contract is not discovered (indexing rules §12); a beta settlement whose transaction carries no approve event naming its contract is found only when another route lists its ledger (none in a sample of 16 of the 105 known); network-wide exact-scheme look-alikes are not measured live.
