### I11. The Lumeus key leaks or reaches another host

Mitigated · Information disclosure · boundary TB19 (The Lumeus discovery source) · asset: Secrets

**Threat.** The Lumeus server key is set inline in the environment, logged, sent to a host that is not Lumeus (a configured URL, a next link, a redirect), or a testnet key is used against mainnet hosts or the reverse.

**What Stellar makes different.** Lumeus keys are per network: lum_test\_ for testnet, lum_live\_ for mainnet; one key covers Activity and RPC.

**Preconditions.** A misconfiguration, a hostile next link or redirect, or access to logs or the process environment. **Impact.** Without protection: a stolen key spends the organisation's Lumeus credits.

**Mitigations**

- The key is read only from a file (EXPLORER_LUMEUS_KEY_FILE, EXPLORER\_&lt;NETWORK&gt;\_LUMEUS_KEY_FILE); an inline key is refused, and no error repeats it. ([`apps/explorer/src/config.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/config.ts), `export function lumeusKey`)
- The key is sent only to the network's own Lumeus host for that product: Activity and RPC URLs are checked against an allow-list, and a key's prefix must match the network. ([`packages/ledger-source/src/lumeus.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/src/lumeus.ts), `export function lumeusEndpoint`)
- RPC clients (the ledger source and the web live lookup) attach the key only for the Lumeus RPC host of its network and refuse any other pairing; next links to another origin and redirects are refused. ([`packages/ledger-source/src/rpc.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/src/rpc.ts), `a Lumeus key is sent only to the Lumeus RPC host of its network`)
- The internet-facing web role takes its own key for live lookups: a read-access server key or a publishable key (lum_pub\_…, read-only RPC, sent with the Explorer's origin), so a web compromise does not expose the indexer's key; the indexer refuses publishable keys. ([`apps/explorer/src/web/live-lookup.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/web/live-lookup.ts), `a publishable Lumeus key needs the https origin it is allowed for`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts): sends a key only to the network's Lumeus host of that product
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts): pairs a lum_test\_ key with testnet and a lum_live\_ key with mainnet only
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts): maps the recorded 401 and 400 answers to coded, final errors without the key
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/rpc.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/rpc.test.ts): refuses a key for any other host or network, without repeating it
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer-units.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer-units.test.ts): read the Lumeus key from a file only, and pair it with its network's hosts
- [`apps/explorer/test/live-lookup.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/live-lookup.test.ts): sends the key as a bearer token to the network's Lumeus RPC, and to no other host
- [`apps/explorer/test/live-lookup.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/live-lookup.test.ts): defaults to the network's Lumeus RPC when its key file is set, never with an inline key
- [`apps/explorer/test/live-lookup.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/live-lookup.test.ts): takes a publishable key apart from the indexer's, sent with the Explorer's origin
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts): accepts a publishable key only where asked, for its own network

**Residual risk.** Anyone who can read the key file on the host can use the key; keep a monthly credit cap on the Lumeus organisation and rotate the key there.

### I12. The discovery source is unavailable, throttled or reindexed

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB19 (The Lumeus discovery source) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** Lumeus Activity or RPC answers 429 or 5xx, asks to wait until a daily cap resets, times out on a heavy window, rebuilds its index under a new generation, or puts arbitrary codes and error text in its answers.

**What Stellar makes different.** Discovery reads history by ledger range; a testnet reset also changes the generation and starts ledger numbers again.

**Preconditions.** Any outage, rate limit or index rebuild of the discovery source. **Impact.** Without protection: a stalled or crashing indexer, hammering a throttled service, or a walk stitched from two read views.

**Mitigations**

- 429 and 5xx answers are retried a bounded number of times after Retry-After; a Retry-After over 60 s stops the walk instead of waiting; query_timeout is never repeated unchanged (the window narrows and resumes after the last page stored); generation_changed restarts there without the old read view. ([`packages/ledger-source/src/lumeus.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/src/lumeus.ts), `async *walkRange(`)
- A transient discovery failure keeps the route's checkpoint and is retried in the next round (after the Retry-After the source asked for); the report and health show the lag meanwhile. ([`apps/explorer/src/indexer/ingester.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/indexer/ingester.ts), `discovery failed for now`)
- Ledger reads through Lumeus RPC follow Retry-After too, never wait more than 60 s, and a longer one pauses every ledger read until then instead of retrying each round. ([`packages/ledger-source/src/http.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/src/http.ts), `export function retryAfter`)
- Metric labels for Lumeus failures come from a fixed set of documented codes (anything else is other), and health shows only the stable code of a stop, never remote error text. ([`packages/ledger-source/src/lumeus.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/src/lumeus.ts), `export function lumeusCodeLabel`)
- A candidate whose bytes or anchor are not available yet waits (15 s, doubling, at most an hour) without holding back newer candidates, and anchors are read in Horizon /ledgers pages for runs of nearby ledgers; the report still stops before a waiting ledger. ([`apps/explorer/src/store.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/store.ts), `async deferLedger`)
- At the head, channel and settlement-authority routes, which Lumeus lists only when they pay a fee or move funds, are walked every EXPLORER_SLOW_ROUTE_ROUNDS rounds instead of every round, bounding empty polls (billed on mainnet). ([`apps/explorer/src/indexer/discovery.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/indexer/discovery.ts), `FEE_PAYING_ROLES`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts): retries busy answers after Retry-After, and stops instead of waiting more than 60 s
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts): never repeats a query_timeout request: the window is halved instead
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts): restarts a window without the old read view after generation_changed
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): keeps a route's checkpoint when discovery fails for now, and fails a bounded run that cannot finish
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/rpc.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/rpc.test.ts): waits for a short Retry-After and stops at once, retryable, on a long one
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): pauses every ledger read after a long Retry-After from the RPC
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): lets a candidate whose bytes are not available yet wait without holding back newer ones
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): walks routes of non-fee-paying roles at a slower cadence once they reach the head
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/lumeus.test.ts): labels failed requests with a bounded set of codes, whatever the body says
- [`packages/ledger-source/test/anchors.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/ledger-source/test/anchors.test.ts): reads many hashes in one /ledgers page per run of up to 200 ledgers

**Residual risk.** While discovery is down the Explorer falls behind and its health is degraded; new payments still open through live lookups while the RPC answers. Discovery resumes from every checkpoint, so nothing is lost.

### I13. A payer named for a transfer it never authorized

Mitigated · Spoofing, Repudiation · boundary TB11 (Ledger sources and the Explorer indexer) · asset: Explorer and report integrity

**Threat.** Someone deploys a token whose transfer never calls from.require_auth() and lands a sponsored transfer whose one authorization entry names a chosen account, unsigned or with a signature that does not verify, so that the Explorer shows that account paying a chosen seller.

**What Stellar makes different.** The host authenticates an address authorization entry, and consumes its nonce, only when a contract calls require_auth for that address; no check covers entries that no contract asked for. A Stellar Asset Contract's transfer always calls from.require_auth(); any other token's own code decides.

**Preconditions.** Any account that can deploy a token contract and pay a transaction fee. An indexed row also needs its ledger read (a registered facilitator relaying it, or registry activity in the same ledger); a live lookup reads it directly. **Impact.** Without protection: an arbitrary account shown as the payer on its account page, the payment page and the API and, through a facilitator that relays any asset, counted in the headline payments and distinct payers.

**Mitigations**

- R-EXACT names a payer only when consensus-bound data proves its authorization (check E7): the token is a verified Stellar Asset Contract, or the payer's own key signed the entry; anything else is excluded as unproven_authorization, and E3 and E4 describe only the entry's fields. ([`packages/x402-index/src/rules/exact.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/src/rules/exact.ts), `unproven_authorization`)
- The signature check rebuilds the payload the network signs (the HashIdPreimage of Address or AddressV2 credentials on this network), requires the form the network checks for an account (1 to 20 signatures, strictly ascending keys) with the payer's own key among them and an expiration ledger not before the transaction's, verifies that key's signature only (one check per transfer, whatever the entry carries), and never proves a contract payer. ([`packages/x402-index/src/authorization.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/src/authorization.ts), `export function signedByPayer`)
- The rules version moved to 2, and the indexer reads every ledger indexed under another rules version again when it starts, so no row of the earlier rules stays in the index. ([`apps/explorer/src/store.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/store.ts), `async resetStale`)
- Until then, and for epochs the indexer no longer reads, the Explorer's reads leave out rows of other rules, its account totals while the epoch holds such ledgers, and reports or network windows of other rules; a transaction of such a row is looked up and classified again. ([`apps/explorer/src/api/queries.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/api/queries.ts), `#settlements()`)
- The SDK's inspectTransaction and rail402 tx say an exact payer authorized the transfer only when a Stellar Asset Contract's transfer or the payer's own signature proves it. ([`packages/x402-index/src/shape.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/src/shape.ts), `not proven by the chain`)

**Tests**

- [`packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts): never names an account as the payer of a custom-token transfer it did not sign
- [`packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts): refuses a signature by another key, for another network, transfer or credential, or past its expiration
- [`packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts): reads co-signers in the form the network checks, and proves the payer by its own signature
- [`packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts): checks one signature per transfer, whatever the entry carries
- [`packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/hostile.test.ts): refuses a contract payer in a custom token, whose own code decides its authorization
- [`packages/x402-index/test/authorization.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/authorization.test.ts): verifies the signatures wallets made on every exact settlement in the fixtures
- [`packages/x402-index/test/authorization.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/x402-index/test/authorization.test.ts): builds the payload the SDK signs, for Address and AddressV2 credentials
- [`apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts): never shows an account as the payer of a custom-token transfer it did not sign
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): reads the ledgers again when the rules version changes, so no row of older rules stays
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): reads no row, total or report of other rules until the indexer reads their ledgers again
- [`apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts): never shows a row indexed under other rules as a settlement, and looks the transaction up again
- [`apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts): serves no report computed under other rules
- [`packages/sdk/test/inspect.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/sdk/test/inspect.test.ts): says a payer authorized a transfer only when the chain proves it

**Residual risk.** A Stellar account's signers, weights and thresholds are ledger state the classifier does not read: in a token other than a Stellar Asset Contract the payer's own key counts whatever its weight, and accounts that authorized only through other signers, like contract payers of such tokens, are excluded rather than shown. Such a token never consumes the nonce either, so an entry the payer really signed can land again until it expires, and each copy is a row.

{/* generated:end */}

## Explorer public pages

The Explorer's pages and read API show on-chain data to anyone: what hostile strings, expensive
queries, live lookups or caching could do to visitors and to the service.

{/* generated:begin topic-explorer-web */}

### W1. Script injected through on-chain strings

Mitigated · Tampering, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB12 (Explorer public pages and read API) · asset: Explorer and report integrity

**Threat.** Token asset topics, contract arguments, result codes and registry text reach the pages; an attacker who controls them tries to inject HTML or script into a visitor's browser.

**What Stellar makes different.** Anyone can deploy a token whose events carry arbitrary strings, and memos and arguments are chosen by whoever builds the transaction.

**Preconditions.** A transaction the indexer or a live lookup classifies, or a crafted request path. **Impact.** Without protection: script running on the Explorer's origin, rewriting what an auditor sees.

**Mitigations**

- Pages are built only from escaping Hono JSX nodes, with no raw-HTML helper anywhere in the web sources; links are rendered only for https URLs; every response carries a strict Content-Security-Policy (own script and stylesheet only, no inline code, no plugins, no framing) and no-transform; no third-party script. The one raw text is the compile-time stylesheet inlined into the standalone snapshot, through a function that takes no input. ([`apps/explorer/src/web/csp.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/web/csp.ts), `CONTENT_SECURITY_POLICY`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts): renders script-bearing on-chain strings as text
- [`apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts): sends the strict CSP on every page
- [`apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts): uses no raw HTML in the web sources
- [`apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts): inlines the stylesheet unescaped in the standalone snapshot

**Residual risk.** None.

### W2. Look-alike addresses, bidirectional text and fake assets

Mitigated · Spoofing · boundary TB12 (Explorer public pages and read API) · asset: Explorer and report integrity

**Threat.** Addresses that share prefixes and suffixes, bidirectional or zero-width characters in strings, a token that names itself USDC, or an upto_settled event from an unregistered contract make one party or asset look like another.

**What Stellar makes different.** Vanity strkeys with matching ends can be ground; asset topics and event topics are data the emitting contract chooses.

**Preconditions.** Any account that can submit transactions or deploy a contract. **Impact.** Without protection: an auditor credits the wrong seller, facilitator or asset.

**Mitigations**

- Control, zero-width, bidirectional, Hangul filler and tag characters are stripped from every displayed string; full strkeys are shown on detail pages and carried in the title, label and copy button wherever a table shortens them; the verified badge comes only from the SAC contract-id rule and fake upto events are shown as not a settlement, through the same classifier as the index. ([`apps/explorer/src/web/text.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/web/text.ts), `displayText`)
- Account totals list at most 100 assets per side (paid and received apart), verified Stellar assets first, then by payments, and say how many more each side has, so payments in throwaway tokens cannot hide the other side or a verified asset without notice. ([`apps/explorer/src/api/queries.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/api/queries.ts), `TOTALS_PER_SIDE`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts): strips bidi controls and keeps the full address copyable
- [`apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts): never marks a fake SAC asset topic as verified
- [`apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts): never shows a fake upto_settled event as a settlement
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): list each side apart, verified assets first, and count what they leave out
- [`apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts): says how many assets the account totals leave out, on the page and in the API

**Residual risk.** None.

### W3. Expensive queries against the Explorer API

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB12 (Explorer public pages and read API) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** Deep pages, unindexed filters and floods of requests load the database the indexer also writes.

**What Stellar makes different.** Not Stellar-specific: the Explorer is a public, unauthenticated service.

**Preconditions.** Network access to the Explorer. **Impact.** Without protection: slow pages for everyone and a stalled indexer.

**Mitigations**

- Keyset pages only (offset refused), limit at most 100, unknown parameters refused so filters stay on indexed columns, cursors bound to their list, a per-client rate limit with Retry-After, and the reader role's statement timeout, checked at startup. ([`apps/explorer/src/api/paging.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/api/paging.ts), `keyset`)
- Bounded database use: a 2 s wait for a pool connection and at most 32 requests in flight, beyond which the answer is 503 explorer_unavailable with Retry-After; account totals are read from per-account totals the indexer maintains, never aggregated per page view; the report is computed once per interval by the indexer and only read by web processes; /health shares one database check, given one second, among all requests in any two seconds. ([`apps/explorer/src/web/app.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/web/app.ts), `maxInFlight`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/explorer/test/api.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/api.test.ts): refuses a limit above 100 and an offset parameter
- [`apps/explorer/test/api.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/api.test.ts): rate-limits per client with Retry-After
- [`apps/explorer/test/web.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/web.integration.test.ts): runs as the reader role, whose statement timeout cancels slow queries
- [`apps/explorer/test/api.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/api.test.ts): answers 503 with Retry-After instead of queueing beyond the in-flight limit
- [`apps/explorer/test/web.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/web.integration.test.ts): serves the report the indexer published, equal to computing it again
- [`apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/indexer.integration.test.ts): follows registry changes: routes come and go with their ledgers, and re-attribution equals a fresh index
- [`apps/explorer/test/api.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/api.test.ts): asks the database at most once per interval for /health, however many clients ask

**Residual risk.** The rate limit is per process (keyed by IPv4 address or IPv6 /64) and resets on restart; the edge proxy's limits stay in front.

### W4. Live lookups amplify RPC use

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB12 (Explorer public pages and read API) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** Each hash the index does not hold triggers an RPC getTransaction; random hashes would turn the Explorer into an RPC load generator.

**What Stellar makes different.** Public RPC providers rate-limit per caller; the facilitator's own settlement provider must not share that load.

**Preconditions.** Network access to the Explorer. **Impact.** Without protection: the Explorer's RPC quota exhausted, or a shared provider throttled.

**Mitigations**

- Hash syntax checked first, a negative cache for unknown hashes and a 60 s cache for found ones, concurrent lookups of one hash share one call, a per-client budget (IPv4 address or IPv6 /64) checked before the network's global budget and refunded when the global budget refuses, a quarter of the global budget kept for clients' first lookup in a minute, and a lookup RPC configured apart from any settlement provider (off on pubnet unless set). ([`apps/explorer/src/web/live-lookup.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/web/live-lookup.ts), `lookupBudget`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/explorer/test/live-lookup.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/live-lookup.test.ts): serves repeated unknown hashes from the negative cache
- [`apps/explorer/test/live-lookup.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/live-lookup.test.ts): enforces the per-client and global lookup budgets
- [`apps/explorer/test/live-lookup.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/live-lookup.test.ts): keeps a reserve for first lookups, refunds refused ones and groups IPv6 clients by /64
- [`apps/explorer/test/live-lookup.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/live-lookup.test.ts): answers a found, not yet indexed transaction from memory for a minute

**Residual risk.** Many distinct clients together can still use up the global budget for a minute; live lookups then degrade to 429 while indexed pages keep working.

### W5. A live or outdated page cached as final

Mitigated · Tampering, Repudiation · boundary TB12 (Explorer public pages and read API) · asset: Explorer and report integrity

**Threat.** A live-lookup answer, a not-found answer or a page rendered under an older rules or registry version is cached for a year by browsers and proxies.

**What Stellar makes different.** Closed ledgers are final, so indexed settlements may be cached for long; live answers are not indexed yet.

**Preconditions.** A caching proxy or browser between the Explorer and its visitors. **Impact.** Without protection: stale attribution or a missing payment shown long after it changed.

**Mitigations**

- No page is immutable: an indexed settlement is cached for an hour only when its row carries the served registry version, and briefly otherwise; a row of other rules is not shown at all; its ETag names the row's own versions, so a re-attribution is seen on revalidation. Lists and the report get short lifetimes; live lookups, not-found answers and errors are no-store and labelled. ([`apps/explorer/src/web/cache.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/web/cache.ts), `cacheHeaders`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts): never caches a live lookup and versions the ETag of indexed pages
- [`apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts): caches a row under an older registry version only briefly, with an ETag of its own versions
- [`apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts): never shows a row indexed under other rules as a settlement, and looks the transaction up again

**Residual risk.** A browser or proxy may show an attribution up to an hour old after a registry change.

### W6. The Explorer used as an open redirect

Mitigated · Spoofing · boundary TB12 (Explorer public pages and read API) · asset: Explorer and report integrity

**Threat.** A redirect that copies part of the request path into Location sends visitors from the trusted Explorer domain to a look-alike site, for example through /public//evil.example/.

**What Stellar makes different.** Not Stellar-specific: auditors follow Explorer links from CLIs, SDK output and payment receipts.

**Preconditions.** A link to the Explorer with a crafted path. **Impact.** Without protection: a phishing page that looks like the audit aid.

**Mitigations**

- Alias redirects strip every leading slash and backslash and always answer a same-origin path; other redirects are built only from validated hashes and strkeys. ([`apps/explorer/src/web/app.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/web/app.ts), `Only a same-origin path`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/pages.test.ts): redirects aliases and upper-case hashes, and answers unknown paths with explorer_not_found

**Residual risk.** None.

### W7. One visitor's refused Bazaar call blanks Bazaar data for everyone

Mitigated · Denial of service · boundary TB12 (Explorer public pages and read API) · asset: Availability

**Threat.** The Explorer's Bazaar calls are charged to each visitor's forwarded budget, but its answers are cached for every visitor. A visitor who spends their own budget would turn each refusal into a cached 'Bazaar down' that every other visitor then sees.

**What Stellar makes different.** n/a.

**Preconditions.** A visitor over their forwarded discovery budget who keeps requesting pages. **Impact.** Without protection: pages without listings, services or paid-for links for every visitor.

**Mitigations**

- A 429, or a 503 with Retry-After, is an answer for this visitor only: the page renders without Bazaar data and nothing is cached, like a call cut short by the page's deadline. ([`apps/explorer/src/web/bazaar.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/src/web/bazaar.ts), `a refusal of this visitor's budget`)

**Tests**

- [`apps/explorer/test/explorer-fixes.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/apps/explorer/test/explorer-fixes.test.ts): never keeps one visitor's refused budget as the Bazaar being down for the next visitor

**Residual risk.** A real Bazaar outage (5xx without Retry-After) is still kept for 5 seconds for everyone, so a down Bazaar is not asked once per visitor.

{/* generated:end */}

## Pubnet conformance runs

The conformance harnesses can run against pubnet with a treasury that funds per-run accounts: what a
harness, a crash or the third-party suite it starts could do with real funds.

{/* generated:begin topic-pubnet-harness */}

### CF1. Run funds stranded in accounts nobody can sign for, or that a stranger can sign for

Mitigated · Denial of service, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB18 (Conformance harness and the treasury) · asset: Harness treasury

**Threat.** A pubnet run funds smart accounts whose owner key is random and dropped, merges nothing, or stops on an exception or Ctrl-C before returning funds, and still reports the sweep as done; or a run account that whoever holds its key merged away is skipped as if it had never been created. Or a stranger makes itself the owner of a funded smart account (one whose owner is set by a call after it is created, which anyone can repeat), signs a transfer of its USDC to itself, and the sweep finds nothing to return.

**What Stellar makes different.** A contract account moves its USDC only with its owner's signature through \_\_check_auth, and its own code decides whose signature that is; a constructor runs inside the transaction that creates the contract, so no other transaction can come first. A G account keeps its reserves until it is merged.

**Preconditions.** Any pubnet run with smart-account cases, an RPC error, a suite crash or an interrupt. **Impact.** Without the controls: USDC and reserves lost on every run, or taken by a stranger, while the evidence claims they came back.

**Mitigations**

- Every run account and smart-account owner key is derived from the treasury seed and a role, and written to the run's owner-only registry (addresses and derivation paths, no secret) before it is funded; a contract account the run does not track, or whose owner it did not derive, is never funded. ([`tools/conformance/src/network.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/src/network.ts), `contractOwner`)
- The smart-account payer is a contract built in this repository whose constructor binds the derived owner key in the transaction that creates it; it exports no other function that writes state, so the key cannot be set first or changed afterwards. ([`contracts/testing/ed25519-account/src/lib.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/ed25519-account/src/lib.rs), `__constructor`)
- Before the treasury sends USDC to a contract account, the run reads the account on chain and refuses it unless it runs the fixture account's code with the run's derived key as owner, or the OpenZeppelin smart account's code; a ledger it cannot read refuses too. ([`tools/conformance/src/network.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/src/network.ts), `pubnet_contract_owner_unverified`)
- Sweep-back returns the USDC of every run account and smart account (authorised by the derived owner), merges the run accounts, then reads every balance again and fails the sweep-back case when anything is left, naming how much. ([`tools/conformance/src/network.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/src/network.ts), `SWEEP_DUST`)
- A run account the run saw on the network that is gone at the sweep (merged by whoever holds its key) fails the sweep; only an account the run never created is passed over. ([`tools/conformance/src/network.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/src/network.ts), `merged outside the run`)
- Sweep-back runs once whatever happens: after the body, after an exception (rethrown afterwards) and on SIGINT or SIGTERM before the process exits. ([`tools/conformance/src/network.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/src/network.ts), `withRun`)
- After a crash, the registry is enough to return the funds of every account of the run. ([`tools/conformance/src/pubnet-run.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/src/pubnet-run.ts), `recover`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/conformance/test/network.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/network.integration.test.ts): returns a smart account's USDC with its derived owner key and merges the run's accounts
- [`tools/conformance/test/network.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/network.integration.test.ts): reports what it could not return and fails the sweep
- [`tools/conformance/test/network.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/network.integration.test.ts): reports a run account merged away by whoever holds its key, instead of skipping it
- [`tools/conformance/test/network.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/network.integration.test.ts): refuses to fund a contract account the run does not track, or whose owner it did not derive
- [`tools/conformance/test/network.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/network.integration.test.ts): funds a smart account whose owner no other account can replace, and gets all of it back
- [`tools/conformance/test/network.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/network.integration.test.ts): refuses to fund a contract account whose owner on chain is not the key it tracks
- [`tools/conformance/test/network.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/network.integration.test.ts): refuses to fund a contract account that does not run the code it was tracked as
- [`packages/testkit/test/smart-account.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/packages/testkit/test/smart-account.test.ts): takes its owner in the constructor and exports no function that could replace it
- [`contracts/testing/ed25519-account/src/test.rs`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/contracts/testing/ed25519-account/src/test.rs): no_call_replaces_the_owner
- [`tools/conformance/test/network.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/network.test.ts): sweeps once after the body, and after a body that throws before rethrowing
- [`tools/conformance/test/network.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/network.test.ts): sweeps on %s before the process exits with %i

**Residual risk.** A smart account's deployment fees and storage rent are spent by design. A SIGKILL skips the sweep; the registry then lets the operator recover the funds. An OpenZeppelin smart account is checked by its code, not its signers: they are the ones its constructor set, which only its own authorization can change.

### CF2. Third-party code a pubnet run starts reads the operator's secrets

Mitigated · Information disclosure, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB18 (Conformance harness and the treasury) · asset: Harness treasury

**Threat.** The upstream e2e suite (third-party clients, servers and scripts) and the packages the live agent run installs with npm, with every dependency npm resolves for them, inherit the harness's environment: the treasury, sponsor and API key file paths, the run's ledger and its confirmation. npm runs the dependencies' install scripts, the installed SDK runs in the process that holds the treasury key, and the keys handed to that code (the suite's buyer, the agent's payer) keep receiving top-ups on later runs.

**What Stellar makes different.** A leaked Stellar seed moves the account's funds at once; nothing on chain can stop it.

**Preconditions.** A compromised or malicious commit in the suite checkout a mainnet run uses, or a compromised version of a package npm resolves for the live agent run. **Impact.** Without the controls: the treasury drained, outside every cap.

**Mitigations**

- The suite runs with an allow-listed environment (toolchain variables minus anything that looks like a secret, token, key, confirmation or ledger) plus its own reviewed variables; a new suite variable is refused until it is added to the list. ([`tools/conformance/src/upstream-e2e-env.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/src/upstream-e2e-env.ts), `suiteEnvironment`)
- The live agent run and pack-check install the packages with npm's lifecycle scripts off, so no dependency runs code while it installs, and with the same allow-listed environment; the code they install runs only in child processes that get that environment or less. ([`tools/conformance/src/upstream-e2e-env.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/src/upstream-e2e-env.ts), `NPM_INSTALL_FLAGS`)
- The live agent run never runs the installed packages in the process that holds the treasury: it starts an agent process with the allow-listed environment, which installs and runs them, and answers only its requests for the payer and for counting each payment. ([`tools/conformance/src/live-agent.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/src/live-agent.ts), `startAgentProcess`)
- On a host that allows it the agent process is launched in its own user, mount and PID namespaces (unshare): its /proc holds only its own processes, so it cannot read the treasury process's environment or memory, and the treasury secret file, the API key file and the runs directory are masked by an unreadable tmpfs. On pubnet, where the run holds a mainnet treasury, this is required: a run refuses to start before anything is spent on a host without it, unless the operator asserts, through a documented opt-out, that the agent runs under a separate OS user. ([`tools/conformance/src/live-agent.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/src/live-agent.ts), `agentCommand`)
- On pubnet the suite's buyer is derived from the treasury seed and the run id, fresh for each run and funded with 1 USDC, then swept and merged; the seller is a treasury-derived account, never the operator's sponsor. ([`tools/conformance/src/upstream-e2e.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/src/upstream-e2e.ts), `suite-buyer`)
- On pubnet the live agent's payer is derived the same way, fresh for each run, so a seed the installed packages saw never controls a later run's payer. ([`tools/conformance/src/live-agent.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/src/live-agent.ts), `perRun: true`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/conformance/test/upstream-e2e-env.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/upstream-e2e-env.test.ts): passes only an allow-listed toolchain environment and the suite's own variables
- [`tools/conformance/test/upstream-e2e-env.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/upstream-e2e-env.test.ts): never passes a secret-ish variable of this process, whatever prefix it has
- [`tools/conformance/test/upstream-e2e-env.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/upstream-e2e-env.test.ts): refuses a suite variable nobody reviewed
- [`tools/conformance/test/upstream-e2e-env.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/upstream-e2e-env.test.ts): keep only the toolchain variables of this process, never a secret's
- [`tools/conformance/test/upstream-e2e-env.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/upstream-e2e-env.test.ts): run no dependency's install script
- [`tools/conformance/test/pubnet-live.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/pubnet-live.test.ts): on pubnet: npm without install scripts or secrets, and the SDK away from the treasury
- [`tools/conformance/test/pubnet-live.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/pubnet-live.test.ts): on testnet: npm without install scripts or secrets, and the SDK in a process of its own
- [`tools/pack-check/test/consumer-install.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/pack-check/test/consumer-install.test.ts): runs npm with install scripts off and the allow-listed toolchain environment only
- [`tools/conformance/test/network.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/network.test.ts): run the agent on pubnet through the run's ledger, counting each payment its seller settled
- [`tools/conformance/test/pubnet-live.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/pubnet-live.test.ts): refuses a pubnet run, before anything is planned or spent, when the agent cannot be isolated
- [`tools/conformance/test/pubnet-live.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/pubnet-live.test.ts): proceeds only with the documented opt-out that the agent runs as a separate OS user

**Residual risk.** npm resolves the live agent run's dependencies at run time within the packages' declared ranges, not from the reviewed lockfile. Where the host cannot provide user, mount and PID namespaces, the agent process is only isolated on pubnet through the operator's opt-out (asserting it runs under a separate OS user); a testnet run there, which holds no treasury, runs third-party code as the same OS user. Run mainnet evidence on a host that allows namespaces, or under a separate OS user that cannot read the treasury file.

### CF3. A pubnet run spends past its caps

Mitigated · Denial of service, Elevation of privilege · boundary TB18 (Conformance harness and the treasury) · asset: Harness treasury

**Threat.** The caps see estimates instead of the fees the network charges, miss contract deployments, count settlements once from a hand-made bound, apply to each harness process alone, and one confirmation authorises any number of reruns.

**What Stellar makes different.** Soroban fees come from simulation and include resource fees and rent; each settlement costs the facilitator's sponsor up to its fee ceiling.

**Preconditions.** A run with more cases, more settlements or higher fees than its estimates, or a rerun with the same confirmation. **Impact.** Without the controls: real XLM and USDC spent beyond what the operator confirmed.

**Mitigations**

- Every transaction a harness submits passes one choke point: nothing is sent before the run's meter is attached, a fee the treasury pays is debited at its maximum before it is sent and lowered to the fee charged, and a fee source outside the run is refused. ([`tools/conformance/src/network.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/src/network.ts), `spend_unmetered`)
- Every /settle request to the target is debited at the facilitator's fee ceiling before it is sent, including those of the upstream suite through the harness's forwarder. ([`tools/conformance/src/facilitator-guard.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/src/facilitator-guard.ts), `guardedFetch`)
- One ledger per evidence run, shared by every harness process under a lock, caps the run's net XLM and net USDC loss; its id includes a fresh nonce, it is claimed once and closed at the end. ([`tools/conformance/src/run-ledger.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/src/run-ledger.ts), `pubnet_run_reused`)
- Caps and fee ceilings are required on pubnet and refused when malformed or zero. ([`tools/conformance/src/network.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/src/network.ts), `positiveInteger`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/conformance/test/network.integration.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/network.integration.test.ts): counts every fee the treasury pays, as charged, and refuses a step past the cap
- [`tools/conformance/test/network.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/network.test.ts): debits every fee the treasury pays before sending, and refuses a fee source outside the run
- [`tools/conformance/test/facilitator-guard.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/facilitator-guard.test.ts): debits every /settle at its scheme's fee ceiling before sending it, and sends none past a cap
- [`tools/conformance/test/facilitator-guard.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/facilitator-guard.test.ts): counts the settlements of a third-party suite that reaches the facilitator through the forwarder
- [`tools/conformance/test/run-ledger.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/run-ledger.test.ts): never lets concurrent harness processes pass the run's cap together
- [`tools/conformance/test/network.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/network.test.ts): plans a run on the first attempt and spends nothing; a confirmed run claims it once
- [`tools/conformance/test/network.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/network.test.ts): refuses a malformed or zero fee ceiling instead of ignoring it

**Residual risk.** A step debited before its transaction is refused stays counted, so a run can stop slightly below its caps.

### CF4. A pubnet run pays through the wrong contract or without access

Mitigated · Spoofing, Tampering · boundary TB18 (Conformance harness and the treasury) · asset: Harness treasury

**Threat.** Pubnet upto clients ignore the configured contract, a contract setting points at code other than the published one, or a run against a keyed facilitator funds its accounts and then has every request refused.

**What Stellar makes different.** An upto payer authorises the settlement contract it is given; another contract with the same interface would receive the authorisation.

**Preconditions.** A pubnet run with upto cases or against a facilitator that requires keys. **Impact.** Without the controls: authorisations signed for unverified code, or funds spent on a run that cannot pass.

**Mitigations**

- The upto contract and its WASM hash are taken together, checked against the published pubnet deployment record when it exists, and the contract's instance is read on chain before anything is spent; every pubnet harness passes it to its upto clients. ([`tools/conformance/src/network.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/src/network.ts), `checkUptoContract`)
- Before anything is spent, an empty /verify shows whether the facilitator serves the run; a missing or refused key stops it, and the key is then sent with every facilitator request. ([`tools/conformance/src/facilitator-guard.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/src/facilitator-guard.ts), `checkFacilitatorAccess`)

**Tests**

- [`tools/conformance/test/network.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/network.test.ts): takes the upto contract and WASM hash together, checked against the published pubnet record
- [`tools/conformance/test/facilitator-guard.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/facilitator-guard.test.ts): refuses a run before spending when the facilitator wants a key it does not have or refuses
- [`tools/conformance/test/facilitator-guard.test.ts`](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/tools/conformance/test/facilitator-guard.test.ts): sends the API key as a bearer token to the facilitator only

**Residual risk.** None.

{/* generated:end */}

## Out of scope

- Whether a seller delivers what it sells, or charges a fair `upto` amount within the cap the buyer signed.
- The security of buyers' wallets and of the machines agents run on.
- The integrity of the Stellar RPC provider beyond what simulation and the ledger prove; the RPC URL is
  operator configuration.
- Attacks on the hosted testnet instance's infrastructure; see [SECURITY.md](https://github.com/tolgayayci/rail402/blob/main/SECURITY.md)
  for how to report a vulnerability.
